## 5. Accepted R1 — full owner/handle relationship

CAND §§B–E and PASS §C are the complete accepted representation contract. R0’s original ownership and transaction rules remain preserved.

| Name | Role |
|---|---|
| S — SourceOwner | Exact private **GC-aware native heap container**, carrying preserved semantic SourceAuthority/R0 responsibility |
| H — SourceHandle | Published exact **non-GC native heap handle**; normal heap-type ownership only, native D lease |
| D | Separately anchored native CS-R1 root/control/evidence cell |
| C_R0 | Fixed native reference/result storage **embedded in S**, with all Python-reference slots included in S’s traversal |
| A_src | SourceLifetimeAdapter acting for canonical S; distinct from the retaining adapter observed by canonical Q |
| Assoc | Canonical capability/Association record, owns S and published H |
| Anchor | Existing external native process-profile anchor; native ownership independent of Python cyclic GC |

```text
Anchor --native lease--> D --owning Python reference--> Registry
                                                       |
                                                       v
                                                     Assoc
                                                    /     \
                                             owns S       owns H
                                                |            |
                                                +--owns H    +--native lease--> D
                                                +--owns A_src / ledger
                                                +--configuration / F,O slots / embedded C_R0
```

The canonical one-to-one relation is `Assoc._source_owner is S`, `Assoc._source_lifetime is H`, `S.handle is H`, `H.cell is D`, and D’s immutable bound-source identity denotes S **for comparison only**. H has no owning or dereferenceable backlink to S. No alias factory, shared H, second S, rebinding or retrieval-through-bind is permitted. Pre-P0 H/D immutable checks are not current canonical graph proof; rooted E1-B traversal performs current identity checks before dependent use.

S owns H, A_src and the existing canonical ledger; embedded configuration has scalars and named callable-slot roles, never a hidden callable cache. Two installed slots each own the selected shared F when populated; two original slots each own the same authenticated O. C_R0 contains individually accounted per-registration original reservations, original verification guards, expected-installed guards and displaced-registration slots. Equal object identity does not collapse separately owned references. Unused slots are empty. Unresolved references remain in these same named slots; no deferred list, replacement owner or hidden native cache appears.

Canonical capability setup establishes S once before responsibility/source mutation. S begins with initialized fixed storage but no H, adapter, callable or R0 temporary obligations. The binding writer obtains S from the current Association, creates D/H with the inherited construction/root/anchor protection, fixes comparison identity, establishes S→H privately, then publishes H at the initialized `Assoc._source_lifetime` member store. S→H alone is not publication. Callback-free, non-yielding native construction and first-publication-wins rules apply. Adapter establishment, callable retention and installation follow only through separately governed eligibility; binding/P0 acquisition is not installation or restoration authority.

Unpublished failure performs inherited root ER0 disposal first using independently live Registry, removes the unpublished anchor lease, clears S.handle while the constructor independently owns H, releases private H under ER1 and drops construction ownership. S remains the same unbound capability-owned S. No source effects or R0 callable responsibility have begun. Postpublication loss/failure retains B2; it cannot undo publication, replace S or invent activation recovery. Uncertain execution/crash is SC, never inferred rollback.

S traverses **every owning Python reference**, including embedded C_R0 and applicable heap-type ownership. Neither S nor H admits caller construction, subclassing, instance dict, weakrefs or Python finalizer. Active/BF S is externally rooted through Anchor→D→Registry→Assoc→S and cannot lawfully be cleared by ordinary GC. GC clearing/deallocation cannot restore sources, substitute LR0, publish terminal results or recover. Before responsibility or after lawful B4, ordinary structural graph disposal is allowed. No invented GC-permission bit or omitted hidden container edge supplies this proof.

Caller loss leaves the external root chain intact; S→H retains H/D while needed. Same-finalizer successful ordering preserves the restoration/coverage transaction and its registration transfers, then restoration disposition, remaining temporary discharge and eligible S callable LR0 discharge, resource-discharge publication, Closing/root release/B4/anchor drop/finalizer-P0 release. A reservation already transferred to registration is absent from C_R0 and cannot be transferred/decremented again. Slot clearing and exactly one non-last decrement follow R0’s independent F/O owners, never fixture aliases. S/A_src/ledger/H structural lifetime continues after callable discharge.

BF retains S and every still-outstanding S/C_R0 obligation through the original external root. References already discharged before a later failure stay discharged. BF permanently closes new P0; P1 remains scalar evidence-only. No retry, successor, restored health, GC cleanup or COMPLETE is implied. After B4, retained H may outlive S and keep only rootless D evidence alive. D cannot dereference S/C_R0; comparison identities are historical scalars. Ordinary GC-visible cycles may then collect. C_R0 cannot independently outlive S or escape to P1.

A_src has no owning F/O cache or persistent raw S pointer. Its operation obtains canonical S through the Association and borrows slots only within admitted owner lifetime. Possession of A_src alone supplies no source authority; retained post-B4/BF structural state supplies no installation/restoration permission.

Future S3/schema/image authentication must cover new `_source_owner`, prospective `_source_lifetime`, all S/embedded reference fields, GC flags/bases/descriptors/unread layout-affecting fields, D identity representation and every establishment/publication/rollback/traversal/clearing/destruction path. ## 6. Accepted R2 — exactly-once rejected-attempt P0 unwind

CAND §§F–I and PASS §D are complete. Every invocation starts `P0_NOT_OWNED`, `E1_NOT_OWNED`. Only its successful acquisition CAS creates `P0_OWNED_BY_THIS_INVOCATION`, recorded in the same uninterrupted native execution. Occupancy, E1 and phase are distinct; another owner’s occupancy does not create local release authority.

- No acquisition → no release.
- Successful acquisition → exactly one intact rejection-path release before Python resumes.
- Lawful E3 → the **same invocation** retains P0 into the existing admitted-finalizer contract.

Only the terminal unwind releases rejected-attempt E1/P0. Scanner/canonical helpers discharge their own reference slots. Atomic P0 release preserves unrelated phase/lease/P1 fields; legitimate P1 CAS interference is retried. Failed CAS is not release. No timeout or inconsistent ownership permits clearing another invocation’s P0. There is no new wait-free progress guarantee, cleanup owner or invented invocation-owned H lease: **H itself owns one D lease**, independently live throughout release. Two Python owners of H do not create two native leases.

### Complete rejection-class matrix

I = actual scanner IR0 owning slots; C = actual canonical ER0 owning temporaries; X = transferred inspection exception. Valid post-T1 paths do not own I and C together. E2/E3 cannot be inferred from phase or error disposition.

| Class | Ownership/progress | Required disposition |
|---|---|---|
| R2-A — P0 acquisition rejection, including earlier context/profile/error gates | No P0/E1; no I/C/X; no protected progress | Native rejection; no root access; no P0/E1 release; preserve entry error. Earlier gate failure may mean no P0 attempt at all. |
| R2-B — E1 acquisition rejection after P0 | P0 only; no scanner/root/canonical ownership | End native acquisition checks; release own P0 once; never release winner’s E1. |
| R2-C — pre-scan precondition/invalidation | P0/E1; no I/C/X | End checks; release E1 then P0. |
| R2-D — initial frame unavailable | P0/E1; no returned frame or C; X only for an actual error | Reject unavailable ancestry; preserve actual error with transfer if present, otherwise clear indicator; release E1/P0. NULL is not empty ancestry; no fabricated MemoryError. |
| R2-E — ancestry identity/depth rejection | P0/E1; actual populated I; no C/X for ordinary rejection | Stop scan; discharge all I; release E1 then P0; no T1 success/E1-B/E2/E3. |
| R2-F — frame/getter/materialization error | P0/E1; I possible; X from indicator; no C | Save X; IR0; release E1/P0; restore X. No truncated success. |
| R2-G — post-T1 transition rejection | P0/E1; I/C/X empty; ancestry and T1 complete | End applicable transition checks; release E1/P0. No invented additional gate; unused where no rejecting operation exists. |
| R2-H — ordinary canonical rejection | P0/E1; I/X empty; C possible | End dependent reads; ER0; keep clear indicator; release E1/P0; no E2/E3. |
| R2-I — unexpected canonical error | P0/E1; I/X empty; C possible; active error stays in indicator | End reads; ER0; release E1/P0 error-neutrally; no X transfer or continuation of validation. |
| R2-J — late invalidation before E2 | Actual scanner/canonical subregion determines I/C/X | Apply actual ownership cleanup, then E1/P0 release; no phase-based guesses or E2/E3 inference. |
| R2-K1 — valid E2, rejection before E3 | P0/E1; I/C/X empty | Retain historical same-attempt E2; end its admission eligibility; release E1/P0; later invocation cannot use old E2. |
| R2-K2 — unexpected inspection result without error | P0/E1; I possible; C/X empty | Fixed rejection, actual IR0 cleanup, E1/P0 release; no fabricated exception. |
| R2-K3 — finite counter/evidence capacity unavailable | Ownership at first applicable check | Before acquisition R2-A; after acquisition clean actual slots and release acquired obligations. No wrap, overwrite or replacement E2 slot. |
| R2-K4 — owner/binding/current-edge mismatch | P0/E1; E1-B entered; I/X empty; C possible | Validate before dependent access; end borrows; ER0; release E1/P0. |

Binding failures use construction/root/ER1 rules, not operational rejection. Every intact operational exit examines its actual ownership vector. Success/E3, BF and SC are distinct from the rejection table.

```text
end dependent work
→ transfer actual inspection exception where applicable
→ finish IR0
→ finish ER0
→ remaining admitted cleanup / end borrows
→ release owned E1
→ release owned P0 exactly once
→ restore transferred inspection exception where applicable
→ return/resume Python
```

All Registry/Assoc/S/adapter/frame/code/container/raw-slot/metadata-derived borrows end before E1 release. Evidence derived from them is already in admitted scalar storage. E1-before-P0 leaves only native terminal control; a newly eligible configuration writer does not gain P0 release authority. P0 release is the last P0-required operation. Post-release markers use separate preallocated storage and never reread D under the released pin.

E1/P0 operations are native scalar/atomic, error-neutral, with no Python API, allocation, decref, callback or deallocation. Inspection transfer uses `PyErr_GetRaisedException`, leaves the indicator empty through cleanup/releases, then restores the same X with `PyErr_SetRaisedException` into an empty destination exactly once. Canonical errors instead remain active through independently non-last ER0 cleanup. Diagnostics occur after obligations end and cannot replace a preserved error. No H/evidence-slot cleanup is secretly left after exception restoration.

E2/result storage remains immutable and finitely bounded; a rejection cannot reset/reuse it or carry E3 eligibility into another call. E3 is a separate one-shot commit changing this invocation’s role, not transferring ownership. Success retains P0 through B4 and anchor drop; BF ends uses, closes new P0 permanently and releases its finalizer P0 while retaining responsibility. SC supplies no intact-unwind claim. Future artifact equivalence must include ownership recording, every cleanup branch, E1/P0 atomic release, error restoration and control-flow dominance.

## 7. Accepted R3-MX — production exclusion and exact primitive qualification

CAND §§J–L and PASS §E replace the ambiguous busy-contender row with two distinct obligations plus production integration/equivalence:

**A. Production actor/path exclusion proof.** Authenticate the admitted executable graph and gate implementation. Ordinary protected-interval production contention is not fabricated to satisfy a matrix row.

**B. Direct helper-scoped qualification of exact production `P0_TRY_ACQUIRE`.** Standalone valid native storage exercises the occupied branch of the same production primitive. It does not demonstrate production contention or source lifetime.

Production gate order is fixed:

1. Safe runtime/profile/native execution-context admission.
2. Bound main-thread/GIL admission before context-dependent Python-object operations.
3. Existing-error rejection and independently live exact H.
4. H lease and immutable D publication/profile/lifetime checks.
5. Native phase eligibility and P0 acquisition decision.
6. E1 acquisition.
7. Pre-scan native preconditions.
8. E1-A.
9. E1-B, including current canonical owner/binding checks.
10. E2 and separately eligible E3.

Foreign native contexts reject before object/P0 access. Other Python threads require the GIL; their native GIL-attempt precursor is not a P0 contender. Same-thread Python reentry/callback and unreviewed native nesting remain excluded by the audited graph. Native signal recording is allowed without Python handler processing. The helper driver is a separately admitted main-thread/GIL route, not nested inside a production protected invocation.

The fixture owns **valid, aligned, live, standalone native control-word storage**, including the complete manifest-bound representation and valid phase/lease/count combinations. Eligible B2 and occupied P0 are selected input, not a canonical acquired occupancy. It creates no S, H, canonical D, Registry, source responsibility, E1, E2/E3 or K/Q/U authority. Numerical masks and layout must instantiate the future authenticated representation; arbitrary bit pairs are insufficient.

The exact primitive loads with admitted memory ordering, rejects wrong phase at a distinct gate, and for eligible occupied state returns:

```text
gate = P0_OCCUPIED
disposition = REJECT_NO_ACQUISITION
```

This branch performs **no write**, no successful-acquisition CAS, no occupancy decrement, no E1 operation and no root access. It cannot acquire, clear, transfer or release incumbent P0. Its tag has exactly one producing branch; the driver cannot inject it. The no-write contract covers all word fields, not just P0.

Evidence: valid fixture → exact primitive call → unique occupied acknowledgement → no-acquisition disposition → unchanged word → no release obligation. Endpoint equality alone cannot exclude intervening illicit writes; exact artifact inspection must prove no-write behavior and execution must attribute the same implementation. Fixture teardown is not PIN_RELEASE or native-cell destruction qualification.

Missing occupied target/attribution, wrong phase, invalid fixture/interference or incomplete equivalence is **INCONCLUSIVE for that target**. Earlier production context rejection is only negative/interface evidence for that gate. Independently attributed forbidden behavior may establish FAIL; cleanup cannot upgrade failure or inconclusive evidence.

Production/helper artifacts must contain the same compiled primitive: representation/masks, phase-before-occupied order, atomic loads/CAS/memory order, result encoding, no-write rejection and relevant success behavior. No mock, patched return, substituted atomic, alternate test-busy branch or artificial production entry point. Production integration separately proves earlier-gate dominance, rejection cannot enter success handling, R2-A releases nothing, and E1/root access is unreachable after rejection. Allowed differences are standalone input storage, native driver scaffolding and preallocated scalar evidence only.

Claim ceiling: **the exact production acquisition primitive, presented with valid local occupied-state input, rejects without modifying or acquiring that occupancy**. This does not qualify production contention scheduling, integrated S/H/root lifetime, E1 exclusion, canonical validation, K/Q/U, E2/E3 or finalization.

## 9. Cross-correction, actors, MX and crash boundaries

PASS §F accepts R1×R2, R1×R3-MX and R2×R3-MX interactions. R1 supplies rooted S and H’s existing D lease; R2 removes only invocation-owned obligations; rejection performs no S callable LR0 or S/H destruction. R3-MX’s standalone helper supplies no source lifetime proof and cannot release its synthetic incumbent. Scalar observation adds no owner, hidden pin, semantic mirror or cleanup executor.

Protections are separate: root/metadata ownership keeps objects/derivatives live; H/native leases keep D live; P0 grants root-capable access; GIL plus no Python excludes Python writers and preserves ancestry; E1 excludes native configuration writers; P1 provides only native evidence-storage rights. A writer resuming after E1 release cannot own this P0. Python resumes only after rejected-invocation cleanup. Native signals may record without Python signal processing.

MX remains evidence/oracle methodology, never live semantic authority. A records actual E1/P0, target and scan/release events; B starts only after A success and zero IR0, records exact H1/current canonical result and ER0 cleanup; E2 follows both successes; E3 is separately witnessed. Target acknowledgement differs from disposition. PASS requires intended target and correct attributed outcome; independently observed forbidden behavior establishes FAIL; absent targets, invalid fixture/equivalence, missing schedule or timeout without decisive evidence are INCONCLUSIVE. Default isolated-history deadline is 12 seconds; changes need separate reviewed justification. No sleep-only race oracle, fixture-created K/Q/U, observer ownership supplying proof or cleanup-based upgrade.

Full CP matrix families remain: anchor/GC/metadata; binding/reentry; handles/pins/destruction; closure/root ER0; BF; helper/raw/error/reference; current-edge/integrity/assigned K/Q/U; writers/signals; A/T1/B; E2/E3; equivalence; crash/containment. CP Appendix R5’s twelve T1 rows remain intact. R3-MX replaces only the ambiguous occupied-P0 route/claim, not the other obligations.

Artifact transfer requires exact relevant schema/images, representation/GC/reference behavior, constructor/publication/rollback, control-word/lease/closure/destruction, raw loads/compiler expansion, helpers/errors/releases, T1, E2/E3, memory ordering and source effects. Allowed differences are fixed scalar evidence, native synchronization and parent-observable markers. Hidden roots, extra strong references, retained scanner frames, added pins or reentry cannot supply a production proof.

Crash/SC is lost execution proof, distinct from intact rejection and retained admitted BF. Observe only completed attributed transitions: no inferred IR0/ER0, E2, E3, root release, restoration, B4 or COMPLETE. Process reclamation is not successful resource restoration. Uncertain E3 is SC, never a normal rejecting exit. Crash recovery and arbitrary corruption/bypass protection remain unqualified.

## 15. Phase-A executable closure and invalidation

Production C1 is a bounded closed process/profile with authenticated runtime/build/main interpreter/main thread, exact loaded native/Python images, admitted runtime/allocator hooks, canonical census, fixed reviewed native/FFI targets and no unrelated executable graph. L0 excludes unreviewed loading after seal. No generic FFI evaluator-writer route, debugger/JIT mutation or external profiler is admitted. Monotonic invalidation covers evaluator mutation even if restored, unreviewed loading, new generic FFI/setter reachability, unsupported debugger/JIT/runtime/allocator/reference-tracer hooks, external profiler, production trace, class/function/image mutation, runtime/association mismatch or unauthenticated identity. ## 16. G1/T1 evaluator, N1-PROFILER and Q2/QC1/C1

G1/T1 is a bounded, exact-version private evaluator getter observation against the actually loaded default evaluator symbol/image. It is read-only and never executes or changes an evaluator. Endpoint equality alone does not prove uninterrupted default-evaluator lifetime. Prior temporary replacement or unprovable continuity invalidates eligibility monotonically, even after restoration to default.

Origin, pre-installation and pre-E1 revalidation remain separate. No arbitrary interpreter selection, evaluator field mutation, setter, pending/tripped access or internal-frame expansion is implied. No evaluator getter was executed in checkpoint creation.

**N1-PROFILER** names the earlier profiler architecture: no external prior profiler at production origin; only exact intrinsic frozen acquisition classifiers, with authenticated function/frame/closure/previous-chain provenance. An active classifier blocks P1; unresolved acquisition blocks Q/U. Frozen profiler installation/restoration is not a general profiler compatibility claim.

The re-review's new MAJOR finding originally called N1 is instead durably named **CS-R1-MAJOR-H1**. Never confuse the surviving N1-PROFILER input with the failed helper-whitelist completeness finding.

Evaluator observation uses private `_PyInterpreterState_GetEvalFrameFunc` on the exact authenticated current main interpreter and comparison to qualified `_PyEval_EvalFrameDefault` identity. No setter, direct `interp->eval_frame`, evaluator invocation or arbitrary function-pointer execution. Continuity comes from closed executable/writer/loading profile, not getter polling. G1/T1's name is distinct from terminal-IR0 option T1.

N1-PROFILER admits only exact intrinsic classifiers produced by frozen `DescriptorAcquisition.acquire()`, factory/capture provenance and predecessor chain ending at None. Existing install/restoration transitions remain frozen. External prior profilers/test observers are not production callback roots.

Q2 permits only separately sealed pre-E3 trace-driven rejection/active-frame histories, exact hook/capture/target and positive native canonical-match evidence. QC1 distinguishes qualification from production. C1 is a closed candidate process, fixed roots/native targets, no unrelated application/test trace/generic FFI/plugin/debugger execution; L0 prohibits new executable loading after seal. Protected successful histories use native/external instrumentation.

## 17. Historical P1 — what was and was not frozen

Original P1 is RECOVERED CONVERSATION EVIDENCE, not implemented bytes. Its full authenticated report is preserved in Appendix R1. Historical states E0 AVAILABLE → E1 RESERVED → E2 VALIDATED → E3 COMMITTED → E4 protected exit are distinct from later B/S states. Clean precommit rejection releases reservation without E3; admitted failure retains responsibility; process failure is SC.

Historical contract: runtime/profile eligibility and rejection of pre-existing Python error before frame inspection/E1; E1 before ancestry scan; same reservation and GIL continuously through semantic validation/E2/E3; public frame APIs only; exact canonical-code census; initial NULL rejects, never empty ancestry; clean terminal NULL required for exhaustion; canonical match rejects before E2/E3; no internal frame read or pending-state mutation. The executing interpreter/generator frame independently owns each materialized live-ancestry frame while GIL/no-Python/no-teardown preserves that ancestry. ## 42. Full historical report preservation — provenance and precedence

Appendices R1–R6 preserve complete recovered report text. They are evidence transcripts, not current execution instructions. Their historical authentication claims describe their own tasks, not fresh checks here. Their historical unresolved statuses/next gates must be read chronologically through the later T1 completion and current synthesis. R2 supplies exact selected native machinery and matrix; R3 audits P1 APIs; R4 preserves the absence of a historical choice; R5 explicitly makes the new choice. R6 is only a historical publication response, not missing V7 raw files.

No transformation below is claimed to reproduce a raw V7 file. Presentation trims trailing whitespace and adds Markdown blockquote prefixes; original extraction hashes remain those in §40.

### Appendix R1. Historical P1 report

**HISTORICAL REPORT / ORIGINAL PROVENANCE RETAINED.** Current status and authorization are in §§22, 36–38 and the bootstrap.


> **P1 — public CPython APIs are sufficient with a pre-established native association/exclusion record.** No direct internal-frame read is required for the bounded profile.
>
> The prospective solution uses conservative code-identity rejection, a provisional native reservation, and a distinct one-shot finalization commit. This is a **CORRECTED-CANDIDATE architecture input**, not independent review, implementation authorization or qualification.
>
> **Authentication and preservation**
>
> Authentication passed:
>
> - Repository `/Users/erale/Documents/orev3`; branch/upstream `research/post-v1` / `origin/research/post-v1`.
> - Local, tracking and independently queried live-remote HEAD: `6f4c59e3871cf12c3bfc70e7ebad0dd88fb867f0`.
> - Ahead/behind **0/0**; index empty.
> - Mutable boundary **5 tracked + 31 untracked = 36**.
> - Protocol/checkpoint/source/test identities matched.
> - All **703 preservation identities/modes** matched.
> - Architecture report/manifest and receipt-authority bindings matched.
> - `git diff --check` passed.
>
> No repository changes, candidate execution, tests, signals, experiments or builds occurred.
>
> **Current mechanism and prospective difference**
>
> [_Ledger._admit()](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/src/orev3/execution/cancellation_capability.py:752) inspects the current live ancestry, canonical code identities, owner locals and admission markers. It rejects competing runners and receipt/runner overlap. Its lifetime is the executing frame; a traceback retaining an escaped frame does not retain admission. No Python `finally` must release a lock.
>
> The prospective native admission preserves that principle but uses a **more conservative predicate**:
>
> > Any live frame matching the finite canonical ingress/continuation census blocks finalization, regardless of its owner locals or whether its Python marker has executed.
>
> This eliminates frame-local inspection. It does not alter existing I3/I4 admission semantics.
>
> The bounded topology permits one canonical source owner for the admitted process-global signal configuration. Rejecting a canonical frame belonging to another owner is safe contention rejection, not evidence that the admitted owner failed.
>
> **Finite frame census**
>
> Code objects are retained and authenticated before source installation. Symbols below identify exact functions in the current bytes or explicitly identified prospective functions.
>
> | Frame | Role and participation | Pre-marker treatment | Finalization rule |
> |---|---|---|---|
> | Prospective F | Runtime callback delegating canonical ingress or post-cut ordinary routing | Reject from publicly visible execution entry, before its first marker | Always blocks while live |
> | Prospective finalization wrapper | Canonical caller of native admission | Exact expected entry wrapper is allowed once | A second live wrapper rejects reentry |
> | `_Ledger._receive` | Receipt/classification | Reject entire frame, not only `_ingress=True` interval | Blocks |
> | `_Ledger._admit` | Runner admission inspection | Not itself receipt authority | Blocks conservatively while live |
> | `_Ledger._prepare` | Preparation mutation | Entire frame | Blocks |
> | `_Ledger._dispatch` | Claim/action/accounting | Entire frame, including claim-before-entry intervals | Blocks |
> | `_Ledger._settle` | Settlement mutation | Entire frame | Blocks |
> | `_Ledger._cut` | Cut arbitration | Must have returned before finalization | Blocks |
> | `_RetainingAdapter._retain` | Receipt ingress spanning capture, publication and return | Entire frame, including before `_ingress` | Blocks |
> | `_RetainingAdapter._dequeue` | Separate retained continuation | Entire frame | Blocks; Q also checks retained continuation |
> | `_AcquisitionBridge._enter` | Acquisition admission/composition | Entire frame | Blocks |
> | `_AcquisitionBridge._establish` | Establishment executor | Live executing ancestry blocks; suspended state is checked through Q | Blocks when active |
> | `_AcquisitionBridge._receive` | Bridge receipt ingress | Entire frame | Blocks |
> | `_AcquisitionBridge._finish` | F2 continuation/finish | Entire frame | Blocks; unresolved bridge state independently blocks Q |
> | `_composition_complete` | Composition completion checks | Entire frame | Blocks |
> | Native finalization entry | No separate Python frame | Native record detects nested entry | Provisional or committed reservation rejects reentry |
>
> `_ingress` is currently a **local marker**, not a standalone function. No new function by that name is needed.
>
> Helpers such as `_graph`, `_receiver` and `_composition_owner` do not independently establish receipt. When invoked within an operation above, its enclosing frame supplies exclusion. A diagnostic helper called independently does not become an invented ingress authority.
>
> A suspended generator, retained traceback, pending caller or completed frame is not ACTIVE merely because it remains reachable. Its unresolved semantic obligations still belong in Q/U.
>
> **F before its first marker**
>
> The native scanner compares F’s exact pre-retained code pointer. It does not need F’s locals, signum, receiver or a marker to reject active F.
>
> Public traversal skips incomplete interpreter frames. For the bounded stock interpreter path, that does not justify ignoring an executing pre-marker F:
>
> - F before execution is not yet participating ingress.
> - At the in-profile RESUME/monitoring/pending-check execution boundary, its execution position is publicly traceable.
> - Reentry from a call/monitoring callback before F’s first body marker therefore encounters F’s live code identity.
>
> This must be qualified at that precise boundary. A substituted evaluator or native hook that can re-enter before public frame visibility is **not covered by this proof**; it is not silently admitted by the version check. The source-level basis is the pinned completeness predicate and RESUME sequence. No direct use of those internal fields is proposed. [CPython 3.14.5 frame representation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Include/internal/pycore_interpframe.h), [RESUME implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Python/bytecodes.c)
>
> **Owner/role authentication**
>
> The scanner does not authenticate an owner by reading frame locals.
>
> A pre-established native record contains:
>
> - Exact SourceAuthority and association identities.
> - Exact source/profile and cut-witness binding.
> - Exact main interpreter/thread binding.
> - Pre-retained canonical code identities.
> - Native writer/reservation state.
> - One-shot finalization state.
> - Retained rejection/admission/result storage.
>
> The canonical association creates and retains this record before source mutation. It cannot be minted from caller-provided booleans or addresses.
>
> Code-pointer comparison answers **“is a disallowed canonical operation active?”** The record answers **“which source authority is requesting finalization?”** These questions remain separate.
>
> No property lookup, descriptor, Python equality/hash, `f_locals` materialization or arbitrary attribute access occurs during native admission.
>
> **Audited public API operation set**
>
> The initial runtime/build gate must already have established compatibility. Admission then permits only this finite inspection set and native scalar/atomic operations:
>
> | Operation | Ownership and behavior | Admission rule |
> |---|---|---|
> | `PyThreadState_Get()` | Borrowed current thread state; requires valid attached context | No thread-state creation |
> | `PyThreadState_GetInterpreter()` | Borrowed interpreter identity | Compare with pre-established identity |
> | `PyThread_get_thread_ident()` | Native thread identifier | Compare with bound main thread |
> | `PyErr_Occurred()` | Borrowed error indicator | Existing error rejects before reservation/inspection |
> | `PyThreadState_GetFrame()` | New frame reference; may materialize; can return NULL without an exception after failure | NULL initial frame always rejects |
> | `PyFrame_GetBack()` | New reference or NULL; may materialize ancestor | NULL plus error rejects; clean NULL ends traversal |
> | `PyFrame_GetCode()` | New code reference; exact frame input | Compare pointer; unexpected failure rejects |
> | `PyErr_GetRaisedException()` | Transfers ownership of an inspection error | Only on terminal rejection path |
> | `PyErr_SetRaisedException()` | Transfers that error back into an empty error indicator | Preserve error without formatting/normalization |
> | `Py_INCREF` / `Py_DECREF` | Restricted to proven anchored frame/code references | IR0 only for scanner cleanup |
> | Pointer/type checks | Exact native identity operations | No rich comparison |
> | Native atomic operations | Reservation/commit/publication | No Python execution |
>
> The pinned frame-materialization implementation explicitly states that it does not execute Python or run GC. It may allocate and produce `MemoryError`; that is not equivalent to success. `PyFrame_GetCode()` uses no allocating lookup in this GIL-enabled path. None of these selected paths intentionally checks signals or releases the GIL. [Frame materialization](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Python/frame.c), [public frame implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/frameobject.c), [thread-state implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Python/pystate.c)
>
> Unsupported allocator/evaluator/runtime hooks cannot be assumed callback-free. They remain outside the admitted build/runtime profile.
>
> **Inspection-reference ownership**
>
> Traversal keeps only a bounded number of temporary references at once: current frame, next frame and current code.
>
> | Reference | Independent anchor | Release point | Classification |
> |---|---|---|---|
> | Initial live frame | Executing interpreter frame owns its materialized frame object | After next reference acquired, or rejection | **IR0** |
> | Live back-frame | Its executing ancestor frame owns its frame object | Same traversal discipline | **IR0** |
> | Code returned by `PyFrame_GetCode` | Executing frame retains executable code; canonical entries also have pre-retained identities | Immediately after comparison | **IR0** |
> | Thread/interpreter pointers | Borrowed runtime identities | No DECREF | No discharge obligation |
> | Canonical census code references | Existing function/module ownership plus retained identity record | Not temporary scanner references | Separate identity-record lifetime |
> | Inspection-generated exception | Error indicator → local owned reference → error indicator | Ownership transfer on rejection | No destructive scanner release |
>
> The same thread’s ancestors remain live while the native scan retains the GIL and executes no Python. Scanner releases therefore do not destroy their locals, trace objects or transitive application objects.
>
> A materialized frame may eventually have callback-capable destruction when its actual execution lifetime ends. The scanner does not trigger that destruction through its temporary references.
>
> No IR2/IR3 scanner reference is deferred into an unexplained cleanup queue.
>
> **Error and allocation-failure contract**
>
> - **Existing Python error:** reject immediately, before reservation or frame APIs; preserve it unchanged.
> - **Initial frame acquisition NULL:** reject even if the API cleared its allocation error. Record only “initial ancestry unavailable,” not “no active ingress” or a fabricated OOM diagnosis.
> - **Ancestor materialization failure:** detect the error; transfer it out temporarily, discharge IR0 scanner references, release provisional reservation, then transfer the error back.
> - **Clean end of ancestry:** accept only clean NULL from back traversal after a valid initial frame.
> - **Identity/depth/profile mismatch:** record a fixed native rejection code; do not format a Python error inside the critical section.
> - **Unexpected frame/API result:** reject, never truncate the scan into success.
> - **Depth bound exceeded:** reject the unsupported depth; do not infer the unvisited ancestry is safe.
>
> The initial-frame API’s internal error-clearing path is relevant. With a clear error indicator at entry, its allocation-failure error is the pinned built-in MemoryError path, not an arbitrary pre-existing exception being destroyed. The materializer’s potentially destructive release of a saved exception is avoided by the clear-error precondition. [Pinned exception implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/exceptions.c)
>
> No error formatting, normalization, unraisable hook or general error helper is permitted inside admission.
>
> **Selected ordering and exclusion state machine**
>
> Select **L3: provisional exclusion, inspect, then commit or synchronously reject**.
>
> “OPEN” below means the writer slot is available; it does not mean old receipt authority is open after cut.
>
> | State | Meaning and allowed behavior |
> |---|---|
> | **E0 AVAILABLE** | Bound source owner exists; no finalization reservation. Normal canonical rules apply |
> | **E1 RESERVED** | Native CAS reserves writer exclusion for this attempt; no restoration effects or one-shot finalization commitment yet |
> | **E2 VALIDATED** | Full ancestry scan and A/K/Q/I/U/C/X revalidation succeeded; inspection references discharged |
> | **E3 COMMITTED** | One-shot finalization admission consumed; same native executor owns admitted responsibility |
> | **E4 TERMINAL_SUCCESS** | Restoration/resource results published; ordinary route may resume at protected exit; finalization cannot repeat |
> | **EF TERMINAL_FAILURE** | Admitted partial/uncertain failure retained; no retry/reuse authority |
> | **ER REJECTED** | Precommit rejection recorded; reservation returned to E0 before Python resumes |
> | **SC PROCESS_FAILURE** | Crash/corruption; no in-process publication/release guarantee |
>
> Transitions:
>
> 1. **E0→E1:** compare-and-exchange, acquire/release semantics, exact owner/attempt identity. Losing contender rejects without changing the winner.
> 2. **E1→E2:** scan complete live ancestry; reject census matches; revalidate semantic record; discharge scanner references. No source effects.
> 3. **E2→E3:** release-published commit transition. This is the **finalization admission linearization point**.
> 4. **E1/E2→ER→E0:** fixed rejection record, IR0 cleanup, release-store reservation removal, then return/error propagation.
> 5. **E3→E4/EF:** same native invocation publishes terminal results and transitions the record. It never resets one-shot admission.
> 6. **Any state→SC:** crash/abort/corruption has no implied successful cleanup.
>
> A precommit rejection does not consume finalization admission. A later otherwise-lawful request is not replay of a restoration effect that never began. This does not permit resuming a FAILED invocation.
>
> After E3, a returned error cannot restore E0. EF is deliberately terminal, not an accidentally forgotten lock.
>
> **Why I is not stale**
>
> Between the scan and E3:
>
> - The main-thread native call retains the GIL.
> - It performs no Python callback or signal check.
> - Native configuration writers are excluded by E1.
> - Native signal recording remains allowed.
> - No admitted cross-thread participating-retention producer exists outside the canonical ingress contract.
> - Any pre-existing main-thread ingress frame is an ancestor and causes rejection.
> - A Python caller waiting for execution has not thereby become participating ingress.
>
> Thus the absence observed by the complete scan remains true until commit. E3 binds I to the exact owner, attempt, cut and census identities.
>
> This is not a general claim that the GIL serializes all native activity.
>
> **Thread and producer boundary**
>
> | Participant | Concurrent with scan? | Treatment |
> |---|---|---|
> | Main-thread F/receipt/retention already active | Native attempt can be nested beneath it | Appears in ancestry; reject |
> | Another Python thread waiting for GIL | Cannot run Python during scan | Pending attempt, not active participation |
> | Cross-thread canonical ingress call | Must obey the current main-thread/profile gate | Cannot become hidden participating retention |
> | Native signal producer | Yes | May record pending delivery; does not need writer token |
> | External signal sender | Yes | Same; generation is not participating retention |
> | Native configuration writer | Yes in principle | Must share the same reservation protocol; losing writer performs no effect |
> | Native participating retainer | Not supplied by this bounded contract | Adding one requires an integrated ingress protocol; cannot be treated as a mere producer |
>
> An external native queue already retaining on behalf of the owner would invalidate this topology. The scan cannot prove that such a queue is empty or absent.
>
> **Ordering alternatives**
>
> - **L1, inspect first:** can work only with a complete no-callback/no-native-writer gap proof and revalidation; offers no advantage here.
> - **L2, acquire irrevocable admission before inspection:** rejected, because an active ancestor would be discovered after consuming finalization authority.
> - **L3, provisional reservation:** selected; permits callback-free clean rejection before one-shot commit.
> - No Python `finally` or later executor participates in release.
>
> **Race proof**
>
> | Race | Resolution |
> |---|---|
> | A. F pre-marker ↔ admission | Live code match rejects; marker is unnecessary |
> | B. Receipt begins ↔ reservation | Existing main-thread frame rejects; new Python execution cannot start during scan |
> | C. Retention begins ↔ admission | Same, plus Q independently detects retained unresolved continuation |
> | D. Post-cut explicit ingress ↔ admission | Canonical old-ingress rejection remains; no fabricated O delivery |
> | E. Pending signal recording ↔ admission | Recording remains allowed; no membership inferred from pending alone |
> | F. Native writer ↔ finalizer | Reservation CAS orders them; loser performs no configuration effect |
> | G. Same-owner reentry | Existing reservation rejects; ancestor census independently catches canonical nested entry |
> | H. Scan succeeds ↔ new ingress attempt | GIL/no-callback interval plus reservation prevents participation before E3 |
> | I. Reservation held ↔ active ancestor found | IR0 cleanup and E1→E0 release occur before returning |
> | J. Rejection ↔ caller resumes | Native reservation is already released; caller continues only under existing cut/receipt rules |
>
> No rejected attempt clears membership, claim, pending state or the cut.
>
> **Future qualification additions**
>
> Common oracle: fixed isolated topology; exact artifact/runtime identities; positive target witness; candidate admission/result evidence captured before ambient cleanup. A missing intended boundary is **INCONCLUSIVE**, not success.
>
> | History | Required witness and outcome |
> |---|---|
> | F pre-marker | Trace/monitor boundary before first body marker, with F visible in ancestry; finalization rejects |
> | F after marker | Exact F frame and marker; same rejection |
> | Receipt/classification active | Canonical frame and original membership state; rejection leaves it unchanged |
> | Retention active | Retention frame/capture boundary; no finalization admission or membership shift |
> | Dequeue/finish active | Exact continuation identity; rejection preserves one-shot continuation |
> | No active ingress | Complete scan, valid semantic record and E3 witness; no skipped ancestors |
> | Same-owner reentry | E1/E3 identity already present; second attempt cannot change it |
> | New Python ingress after reservation | Attempt blocks on actual execution; after return it obeys cut rules |
> | Clean rejection after reservation | E1 observed, rejection cause observed, E0 published before caller resumes |
> | Native pending recording during scan | Native generation/recording witness; no Python callback or pending clearing inside scan |
> | Native writer contention | Shared-token contention; losing writer produces zero effects |
> | Initial-frame allocation failure | NULL initial acquisition leads to rejection, never empty-ancestry admission |
> | Ancestor allocation failure | Error preserved, reservation released and all scanner references accounted |
> | Code/frame mismatch | No partial scan accepted |
> | Existing error state | Exact original error retained; no reservation, formatting or replacement |
> | Inspection cleanup | Refcount/ownership instrumentation proves no scanner-induced frame/code destruction |
> | Failure after E3 | EF/result retained; no E0 reset, replay or cleanup-as-success |
> | Native crash | Parent records only independently observed evidence; no assumed release/publication |
>
> Pre-marker instrumentation establishes a reentry stress schedule, not proof that production instrumentation exists. Native producer tests must not claim that a Python producer ran while another thread retained the GIL.
>
> **Exact admission contract**
>
> > After safe runtime/profile authentication, the existing source owner may reserve the native writer slot provisionally. With the GIL retained and no Python execution, the admission operation traverses the current live ancestry through the audited public APIs, rejects every canonical census-code match, validates the pre-established association and semantic facts, and discharges all inspection references. Only then may it atomically publish one-shot finalization admission. Precommit rejection synchronously releases the provisional reservation before Python resumes. Postcommit failure retains admitted responsibility and never restores eligibility. Runtime-pending signal recording remains permitted throughout. No frame-local inspection, internal-frame access, pending-state mutation or Python-finally release is used.
>
> **Effect on the six findings**
>
> - **BLOCKING 1:** R0 remains a prospective correction; scanner references now have their separate ownership contract.
> - **BLOCKING 2:** R1 gains an operational I witness.
> - **BLOCKING 3:** publication can bind to E3’s owner/attempt/census/semantic identities.
> - **MAJOR 4:** this active-ingress admission boundary is now a **CORRECTED-CANDIDATE input**.
> - **MAJOR 5:** incorporate the admission oracles above.
> - **MAJOR 6:** bind the public API paths, stock evaluator/GIL profile and code census into runtime/build qualification.
>
> No finding is independently reviewed PASS by this task. C2 is unchanged.
>
> Resume the final six-finding architecture integration with this admission contract. V4 remains unavailable and unreconstructed. All existing I1–I4/H8/Gap/V7 limits and the Case-F ambient-cleanup distinction remain unchanged. I5, the native primitive and all stated production capabilities remain unqualified.
>
> **A — ACTIVE-INGRESS ADMISSION BOUNDARY RESOLVED; FINAL ARCHITECTURE INTEGRATION MAY RESUME**

> ## 5. B2.1 selected representation
>
> **Select option B: an explicitly owned native anchor, separate from the Python collectible graph.**
>
> This candidate admits **one published source-lifetime binding per closed process profile**. This restriction applies to prospective CS-R1, not existing I1–I4 APIs. The Registry may already contain other associations; CS-R1 still validates current membership and selected-work identity.
>
> The representation consists of:
>
> 1. **Process-lifetime native profile anchor.**
>    - Created during S3/Phase-A bootstrap.
>    - Owns the metadata bundle.
>    - Owns one native cell lease from successful binding construction until successful extra-root release.
>    - Does not select work, grant admission or perform cleanup.
>
> 2. **Native cell.**
>    - Ordinary native allocation, not a Python object.
>    - Holds exactly one owning Registry reference while root-held.
>    - Holds immutable lifetime/profile/table identities, native control state and bounded evidence.
>    - Stores Association/work identities only for comparison after current root traversal.
>
> 3. **Exact Python-visible SourceAuthority handle.**
>    - One immutable native heap-type instance per cell.
>    - Contains a native cell pointer and owns one native cell lease.
>    - Contains no Python root, Association, work or metadata reference.
>    - Is retained canonically by `Association._source_lifetime`.
>
> Alternative assessment:
>
> | Option | Result |
> |---|---|
> | A: fully traversed GC cell alone | Insufficient: an otherwise unreachable cycle can be collected while responsibility remains unresolved. |
> | B: independently owned native anchor | Selected; root ownership exists independently of collection of Python cycles. |
> | C: Python owner plus separate token | Can express similar ownership, but adds another handle/token relationship without reducing the required anchor proof. |
> | D: smaller representation | No smaller representation was established that preserves caller-loss retention and ordinary successful release. |
>
> This is reference ownership, not new K/Q/U authority.
>
> ## 6. B2.1 GC contract
>
> The SourceAuthority handle is a non-GC native heap-type instance:
>
> - Direct base: `object`.
> - No instance dictionary, weak references, subclassing or Python finalizer.
> - No caller-accessible construction.
> - No Python-reference fields beyond its ordinary heap-type reference.
> - No `Py_TPFLAGS_HAVE_GC`, `tp_traverse` or `tp_clear`.
> - Fixed native allocation/deallocation functions.
> - Deallocation drops its native lease, frees handle memory and releases its heap-type reference.
>
> The metadata anchor independently retains the handle type, so that type decrement is non-last during supported execution.
>
> The Registry reference is **not an omitted reference owned by the handle**. It belongs to the separately anchored native cell. Handle deallocation cannot release it.
>
> Current Registry/Association/Invocation containers retain their existing CPython GC behavior. The external owning Registry reference prevents their reachable responsibility graph from becoming an isolated collectible cycle. After successful root release, ordinary GC may collect that graph when its other owners disappear.
>
> This distinction follows CPython’s requirement that extension containers account for their own Python references; it does not justify concealing an ordinary container-owned edge. [CPython 3.14.5 GC contract](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Doc/c-api/gcsupport.rst)
>
> ## 7. B2.1 metadata lifetime
>
> The native profile anchor owns a finite, immutable bundle containing:
>
> - Expected-schema identity and complete S3 table.
> - Loaded project/native type identities.
> - Required descriptor/member identities.
> - Expected class-dictionary entries.
> - Required function/code/default/keyword-default/closure identities.
> - Required singleton and enum identities.
> - Exact associated module objects and native artifact identity.
> - Profile eligibility latch.
>
> References are acquired before canonical responsibility. Each retained object has an explicit owning entry; identity deduplication does not remove required ownership.
>
> The bundle is retained for the supported process lifetime—**ER2**, not speculative teardown. Successful cell completion does not destroy it. A failed bootstrap after acquiring references permanently makes that bootstrap ineligible and retains its bounded partial bundle until process termination.
>
> Consequences:
>
> - Removing a module name from `sys.modules` cannot destroy the owned module.
> - Module/type names are never ownership evidence.
> - Cell destruction requires no metadata decrements.
> - P1 evidence readers need only native scalar data.
> - No reseal, second bootstrap or module-unload/reinitialization cycle is admitted.
>
> Interpreter finalization remains outside the valid Python-object access interval.
>
> ## 8. B2.1 unresolved-failure retention
>
> After BF:
>
> - The anchor retains its native cell lease.
> - The cell retains the extra Registry reference.
> - The Registry retains its existing association graph.
> - Association retains SourceAuthority; SourceAuthority retains its native lease.
> - The metadata bundle remains retained.
> - New root-capable entry is permanently forbidden.
> - Evidence-only reading remains permitted.
> - Binding replacement, new source lifetime and source-work reuse are forbidden.
>
> Evidence storage is fixed-size: one immutable E2/result area plus a bounded atomic diagnostic record. No list grows per rejection or reader operation. Attempt counters cannot wrap into reusable identity; exhaustion rejects further entry.
>
> The retained graph includes everything already owned by the Registry, potentially more than one Association. The finite claim therefore requires the closed-profile prohibition on new work and replacement lifetimes. It is **finite retained responsibility**, not successful cleanup or leak-free operation.
>
> ## 9. B2.1 successful release / ER classification
>
> The additional Registry decrement is **ER0**, conditional on a current proof immediately before it:
>
> 1. Exact root and exact association map.
> 2. Selected work still maps to selected Association.
> 3. Selected Association’s `registry` slot still strongly owns the exact root.
> 4. No other root-capable operation exists.
> 5. No Python, GC, teardown or canonical writer can intervene.
> 6. All borrowed descendant uses finish before clearing the native root slot.
>
> Then:
>
> - Clear the native root slot once.
> - Decrement the former owning reference once.
> - Publish root-released state.
> - Drop the anchor’s cell lease.
>
> The Association-owned reference makes the decrement non-last. Instrumentation contributes no reference to that proof.
>
> A failed release proof after E3 produces retained failure, not precommit rejection or speculative decrement.
>
> ## 10. B2.1 shutdown boundary
>
> Supported success ends before interpreter finalization begins.
>
> Unsupported:
>
> - `Py_Finalize`/reinitialization while a cell may operate.
> - Subinterpreter teardown/reuse.
> - Extension unloading.
> - Python-object access after interpreter finalization starts.
>
> At process/interpreter shutdown with unresolved responsibility, retained native references are not decremented as a cleanup strategy. Process reclamation establishes neither restoration nor resource completion.
>
> Handle/native-cell destructors perform no source restoration, receipt, cut, repair or COMPLETE transition.
>

>
> The sole writer remains the **exact Registry**, acting on the exact current Association through prospective `_Registry._bind_source_lifetime`.

> Its binding operation authenticates:
>

> - Requested work by exact-string value.

> ## 12. B2.2 state machine
>
> | State | Representation | Legal exits |
> |---|---|---|
> | B0 UNBOUND | Association slot is `None`; no published cell | B1 |
> | B1 CONSTRUCTING | Native-local candidate and construction lease; not externally accessible | B0 on clean failure; B2 on publication; SC |
> | B2 PUBLISHED | Canonical slot owns initialized handle; native cell active/root-held | Precommit rejection remains B2; B3; permanent ineligibility without admission; SC |
> | B3 ADMITTED | E3 committed; original finalizer owns P0 | Closing; BF; SC |
> | Closing | New P0 permanently closed; original finalizer completes release proof | B4; BF; SC |
> | B4 ROOT RELEASED | Root NULL; P0 permanently closed; evidence retained | Ordinary native destruction after leases end |
> | BF UNRESOLVED | Root/anchor retained; P0 permanently closed | Evidence reads; SC/process termination |
> | SC | Execution/lifetime proof lost | No inferred clean transition |

> Profile invalidation before E3 does not manufacture BF or invocation FAILED. It makes further semantic admission ineligible while preserving owned state.
