> ## 13. B2.2 publication linearization
>
> The operation runs under the admitted main-thread GIL without Python execution, signal processing or GIL release during its indivisible portion.
>
> Before publication:
>
> - Native cell and handle are allocated.
> - Root ownership is acquired.
> - All immutable identities, counters, evidence and native leases are initialized.
> - Anchor ownership is established.
> - The Association slot remains `None`.
>
> **Publication linearizes at the canonical assignment of the initialized handle to the authenticated `_source_lifetime` member.**
>
> The selected writer uses the exact native member-assignment path through `PyObject_GenericSetAttr`, restricted to that authenticated member. It is not an E1→E2 helper and cannot be used for canonical reads or arbitrary assignments. With the exact member descriptor, ready type and previous `None`, the admitted setter path performs reference transfer without Python reentry. [Pinned assignment implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/object.c), [member setter](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/descrobject.c)
>
> No externally callable handle exists before this store. Source installation must reauthenticate the resulting canonical edge.
>
> ## 14. Reentrant/repeated binding
>
> | Case | Frozen result |
> |---|---|
> | First legitimate bind | Publish and return the exact handle. |
> | Reentry before native operation begins | Whichever native operation first completes publication wins; later call rejects. |
> | Reentry during native construction | Impossible in the admitted callback-free construction path. |
> | Other Python thread during construction | Cannot enter the GIL-held native operation. |
> | Native/non-main-thread binding attempt | Clean context rejection before construction. |
> | Any repeated bind after publication | Clean rejection; never retrieval or replacement. |
> | Foreign Registry, stale Association, wrong work/Association | Clean rejection before root acquisition. |
> | Wrong profile/lifetime | Clean rejection. |
> | Bind after installation, BF or B4 | Clean rejection. |
>
> Obtaining an existing binding for an otherwise authorized operation means traversing the canonical edge; it does not mean calling bind again.
>
> ## 15. Unpublished/losing disposal
>
> A contender that finds an existing binding rejects **before allocation**. It owns no candidate root or wrapper.
>
> For construction failure:
>
> 1. Candidate is still private and has no source effects.
> 2. Finish all borrowed uses.
> 3. Clear/decrement any candidate root reference while the binding call’s independently live Registry argument still owns it: ER0.
> 4. Remove the unpublished anchor lease.
> 5. Release the private handle.
> 6. Drop the construction lease and free native storage.
>
> A private handle’s last decrement is **ER1**: its exact native destructor has no weakref, Python finalizer, arbitrary Python-owned fields or source cleanup. Its heap-type release is ER0.
>
> Metadata is shared anchor-owned ER2 storage; no losing object releases it.
>
> ## 16. Interruption-before-publication table
>
> | Boundary | Authority/visibility | Required outcome |
> |---|---|---|
> | Before root acquisition | B0; no candidate authority | Clean rejection; later separately initiated bind may occur. |
> | After native allocation | Private B1 | Free native candidate. |
> | After root acquisition | Private B1, independently anchored | ER0 root discharge, then native disposal. |
> | After metadata binding | Private B1 | Drop borrowed bundle pointer; bundle remains ER2. |
> | After handle allocation | Private B1 | Root discharge, ER1 private-handle destruction, native disposal. |
> | Immediately before publication | Still unbound | Same clean disposal if a native failure occurs. |
> | Immediately after publication | B2 is authoritative | Retain binding; never treat it as unpublished. |
> | Process crash at any boundary | SC | No cleanup/publication success inferred. |
>
> Python exceptions/signals do not interrupt the indivisible native portion. A Python exception after return can lose the return value, but cannot remove the published binding. None of these binding failures manufactures invocation FAILED.
>
> ## 17. Source-installation gate
>
> Installation remains forbidden unless it independently confirms:
>
> - Same current Registry/map/work/Association.
> - Same canonical SourceAuthority handle and cell.
> - Fully published binding.
> - Same runtime/table/profile.
> - Eligible, non-invalidated native state.
> - No stale or replaced canonical edge.
> - Existing source-coverage and installation prerequisites.
>
> Binding makes installation *eligible for further checks*. It does not establish R1 or authorize installation.
>
> ## 18. Live-handle model
>
> Select **model A**.
>
> Every operation starts from an independently live exact SourceAuthority handle passed through the ordinary Python/C call boundary. Its native lease keeps the cell alive before pin acquisition.
>
> No API accepts a raw address, cell ID or E2 record as a substitute.
>
> A native evidence worker may receive an already-acquired P1 pin before it starts. That pin transfer carries native storage lifetime only; it carries no Python object access.
>
> A stale but live handle can safely reach the cell and reject based on terminal native state.
>
> ## 19. Pin taxonomy
>
> Here **P0/P1-pin** are native pin names; **P1-ancestry** denotes the preserved public-frame contract.
>
> | Pin | Acquisition | Rights | Terminal behavior |
> |---|---|---|---|
> | P0 root-capable | Live handle; main-thread GIL; eligible B2; no existing P0 | Root traversal, semantic validation, E2/E3 and final release proof | No new acquisition after E3, closing, BF or B4 |
> | P1 evidence-only | Live handle or previously transferred live pin | Acquire-read committed native scalar evidence only | Allowed through B3/closing/BF/B4 |
> | Construction lease | Private native constructor only | Prevent unpublished cell destruction | Ends before returning a published handle or after disposal |
>
> Only one P0 exists. P1 count has a checked finite maximum; overflow rejects rather than wrapping. Construction ownership is not a third operational capability.
>
> ## 20. Pin/destruction protocol
>
> One lock-free native control word contains phase, lease-presence flags, P0 occupancy and the checked P1 count. Its representation and allowed combinations are manifest-bound.
>
> Acquisition/decrement uses compare-exchange with acquire/release ordering. A live handle lease exists before touching the word.
>
> Destruction is possible only after:
>
> - Anchor lease absent.
> - Handle lease absent.
> - P0 absent.
> - P1 count zero.
> - Root NULL.
> - State permits ordinary destruction.
>
> The last lease transition exclusively claims destruction. No second caller can acquire from freed storage because all acquisition routes require an already-live lease.
>
> Native destruction frees native storage only. A root-held last-lease state is an invariant failure, not permission to decrement the root in a destructor.
>
> ## 21. Permanent read closure
>
> The successful finalizer changes B3 to **Closing** after resource terminal publication.
>
> This transition:
>
> - Permanently forbids new P0.
> - Preserves the finalizer’s existing P0.
> - Leaves P1 readers unaffected.
> - Does not mean source CLOSED, invocation COMPLETE or object destruction.
>
> A contender cannot reopen B2. It rejects from native state without reading the root.
>
> ## 22. Root release with pins
>
> Frozen order:
>
> 1. Existing enclosing operation publishes verified restoration.
> 2. R0 callable references are discharged under R0.
> 3. Resource terminal result is published.
> 4. Finalizer commits permanent P0 closure.
> 5. Confirm its P0 is the sole P0.
> 6. Perform final current-edge ER0 proof.
> 7. End borrowed descendant use.
> 8. Clear root slot.
> 9. Perform exactly one ER0 root decrement.
> 10. Publish B4/root-released.
> 11. Drop anchor lease.
> 12. Release finalizer P0.
> 13. Permit protected exit.
>
> The finalizer remains counted as the sole P0 until step 12; no special uncounted reader exists. P1 readers never delay root release, but can delay native storage destruction.
>
> ## 23. BF behavior
>
> The admitted executor records bounded failure evidence, ends borrowed uses and commits BF with P0 entry permanently closed.
>
> Then it releases its P0 while:
>
> - Anchor/root/metadata remain retained.
> - No new finalization attempt is admitted.
> - P1 evidence reading remains possible.
> - No diagnostic root traversal is added.
> - No cleanup/recovery executor is created.
>
> BF does not reset the E3 admission or make source configuration healthy.
>
> ## 24. Successful post-root-release state
>
> Association may continue owning SourceAuthority; SourceAuthority continues owning a rootless cell.
>
> Remaining cell data is native:
>
> - Lifetime/attempt identity.
> - E2/E3 and enclosing result witnesses.
> - Terminal phase.
> - P1 reader accounting.
> - Borrowed pointer to process-lifetime metadata, never used by P1 to dereference Python objects.
>
> Root is NULL and P0 is permanently closed. Later handle/cell destruction performs no semantic cleanup.
>
> Source handoff to O is an enclosing R0/R1 prerequisite of this release sequence, not a result inferred from B4.
>
> ## 25. H1 complete whitelist
>
> **A complete whole-E1→E2 whitelist is not established.** The following is the exact proposed whitelist for CS-R1’s canonical-validation portion.
>
> Common conditions for every admitted row:
>
> - Independently live input.
> - Exact precondition checked before operation.
> - GIL held; stable current owners; no teardown.
> - No Python callback, descriptor invocation, hashing/equality dispatch, coercion, signal check, pending-call processing or GIL release.
> - No allocation/GC in the admitted path.
> - Borrowed results remain within their owner’s protected lifetime.
> - Unexpected failures stop validation; no diagnostic formatting occurs.
>
> | Exact operation | Purpose/precondition | Classification; ownership | Path |
> |---|---|---|---|
> | `Py_TYPE` and C pointer equality | Exact live-object category and identity | Public full C API; borrowed/scalar | Validation |
> | `PyDict_Size` | Exact dict cardinality | Public/Stable API; scalar | Validation |
> | `PyDict_Next` | Exact stable dict entries; local position starts at zero | Public/Stable API; borrowed key/value | Validation |
> | `PyTuple_GET_SIZE` | Exact tuple length | Public full-API macro; scalar | Validation |
> | `PyTuple_GET_ITEM` | Exact tuple, proven bounds | Public full-API macro; borrowed item | Validation |
> | `PyUnicode_GET_LENGTH` | Exact Unicode length | Public full-API representation accessor; scalar | Validation |
> | `PyUnicode_KIND`, `PyUnicode_DATA`, `PyUnicode_READ` | Exact Unicode; valid kind; checked index | Public full-API representation accessors; borrowed buffer/scalar | Validation |
> | `PyLong_AsLongAndOverflow` | Exact `PyLong_Type` before call | Public API; scalar; no coercion branch admitted | Validation |
> | `PyType_GetDict` | Retained, ready, exact authenticated type | Public API; new dict reference | Validation |
> | `PyGen_GetCode` | Exact live generator | Public full API; new code reference | Validation |
> | `PyFunction_GetCode` | Exact authenticated function | Public API; borrowed required code | Validation |
> | `PyFunction_GetDefaults` | Exact authenticated function | Public API; borrowed or NULL-as-absent | Validation |
> | `PyFunction_GetKwDefaults` | Same | Public API; borrowed or NULL-as-absent | Validation |
> | `PyFunction_GetClosure` | Same | Public API; borrowed or NULL-as-absent | Validation |
> | `PyErr_Occurred` | Attached admitted thread state | Public API; borrowed observation only | Entry/error check |
> | `Py_DECREF` | Only the explicitly proved ER0 temporaries | Public macro; non-last | Validation/rejection |
> | Fixed-size `memcpy` | Authenticated object-reference slot | C operation; borrowed pointer result | Validation |
> | Checked integer arithmetic, branch, fixed-array access | Native lengths, masks and indices | C scalar operations | Both |
> | `atomic_load_explicit`, `atomic_store_explicit`, `atomic_compare_exchange_strong_explicit` | Manifest-bound lock-free native state | C11 atomics; no Python ownership | Both |
> | Fixed native rejection/evidence stores | Preallocated storage only | Native | Both |
>
> No generic attribute getter, Python iterator, dictionary lookup, UTF-8 conversion or arbitrary sequence/numeric API is admitted.
>
> The pinned implementations support the stated dictionary, type-dictionary and function-getter ownership distinctions. [Dictionary implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/dictobject.c), [type dictionary getter](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/typeobject.c), [function getters](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/funcobject.c)
>
> **Open boundary:** P1-ancestry’s exact helpers are not added speculatively. Public frame materialization can allocate; `PyThreadState_GetFrame` also clears an error on failed materialization. The checkpoint does not establish whether those operations occur inside the interval now required to be allocation-free. [Pinned thread-state accessor](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Python/pystate.c), [frame materialization](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Python/frame.c)
>
> ## 26. Exact string/integer validation
>
> | Family | Selected method |
> |---|---|
> | Registry work keys during runtime traversal | Exact Unicode, nonzero length; current Association.work and selected bound work compared by pointer identity |
> | Work supplied to binding | Exact Unicode; nonallocating code-point comparison against current canonical map keys |
> | Fixed preparation/composition labels | Exact Unicode; length plus canonical code-point comparison against fixed native labels |
> | Class-image dictionary names | Same nonallocating comparison against retained expected names; corresponding values by identity |
> | Integer cell keys | Exact `PyLong_Type`, then `PyLong_AsLongAndOverflow`; accept only result zero with no overflow |
> | `None`, booleans, `_CUT`, profile/outcome singletons | Exact retained pointer identity |
>
> No interning assumption is required for equal string labels. Exact integers exclude bool and subclasses before conversion; the pinned exact-long branch avoids `__index__` and owning temporaries. [Unicode accessors](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Include/cpython/unicodeobject.h), [integer conversion](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/longobject.c)
>
> Finite container contracts:
>
> | Container | Representation |
> |---|---|
> | Registry map | Exact dict; nonempty exact-string keys; exact Associations with reverse identities |
> | Fault dictionaries | Exact dict; nonempty means rejection; no formatting of contents |
> | First/claim/entry cells | Exact dict; at most key zero; exact object-token value |
> | Preparation | Exact dict; only zero-token and `"configuration"` tuple entries |
> | Configuration | Exact tuple of length two; current facts and binding by identity |
> | Composition | Exact dict; only `"adapter"` and `"acquisition"` with exact role types |
> | Adapter attempt/dequeue | Exact bounded zero-key object-token cells |
> | Bridge errors | Exact dict; success requires empty |
> | Class-image dictionaries | Exact size and complete expected name/value matching; no order dependency |
>
> ## 27. Raw slot-load primitive
>
> Select a **fixed-size byte copy into a local `PyObject *` representation**.
>
> Before loading:
>
> 1. Containing object is live independently of this load.
> 2. Exact role/type is authenticated.
> 3. A compiled internal role selects the immutable S3 entry.
> 4. Defining class/member/object-reference representation match.
> 5. Offset, object extent and alignment were checked at bootstrap.
> 6. Runtime arithmetic verifies the offset and pointer-size extent without overflow.
>
> The helper copies exactly `sizeof(PyObject *)` bytes from the authenticated slot representation into the local pointer object. This avoids dereferencing a fabricated pointer-to-pointer lvalue with unresolved aliasing assumptions.
>
> NULL is a native rejection. Non-NULL is borrowed; nested access requires its own representation/type check.
>
> The admitted Darwin arm64 build must establish pointer width, object layout, compiler behavior and actual load expansion. No atomic slot load is required when GIL, root lifetime and writer exclusion are genuine. Atomicity cannot repair any missing premise.
>
> ## 28. Python-error policy
>
> For the canonical-validation portion:
>
> | Situation | Required behavior |
> |---|---|
> | Error already set | Reject before semantic loads; leave error untouched |
> | Helper unexpectedly sets error | Stop immediately; preserve indicator; native reason records helper failure |
> | Semantic rejection without Python error | Record native reason; construct Python diagnostic only after protected release |
> | Temporary cleanup | ER0 releases only; no fetch/normalize/format/clear |
> | Returning to Python | Preserve existing error; otherwise create a fixed diagnostic after E1 release |
>
> An impossible representation/native corruption is SC, not a promise that an API’s arbitrary error path is safe.
>
> This policy cannot yet be claimed for all of E1→E2 because the preserved P1 helper/error behavior and ordering remain unspecified in the accessible fixed contract.
>
> ## 29. Temporary-reference inventory
>
> | Reference | Owner/lifetime | Release class |
> |---|---|---|
> | Registry root | Anchored cell; entire root-held lifetime | Successful ER0; unresolved retention |
> | Current slot results | Current strong parent edges | Borrowed; no decrement |
> | Dict keys/values | Stable exact dict | Borrowed |
> | Tuple elements | Stable exact tuple | Borrowed |
> | Unicode data | Live immutable exact string | Borrowed |
> | Function getter results | Retained function | Borrowed; optional NULL distinguished |
> | `PyGen_GetCode` result | Temporary plus live generator’s executable ownership | ER0 |
> | `PyType_GetDict` result | Temporary plus live retained type ownership | ER0 |
> | Metadata bundle references | Explicit process anchor | ER2 |
> | Existing Python error | Interpreter-owned; only observed | No acquired reference |
> | Private unpublished handle | Native constructor | ER1 destructor; its type release ER0 |
> | Published handle during construction return | Canonical Association independently owns it | ER0 for a discarded local reference |
> | P1-ancestry references | Preserved separate IR0 contract | Exact inventory/order still required |
>
> `PyType_GetDict` is deliberately included during runtime class-image validation as well as bootstrap; eliminating it would require a separate equivalent image proof.
>
> `PyGen_GetCode` increments the returned code reference in the pinned implementation. [Generator implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/genobject.c)
>
> No descendant convenience INCREF is introduced.
>
> ## 30. Callback-free bounded claim
>
> For the listed canonical-validation operations, under their exact preconditions, the proposed interval performs no Python execution, allocation, GC, signal processing, GIL release or callback-capable last decrement.
>
> **This is not yet a claim about the complete E1→E2 interval.**
>
> The smallest missing fact is:
>
> > What exact P1-ancestry operations and reference discharges occur after E1 acquisition, and does the frozen contract already place potentially allocating public-frame materialization outside the interval required here to be allocation-free?
>
> Moving P1, pre-materializing frames or weakening “allocation-free” would be architectural choices; none is silently selected.
>
> ## 31. MX setup classes
>
> Use exactly:
>
> - **C:** canonical history.
> - **D:** safe non-authoritative decoy.
> - **N:** separately authenticated negative artifact.
> - **I:** fixed native instrumentation.
> - **O:** external/parent observation.
> - **Q:** previously permitted Q2 pre-E3 instrumentation only.
>
> D/N/Q never manufacture canonical K/Q/U. Instrumented native evidence contains scalars and IDs, not extra candidate Python owners.
>
> ## 32. Target-acknowledgement contract
>
> Each history records:
>
> - Artifact/profile/run identity.
> - Intended gate ID.
> - Attempt/lifetime ID.
> - Target-reached marker.
> - Current-edge attribution where relevant.
> - Candidate disposition.
> - Protected interval boundaries.
> - Cleanup-start marker.
>
> Target and disposition are separate. Missing target is INCONCLUSIVE. A forbidden effect can independently establish FAIL even when a normal target marker is absent.
>
> E2/E3 markers come from their actual commit operations, never fixture assignments.
>
> ## 33. Internal-gate decoy rule
>
> A decoy may test only the gate it actually reaches.
>
> For internal comparisons, a qualification-only helper harness may supply a non-authoritative comparison operand **after canonical traversal has identified the real current object**. Its claim is limited to that comparison helper.
>
> It does not prove that an end-to-end candidate can acquire a stale canonical graph.
>
> Impossible current states are covered by source/invariant proof or safely rejected negative artifacts. They are not manufactured by editing canonical dictionaries.
>
> ## 34. Writer-attempt oracles
>
> Three different producer protocols are required:
>
> | Producer | Positive evidence |
> |---|---|
> | Python writer | Native precursor marks “about to acquire GIL”; handshake establishes validation active; effect marker cannot precede protected exit |
> | Native configuration writer | Exact reservation-acquisition attempt and failed/blocked result while candidate owns E1; zero source effect |
> | Runtime signal producer | Actual permitted signal generation and native recording; no Python signal processing inside protected interval |
>
> A Python thread holding the GIL does not let another Python thread execute a Python “attempt” marker inside the interval. That attempt must be acknowledged before GIL acquisition in the native producer path.
>
> ## 35. Pin/destruction oracles
>
> A live handle must exist before a pin attempt.
>
> Required native markers are:
>
> `HANDLE_LIVE → PIN_ATTEMPT → PIN_RESULT → USE_BEGIN/NO_USE → PIN_RELEASE`
>
> Destruction has:
>
> `LAST_LEASE_ATTEMPT → DESTROY_CLAIMED → FREE`
>
> The impossible combination “object destroyed while a valid handle still owns its lease” is an invariant violation. A fixture must not create a dangling pointer to simulate a legitimate acquisition.
>
> Native barriers establish P1 overlap and contender timing. A P0 contender can test busy/closed rejection; it cannot create a second valid P0.
>
> ## 36. Root-release/ER0 oracle
>
> The release operation emits preallocated scalar acknowledgements at:
>
> 1. Current map selected exact Association.
> 2. Reverse Registry edge matched.
> 3. New P0 closed.
> 4. Sole finalizer P0 confirmed.
> 5. Root held.
> 6. Borrowed uses ended.
> 7. Root slot cleared.
> 8. Root decrement executed.
> 9. Root-released publication completed.
>
> The instrumentation records identities without owning references. Its source audit must prove no observer INCREF, GC edge or hidden wrapper-to-Registry reference.
>
> Survival alone is not the oracle. The oracle combines independently audited strong-edge semantics with actual current-edge and decrement acknowledgements.
>
> A helper-only release test does not qualify enclosing restoration/resource publication.
>
> ## 37. Callback-free interval oracle
>
> Two kinds of evidence are required:
>
> - **Source/artifact proof:** closed call graph, permitted operations, reference releases, generated native code and instrumentation equivalence.
> - **Execution evidence:** actual protected entry, operation/exit markers, native callback/GIL/GC/pending-path observations appropriate to the exact artifact.
>
> An experiment cannot prove a universal negative by observing one quiet run.
>
> The complete interval oracle remains pending the P1 ordering question. No “no allocation” PASS may exclude P1 operations while labelling itself whole-E1→E2.
>
> ## 38. Artifact-equivalence contract
>
> Transfer requires identical relevant:
>
> - Project source and schema.
> - Native cell representation and ownership.
> - GC/type/deallocation behavior.
> - Binding/pin/closure protocol.
> - Raw-load body.
> - Helper/error/reference behavior.
> - Memory ordering.
> - E2/E3 commit logic.
> - Source effects.
>
> Permitted differences are fixed scalar observation storage and native barriers. Barriers must not release the GIL, acquire Python references or replace reservation operations.
>
> Helper harnesses, negative artifacts and Q2 histories retain narrower claims. Their results do not automatically qualify production success.
>
> ## 39. Complete corrected qualification matrix
>
> **The whole-target matrix is not closed**, because the complete P1-inclusive interval target cannot yet be specified faithfully. The following is the concrete prospective matrix for the resolved mechanisms and identified helper boundaries.
>
> Common row contract:
>
> - **Topology:** isolated candidate process; one canonical binding; parent observer. Additional native/Python actors are named below.
> - **Deadline:** 12 seconds.
> - **Synchronization:** explicit native handshake/barrier, never sleep-only.
> - **PASS:** target reached, expected disposition and attributed evidence.
> - **FAIL:** contradictory disposition or independently observed forbidden effect.
> - **INCONCLUSIVE:** absent target, invalid fixture, missing attribution or timeout without decisive evidence.
> - **Cleanup:** capture and commit candidate evidence first; cleanup cannot upgrade it.
> - **No execution is authorized by this matrix.**
>
> Every row inherits these rules.
>
> | ID | Setup | Exact precondition / actor / synchronization | Positive target and candidate evidence | Expected disposition / bounded limitation |
> |---|---|---|---|---|
> | A1 | C+O | Published binding; initiating references dropped before collection | Anchor/root-held and canonical binding identities | Retained; tests caller-loss ownership |
> | A2 | C+I+O | A1; ordinary GC before E1 | GC completed, same binding/root available at later valid entry | Survives; no GC during validation |
> | A3 | C+I+O | Canonically produced BF; caller loss then GC | BF/root-held, same native cell | Retained; no successful cleanup claim |
> | A4 | C+I | Successful enclosing release, then ordinary owner loss/GC | B4, handle release and last native lease | Native destruction without source effects |
> | A5 | C+D+I | Module name removed through admitted setup before seal, or removal attempted after seal | Module/type references remain independently owned; eligibility result | Ownership survives; prohibited later mutation invalidates |
> | A6 | C+I | Live P0/P1 with metadata anchor present | Bundle identity and owned type/module chain | Metadata remains live |
> | A7 | N+O | Unsupported unload/finalization artifact or entry request | Pre-entry profile gate | Rejection; does not simulate safe use after teardown |
> | B1 | C+I | Exact current unbound Association | Publication member-store marker; fully initialized cell | One binding published |
> | B2 | C+I | Two admitted calls serialized at native-entry barrier | First publication, second bound check | First wins; second rejects before allocation |
> | B3 | C+Q | Reentry before outer native entry | Inner publication and outer bound rejection | Same one-shot outcome; pre-E3 only |
> | B4 | C+D | Published binding in B2/B3/BF/B4 | Exact repeated-bind gate | Reject each; no retrieval/replacement |
> | B5 | C+D | Foreign Registry/work/Association/profile operands | Exact public binding-validation gate | Reject before root acquisition; interface claim |
> | B6 | C+N+I | Native allocation failure before root acquisition | Allocation-failure marker, no root/publication | Clean failure; B0 remains |
> | B7 | C+N+I | Failure after root acquisition | Root acquisition and ER0 discharge markers | Clean disposal; no source effect |
> | B8 | C+N+I | Failure after metadata binding | Candidate disposal; shared bundle unchanged | No per-cell metadata decrement |
> | B9 | C+N+I | Handle allocation failure or later prepublication failure | Root discharge, private-handle/native disposal | No partial binding |
> | B10 | C+I+O | Interruption after native publication returns | Canonical edge remains bound | Retained publication; no rollback inference |
> | B11 | C+D | Installation request before publication | Installation binding gate | Reject; zero source effect |
> | B12 | C+I | Valid publication; installation eligibility check only | Exact binding/profile/current-edge checks | Eligibility only, not installation success |
> | C1 | C+I | Live handle, B2, no P0 | Actual P0 CAS succeeds | One root-capable pin |
> | C2 | C+I | P0 already active; contender acknowledges attempt | Busy/closed result and no-use marker | Reject contender |
> | C3 | C+I | P1 acquired from live handle, then transferred to native reader | Pin acquired before worker use | Native evidence access only |
> | C4 | C+I | P1 reader overlaps B3/Closing/B4 | Barrier overlap, acquire-read committed evidence | Reader survives; no root access |
> | C5 | C+I | Last handle lease drops while P1 exists | Last-handle marker, no destruction until reader release | Destruction delayed |
> | C6 | C+I | B4, no anchor/P0, final P1 release | Last-lease CAS and free marker | Exactly one destruction |
> | C7 | C+D | Live stale handle after B4/BF | Terminal native gate; no root-use marker | P0 rejects; P1 allowed |
> | D1 | C+I | Real enclosing success prerequisites present | Resource terminal precedes Closing | Permanent new-P0 closure |
> | D2 | C+I | Closing finalizer holds sole P0 | Sole-P0/current-edge proof | Proceed to release body |
> | D3 | C+I | D2 with no observer Python ownership | Clear/decrement/released markers | Exactly one ER0 discharge |
> | D4 | C+D+I | Release-proof helper compared with safe non-authoritative wrong edge | Intended comparison reached | Reject comparison; helper-only claim |
> | D5 | C+I | B4 plus new P0 attempt | Read-closed gate | No reopening |
> | E1 | C+I+O | Actual admitted partial/uncertain failure | BF commit and root/anchor retention | No release/retry |
> | E2 | C+D | BF plus bind/finalize attempts | Bound/BF gates | Reject; evidence-only access remains |
> | E3 | C+I+O | BF over repeated observation/GC history | Fixed storage/counters; no new lifetime allocation | Finite retention; no healthy cleanup |
> | F1 | C+I | Every listed helper with valid canonical operands | Exact helper and valid-result markers | Valid canonical-read subset |
> | F2 | D+I | Wrong exact type for each helper family | Type gate before helper | Reject without dispatch; helper/interface claim |
> | F3 | N+I | Wrong schema/table/role/bounds/alignment artifact | Named pre-read gate | Reject before raw load |
> | F4 | D+I | Safe helper fixture with NULL required slot result | NULL gate, no nested dereference | Native rejection; no malformed live memory |
> | F5 | C+I | Existing error established before canonical validation | Error-entry gate; preserved indicator at exit | Reject before semantic load |
> | F6 | N+I | Controlled helper-error return in separate helper harness | Error-detection and cleanup markers | Preserve error; harness-only error-path claim |
> | F7 | C+I | Generator/type-dict owning temporary | Acquisition and ER0 release with independent owner proof | No last-reference destruction |
> | F8 | D+I | Release precondition deliberately absent in a standalone ownership helper | ER0 eligibility gate | Reject; never perform speculative decrement |
> | F9 | D+I | Wrong dict/tuple/length/string/integer operands | Specific representation gate | Reject before dependent access |
> | F10 | N+I | Instrumentation has allocation or Python-owner differences | Artifact-equivalence audit | Ineligible; no production transfer |
> | G1 | C+I | Canonical winning cut and valid graph | Current map/Association/ledger/epoch/cell acknowledgements | Assigned K observed |
> | G2 | C+I | Lawful earlier-epoch adapter dequeue, later settlement/cut | Current adapter role and dequeue evidence | Accept minimized adapter obligation |
> | G3 | C+I | Canonically released, error-free bridge | Current role/authority/fault/terminal observations | Accept assigned bridge obligation |
> | G4 | C+I | Canonically unresolved adapter/bridge/fault | Exact relevant canonical cell observed | Reject assigned Q/U |
> | G5 | C+D+I | Stale retained identity supplied only to comparison harness | Current traversal first, comparison gate second | Reject stale comparison; not end-to-end stale-state manufacture |
> | H1 | C+I+O | Python writer native precursor waits for interval marker | GIL acquisition attempt overlaps interval | No Python writer effect before exit |
> | H2 | C+I+O | Native configuration writer attempts reservation | Attempt plus occupied reservation | Zero forbidden configuration effect |
> | H3 | C+I+O | Permitted actual signal producer during validation | Generation/native recording and protected interval | Recording allowed; processing deferred |
> | I1 | C+I | All assigned checks succeed | Actual E2 release-store marker | Immutable same-attempt witness |
> | I2 | C+I | Same finalizer and continuous reservation | Actual E3 CAS after E2 | One admission |
> | I3 | C+D | Old E2/lifetime/address record presented | Certificate/handle interface gate | Reject as authority |
> | I4 | C+I | P1 reader overlaps E2 publication | Acquire observation of commit marker and payload | No partial payload treated committed |
> | J1 | N+I+O | Production/instrumented artifacts compared | Complete equivalence record | Transfer only unchanged properties |
> | J2 | D+I | Target marker missing or wrong gate reached | Oracle evaluator receives separate target/disposition fields | INCONCLUSIVE for intended target |
> | J3 | D+I | Cleanup success after recorded candidate failure | Candidate commit precedes cleanup marker | Failure remains failure |
> | K1 | C+I+O | Parent terminates at pre-E2 native boundary | Last completed native marker | SC; no E2 inferred |
> | K2 | C+I+O | Parent terminates after E2, before E3 | E2 present; E3 absent | No admission inferred |
> | K3 | C+I+O | Process exits with BF retained | BF before exit | Physical reclamation only |
> | K4 | N+O | Corrupting/raw-load-fault artifact is not safely admissible | Artifact rejection/source audit | No deliberate corruption test required |
>
> **Not yet a valid row:** “whole E1→E2 executes without allocation/error clearing.” Its exact P1-inclusive precondition and call graph are missing. It is not disguised as F1 or a quiet-run observation.
>

> ## 41. Cross-target consistency
>
> The selected mechanisms are compatible:
>
> - Anchor ownership exists before publication.
> - GC does not govern the external root owner.
> - Private construction disposal occurs outside the semantic-validation whitelist.
> - P0 closure does not tear down metadata.
> - P1 evidence readers require no Python metadata access.
> - Metadata ownership does not retain successful cells.
> - BF retains root and metadata while closing P0.
> - Raw loads require exactly P0, not a stronger hidden pin.
> - Root-release instrumentation owns no Registry reference.
> - Qualification barriers cannot change production memory ordering or GIL behavior.
>
> The unresolved P1-inclusive whitelist prevents a complete cross-target E1→E2 claim.
>
> ## 42. Frozen-input contradiction test
>
> | Input | Result |
> |---|---|

> ## 3. Exact P1 ordering
>
> The recovered contract establishes this ordering:
>
> 1. Runtime/profile eligibility.
> 2. Existing-error rejection before reservation or frame inspection.
> 3. Provisional E1 reservation.
> 4. Public current-thread ancestry inspection and canonical-code comparisons.
> 5. Semantic revalidation.
> 6. All inspection references discharged before E2.
> 7. E2 validation publication.
> 8. Same-attempt E3 one-shot admission.
>
> Thread/interpreter identity must be authenticated before relying on the selected thread’s frames. The report admits the necessary public identity getters.
>
> Linearization points:
>
> | Event | Established boundary |
> |---|---|
> | E1 reservation | Successful owner/attempt-bound native CAS |
> | Ancestry completion | Complete traversal with clean terminal NULL and no prohibited match |
> | E2 | Validation publication after ancestry, semantic validation and inspection-reference discharge |
> | E3 | One-shot finalization-admission transition |
>
> The original transition description says:
>
> > “scan complete live ancestry; reject census matches; revalidate semantic record; discharge scanner references.”
>
> This establishes discharge before E2. It does **not** establish that the final successful frame release precedes semantic revalidation.
>
> ## 4. Interval definitions
>
> | Interval | Meaning |
> |---|---|
> | I0 | Before provisional reservation |
> | I1 | E1 held; ancestry validation incomplete |
> | I2 | Ancestry complete; canonical semantic validation incomplete |
> | I3 | Semantic validation complete through E2 publication |
> | I4 | E2 published through E3 |
>
> E1-A would correspond to ancestry inspection **plus its completed cleanup**. That cleanup-inclusive endpoint is the unresolved historical detail.
>
> E1-B would begin only after that endpoint. Neither name creates another reservation or admission state.
>
> ## 5. Public frame API audit
>
> All findings below concern pinned **CPython v3.14.5**, GIL-enabled execution, valid live inputs and the admitted runtime-hook profile.
>
> | Operation | Declaration / classification | Ownership and behavior |
> |---|---|---|
> | `PyThreadState_Get()` | Public C API | Borrowed attached thread state; invalid attachment is not a recoverable scanner input |
> | `PyThreadState_GetInterpreter(tstate)` | Public C API | Borrowed identity; no allocation or Python dispatch |
> | `PyThread_get_thread_ident()` | Public C API | Native scalar identity; runtime/thread subsystem already initialized |
> | `PyThreadState_GetFrame(tstate)` | `Include/pystate.h`; Stable ABI since 3.10 | New frame reference; may materialize; initial NULL always rejects |
> | `PyFrame_GetBack(frame)` | `Include/cpython/pyframe.h`; public full API, not Limited/Stable | New reference or NULL; may materialize an ancestor |
> | `PyFrame_GetCode(frame)` | `Include/pyframe.h`; Stable ABI since 3.10 | New code reference; no materialization or allocating lookup on valid input |
> | `Py_TYPE` and pointer comparisons | Public C operations | No owning result, coercion, descriptor or rich comparison |
> | `Py_DECREF` | Public reference operation | Safe here only through the specific IR0 non-last proof |
> | `PyErr_Occurred()` | Public C API | Borrowed error observation |
> | `PyErr_GetRaisedException()` | Public; Stable ABI since 3.12 | Transfers error-indicator ownership to the caller |
> | `PyErr_SetRaisedException(exc)` | Public; Stable ABI since 3.12 | Transfers ownership back; safe here only with an empty destination indicator |
>
> The public frame getters do not themselves release the GIL or process signals/pending calls on these admitted paths. `PyFrame_GetCode`’s critical-section macros do not release the GIL in this build configuration. [Pinned declarations](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Include/pyframe.h), [frame implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/frameobject.c), [Stable ABI inventory](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Misc/stable_abi.toml)
>
> Internal implementations were inspected as evidence; no internal frame accessor is proposed for candidate use.
>
> ## 6. Materialization behavior
>
> Initial and ancestor frame acquisition can reach `_PyFrame_MakeAndSetFrameObject` and `_PyFrame_New_NoTrack`. Code retrieval from an existing valid frame does not materialize another frame.
>
> The allocation path:
>
> - Can allocate frame storage.
> - Does not execute Python or run GC under the admitted allocator/runtime profile.
> - Does not unwind frames, release the GIL or process pending calls.
> - Can produce `MemoryError`.
> - Can **schedule later GC** by setting the GC scheduling bit; scheduling is distinct from running collection.
>
> Thus “no GC” must mean no collection execution during the protected interval, not an unchanged GC allocation counter or scheduling bit. [Frame materialization](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Python/frame.c), [GC allocation and scheduling](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Python/gc.c)
>
> This is not valid for arbitrary allocator or reference-tracer hooks. Object allocation dispatches through configured allocator machinery, and new-reference processing includes the reference-tracer path. Those dependencies must remain within the previously required authenticated hook profile. [Allocator dispatch](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/obmalloc.c), [reference initialization](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/object.c)
>
> ## 7. Initial-frame failure
>
> `PyThreadState_GetFrame` clears the error when frame materialization returns NULL. A NULL result can therefore lack an error indicator.
>
> The recovered P1 rule is correct:
>
> - Reject initial NULL.
> - Record **initial ancestry unavailable**.
> - Do not infer empty ancestry.
> - Do not fabricate an OOM diagnosis.
> - Do not enter canonical validation or publish E2/E3.
> - Release any acquired reservation before returning.
>
> A clear error indicator before inspection is essential: otherwise materialization could dispose of a previously saved exception on failure. [Pinned thread-state getter](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Python/pystate.c)
>
> The internal clearing of its newly obtained built-in `MemoryError` is a separately bounded cleanup path, not a general permission to clear arbitrary exceptions.
>
> ## 8. Back-frame failure
>
> After a valid initial frame:
>
> - `PyFrame_GetBack` returning NULL with a clear error indicator means clean ancestry exhaustion.
> - NULL with an error means failed traversal; reject without accepting the scanned prefix.
>
> The pinned implementation can propagate ancestor-materialization failure, despite the documentation’s broader “raises no exceptions” wording. The implementation therefore controls this exact-build failure analysis. [Implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/frameobject.c), [pinned documentation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Doc/c-api/frame.rst)
>
> On failure, P1 transfers the error out, releases IR0 references and E1, then restores the same error without formatting.
>
> ## 9. IR0 ownership/release
>
> | Reference | Independent owner | Established release rule |
> |---|---|---|
> | Initial frame | Its live interpreter frame owns the materialized frame object | After acquiring the next frame, or during rejection cleanup |
> | Each live ancestor | Its own live interpreter/generator frame | Same traversal discipline |
> | Returned code | Live frame’s executable ownership | Immediately after identity comparison |
> | Inspection exception | Ownership transferred from/to error indicator | No destructive scanner release |
> | Thread/interpreter identities | Runtime-owned, borrowed | No DECREF |
>
> Under retained GIL, no Python execution and no teardown, these live frames cannot unwind during scanning. Their scanner references therefore cannot be last references. Completed frames and suspended generators supplied independently are not valid substitutes for current ancestry.
>
> The unresolved point is narrower than this ownership proof: **when the final current-frame reference is released after clean ancestry exhaustion, relative to semantic validation**.
>
> The source proves that an earlier non-last release can be safe. It cannot prove that earlier release was the historically selected P1 sequence.
>
> ## 10. Error-state interaction
>
> | Case | Preserved treatment |
> |---|---|
> | Error already set | Reject before E1 and inspection; preserve unchanged |
> | Initial materialization fails and clears error | Fixed native rejection; no invented exception |
> | Ancestor materialization fails | Transfer error out, IR0 cleanup, E1 release, transfer back |
> | Valid code getter unexpectedly fails | Reject; never treat failure as completed inspection |
> | Canonical match without error | Native rejection reason; no formatting inside reservation |
> | Cleanup with an inspection error | Transfer ownership rather than destructively clear it |
>
> `PyErr_SetRaisedException` decrements an existing destination exception. Its P1 use therefore requires the destination to remain empty. [Error ownership implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Python/errors.c)
>
> The initial failure’s built-in `MemoryError` comes from the cleared freelist or retained last-resort object. Its disposal is bounded by that exact construction, not merely by its exception class name. [MemoryError implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/exceptions.c)
>
> ## 11. P1 allocation classification
>
> **P1-B — frame scan may allocate, but allocation is proved not to execute Python or run GC for the admitted exact runtime.**
>
> This remains conditional on the authenticated GIL-enabled build and permitted allocator/runtime hooks. It is not runtime qualification and does not establish the disputed cleanup ordering.
>

> **D4 — EVIDENCE INSUFFICIENT.**
>

>

> ## 13. Reservation-purpose analysis
>
> E1 protects the native configuration-writer slot and orders competing finalization attempts. It is already held during P1 scanning.
>
> It does not independently:
>
> - Keep native-cell storage alive—that belongs to live-handle/pin ownership.
> - Prevent Python execution—that requires retained GIL and callback exclusion.
> - Stop runtime signal recording.
>
> Moving the scan before E1 would alter the selected native-writer ordering and would not be a naming correction.
>
> ## 14. GIL-purpose analysis
>
> Retaining the GIL, while executing no Python, prevents another Python thread from beginning canonical mutation and prevents the current Python ancestry from advancing or unwinding.
>
> It supports both live-frame ownership and later canonical-container stability.
>
> It does not exclude unrelated native configuration writers or runtime signal producers. E1 excludes the former; the latter remain allowed to record pending state.
>
> ## 15. Mechanical property matrix by interval
>
> “Required” below describes the protected design obligation, not an executed qualification result.
>
> | Property | I0 | I1: ancestry | I2: canonical | I3: E2 | I4: E3 |
> |---|---|---|---|---|---|
> | No Python callback/bytecode | Before-entry rules | Required | Required | Required | Required |
> | No GC/weakref/finalizer execution | Before-entry rules | Required | Required | Required | Required |
> | GIL continuously held | Established before inspection | Required | Required | Required | Required |
> | No signal checking/pending-call processing | Before-entry rules | Required | Required | Required | Required |
> | No canonical Python writer | Not inferred from E1 | GIL/no-callback | GIL/no-callback | Same | Same |
> | No native configuration writer | Reservation not yet held | E1 | Same E1 | Same E1 | Same E1 |
> | No allocation | Not generally required | **Not required** | H1 requires | Required | Required |
> | No error-state mutation | Existing error preserved | P1-specific exceptions | Strict H1 admitted path | Required | Required |
> | No callback-capable last release | Required for protected cleanup | IR0/bounded internal error path | ER0 | Same | Same |
>
> P1 needs stable live ancestry. CS-R1 needs stable canonical owning edges. E2/E3 need continuous exclusion and same-attempt publication. Qualification must observe these separately; allocation-freedom cannot substitute for them.
>
> The exact IR0 boundary between I1 and I2 remains unresolved.

> ## 2. Fixed historical fact
>

> **R3 — ORDERING NEVER FROZEN.**

> ## 3. T1 analysis
>
> T1 is viable.
>
> After complete, clean ancestry exhaustion, the scanner no longer needs its final frame. Releasing its owning reference then:
>
> - Preserves the IR0 independent-owner proof.
> - Leaves ancestry evidence valid under continuous exclusion.
> - Removes scanner ownership from every later semantic path.
> - Gives MX an explicit cleanup-complete boundary.
> - Keeps P1-specific operations outside the canonical H1 interval.
>
> No semantic-validation input requires the released scanner reference.
>
> ## 4. T2 analysis
>
> T2 is also technically viable under the historical constraints.
>
> The final live frame remains independently anchored throughout callback-free, GIL-held semantic validation. Its later decrement can remain IR0 and preserve the error indicator.
>
> However, T2 requires:
>
> - Adding the retained frame to the canonical interval’s ownership inventory.
> - Releasing it on semantic success, rejection and unexpected helper-error paths.
> - Distinguishing semantic completion from subsequent scanner cleanup.
> - Preventing E2 until that additional cleanup finishes.
>
> T2 is not rejected as unsafe. It offers no necessary semantic benefit and creates a larger cleanup obligation.
>
> ## 5. Criteria comparison
>
> | Criterion | Result |
> |---|---|
> | A. Ancestry correctness | Equivalent |
> | B. IR0 non-last proof | Equivalent |
> | C. Callback freedom | Equivalent |
> | D. GC/finalizer freedom | Equivalent |
> | E. Error-state handling | T1 better: fewer later cleanup obligations |
> | F. Continuous E1 | Equivalent |
> | G. Continuous P0 | Equivalent |
> | H. Continuous GIL | Equivalent |

> ## 6. Minimum-lifetime principle
>
> Adopt prospectively, specifically for P1 inspection temporaries:
>
> > Release an inspection-owned reference when inspection no longer needs it, while its established IR0 independent-owner proof remains valid.
>
> This completes reference-lifetime architecture. It does not change canonical state, ancestry detection, reservation semantics or semantic authority.
>
> It does not become a general rule permitting early release of other ownership classes. No governance change is required. The completed candidate still requires independent HIGH-risk review.
>
> ## 7. Selected T1/T2 decision
>
> **Select OPTION T1 — RELEASE AT SCANNER COMPLETION.**
>
> Freeze this successful sequence:
>
> 1. Inspect and compare the current frame’s code.
> 2. Release the returned code reference immediately after comparison.
> 3. Acquire the back-frame result while retaining the current frame.
> 4. For a valid successor, release the predecessor reference and continue.
> 5. For terminal NULL, establish that the error indicator is clear.
> 6. Release the final current-frame reference exactly once under IR0.
> 7. Establish that all scanner-owned frame/code slots are empty and no inspection error is owned.
> 8. Complete E1-A.
> 9. Enter canonical E1-B under the same E1/P0/GIL.
> 10. Complete canonical validation and its required temporary cleanup.
> 11. Publish E2.
> 12. Permit E3 only through the separately constrained same-attempt admission transition.
>
> No scanner-owned reference survives step 7.
>
> ## 8. Selected-option safety proof
>
> At the terminal decrement:
>
> - Clean ancestry exhaustion has already been established.
> - The final frame belongs to current live ancestry.
> - Its executing interpreter/generator frame still independently owns the materialized frame object.
> - No Python execution, GIL release or teardown has occurred.
> - The scanner decrement therefore cannot be last.
> - No frame destruction, locals destruction, weakref/finalizer execution or collection follows from that decrement.
> - A non-last decrement does not change the Python error indicator.
> - E1 and P0 remain held.
>
> The pinned ownership transfer and clearing paths support that independent ownership; the public getters supply separate owning inspection references. [Frame ownership](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Python/frame.c), [public getter implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/frameobject.c)
>
> Ancestry does not become stale when the scanner reference is released: continuous GIL/no-callback execution prevents Python ancestry changes, while E1 continues excluding native configuration writers.
>
> No separate persistent native state bit is required.
>
> ## 9. Error-state consequence
>
> | Case | T1 | T2 |
> |---|---|---|
> | P1 success, clear error | Final IR0 released before semantic entry | Final frame retained |
> | Semantic success | No scanner cleanup remains | Release final IR0 before E2 |
> | Semantic rejection without error | Canonical cleanup only | Canonical cleanup plus retained frame |
> | Unexpected semantic helper error | Preserve error; canonical cleanup only | Preserve error while also releasing frame |
> | Crash/SC | No inferred cleanup beyond observed evidence | Same limitation |
>
> For selected T1, E1-B requires a clear error indicator at entry. An unexpected helper error terminates validation; it does not become an accepted error-mutating path.
>
> P1’s existing rejection-side error transfer remains unchanged. `PyErr_SetRaisedException` is safe for that transfer only with an empty destination indicator, because replacing an existing exception can decrement it. [Pinned error implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Python/errors.c)
>
> ## 10. Failure-path consequence
>
> | Boundary | Frozen treatment |
> |---|---|
> | Active canonical frame | Stop inspection; release code/frame IR0; fixed rejection evidence; release E1 before Python resumes |
> | Initial-frame NULL | Reject as ancestry unavailable; no semantic entry; no fabricated OOM diagnosis |
> | Ancestor failure | Preserve the inspection error through the existing transfer discipline; IR0 cleanup; synchronous reservation release |
> | Ancestry success, semantic rejection | IR0 is already complete; release canonical ER0 temporaries, record rejection and release reservation |
> | Ancestry success, helper error | Same, preserving the error; no protected formatting, normalization or replacement |
> | Failure before E2 publication | No E2/E3; intact-runtime rejection cleanup applies |
> | Uncertainty about whether E2 published | No inferred E3; classify uncertainty/SC according to the existing publication contract |
> | Crash after ancestry success, before semantic entry | Only independently recorded evidence survives; no guaranteed cleanup |
> | Crash during semantic validation | No inferred E2, E3, restoration or reservation release |
>
> An ordinary intact-runtime publication path uses preallocated native storage. Corruption or uncertain execution must not be disguised as a clean publication failure.
>
> No precommit rejection performs source finalization.
>
> ## 11. E1-A/E1-B boundary
>
> Adopt these prospective names:
>
> **E1-A:** reserved ancestry validation, including complete IR0 cleanup.
> **E1-B:** canonical CS-R1 validation under the allocation-free, error-preserving H1 whitelist.
>
> The transition requires:
>
> - Complete successful ancestry traversal.
> - No prohibited canonical match.
> - Every scanner-owned frame/code reference discharged.
> - No locally owned inspection exception.
> - Clear error indicator.
> - Same lifetime, attempt, E1 reservation and P0 pin.
> - Continuously held GIL and writer exclusion.
>
> This is a **control-flow boundary**. Qualification may record it using fixed native evidence. It is not a caller-supplied certificate, persistent admission capability or new semantic state.
>
> The same native invocation proceeds directly; there is no return, wait-for-Python step or release/reacquisition of E1, P0 or GIL.
>
> ## 12. H1 final scope
>
> Adopt:
>
> > The protected admission operation contains two consecutive audited callback-free regions under one continuous E1 reservation, P0 pin and GIL. P1 ancestry validation may perform its exact-build bounded frame materialization, which may allocate but does not execute Python or run GC in the admitted profile. P1 completes by discharging all IR0 inspection references. Canonical CS-R1 validation then executes its exact allocation-free, error-preserving H1 whitelist. Neither region permits Python execution, Python callback/finalizer/weakref execution, GC collection, GIL release, signal/pending processing or admission of an excluded writer.
>
> Qualifications to this wording:
>
> - Runtime signal recording remains allowed.
> - Frame allocation may schedule later GC; collection does not run in the interval.
> - P1 retains its distinct failure/error-transfer contract.
> - “Error-preserving” describes the admitted canonical path. Unexpected helper errors cause rejection and preservation, not continued validation.
> - The exact allocator/runtime-hook restrictions remain required.
>
> The canonical whitelist remains the preceding candidate: exact identity checks; audited dict/tuple/Unicode/integer operations; selected type/function/generator getters; `PyErr_Occurred`; specified ER0 decrements; authenticated fixed-size slot loading; checked native arithmetic, evidence stores and atomics.
>
> P1 frame getters and error-transfer operations are **not** added to E1-B.
>
> ## 13. MX linked oracle
>
> Freeze two linked evidence regions.
>
> **MX-A records:**
>
> - Lifetime/attempt and actual E1/P0 ownership.
> - Ancestry entry.
> - Actual materialization or reuse target where relevant.
> - Canonical comparison outcome.
> - Clean terminal ancestry result for success.
> - Completed scanner-reference discharges.
> - E1-A disposition.

> **MX-B records:**
>
> - Entry after E1-A success and cleanup completion.
> - Exact H1 artifact/whitelist identity.
> - Actual canonical-validation outcome.
> - Required canonical ER0 cleanup.
> - E1-B disposition.
> - E2 publication.
>
> Markers use preallocated scalar storage. Publication to native observers follows the existing release/acquire evidence discipline. They own no Python objects and authorize no later dereference.
>
> A successful complete admission history requires both region PASSes, the ordered transition, E2 after both, and E3 after E2 under the same uninterrupted ownership. A semantic-rejection row may PASS its rejection oracle without being a successful admission history.
>
> Missing targets or attribution remain INCONCLUSIVE. A quiet run is not a universal proof of callback freedom.
>
> ## 14. Qualification delta
>
> These are prospective rows only. All use the inherited exact-artifact, isolated-process, **12-second deadline**, evidence-before-cleanup and PASS/FAIL/INCONCLUSIVE rules.
>
> | Row | Construction and synchronization | Positive oracle |
> |---|---|---|
> | A. Exhaustion → final release | Canonical no-match ancestry; native markers around terminal NULL/error check and final decrement | Clean exhaustion precedes exactly one final IR0 discharge |
> | B. Release → canonical entry | Same invocation; ordered scalar markers after decrement and at E1-B entry | E1-B follows zero outstanding IR0 ownership; same attempt/E1/P0 |
> | C. Semantic rejection | Canonical history reaches an existing false semantic predicate; no direct fixture mutation | E1-A passes, E1-B rejects, no E2/E3; no scanner cleanup remains |
> | D. Semantic helper error | Separately authenticated negative artifact supplies a fixed pre-owned exception at one designated helper-failure boundary | Intended failure branch reached; error preserved; canonical cleanup and rejection; no scanner reference |
> | E. Crash between regions | Parent waits for completed-IR0 marker; separately qualified native barrier precedes E1-B; parent terminates candidate | Containment plus observed markers only; no inferred E1-B/E2/E3 |
> | F. Continuous ownership | Instrument actual E1/P0 acquisition/release sites and region boundaries; source/artifact audit covers GIL-retention path | One uninterrupted E1/P0 ownership interval; no detach/reacquisition |
> | G. Writer across transition | Native writer signals readiness, waits for transition window, performs actual reservation attempt and publishes its result | Attempt witnessed inside held E1; zero configuration effect |
> | H. Actual signal across transition | Parent/native producer is released by transition-window marker; actual signal recording acknowledged natively | Recording occurred; no Python delivery or pending-state processing in the transition |
> | I. No callback | Native entry observation plus audited call graph; no Python trace hook in success path | Transition reached; any forbidden callback entry is FAIL |

## C. B2.1 — INDEPENDENT HIGH-RISK FINDING

**Verdict: FAIL — BLOCKING architecture incompleteness at the preserved SourceAuthority ownership interface.**

### Finding R1: SourceAuthority’s two ownership contracts are not reconciled

CP §13 expressly preserves:

> “explicit SourceAuthority callable slots”

and states that the CS-R1 Registry anchor cannot absorb or replace R0 callable responsibility. See [CP §13](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-four-target-correction-comprehensive-continuation.md:154).

But the selected B2.1 representation defines the published SourceAuthority handle as a non-GC native heap-type instance with:

> “No Python-reference fields beyond its ordinary heap-type reference.”

See [CP Appendix R2 §6](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-four-target-correction-comprehensive-continuation.md:1650), together with Appendix R2 §5 and CP §24.

This is consequential because preserved R0 assigns SourceAuthority ownership of:

- Its SourceLifetimeAdapter and source configuration.
- Explicit per-source F/O callable slots.
- References retained after partial/uncertain restoration.
- Successful slot clearing by the same native finalization invocation.

I checked the original R0 report as additional historical evidence:

- Message `msg_049dffb3f451c098016aa6dbe38da087d0aec81d8571aae3fd`.
- Timestamp `2026-09-13T17:24:16.506Z`.
- 16,608 extracted UTF-8 bytes.
- SHA-256 `b2c4888cf4506ef94428523c84593c2fd17520a3d729e5ee0b15c18a46668856`.

That report distinguishes SourceAuthority **S**, its adapter **A**, and native reference/result cell **C**. It does not supply a previously frozen mapping that reconciles the new handle representation.

**The unresolved choice:** Is the new handle the existing source owner, a separate handle owned by that source owner, or a representation whose source ownership resides elsewhere? The corrected package does not freeze the corresponding ownership edges and their lifetime.

I cannot repair this by assuming a companion owner, adding native callable slots, or interpreting logical ownership as an unspecified indirect relationship. Those choices affect:

- GC/reference accounting.
- Caller-loss and BF retention.
- Binding identity.
- ER1 handle destruction.
- R0 discharge before resource publication.
- Artifact-equivalence requirements.

This is **not a demand to implement I5 now**. It is a current compatibility requirement because B2.1 changes the representation of the named owner while expressly preserving R0.

### Positive B2.1 conclusions

The isolated Registry-root mechanism is substantially improved and coherent:

- An external native anchor owns the cell independently of Python cyclic GC.
- The cell owns the extra Registry reference.
- Metadata has explicit process-lifetime ER2 ownership.
- Caller disappearance does not remove the Registry root.
- Successful root release uses the current Association→Registry strong edge for a non-last decrement.
- BF retains responsibility rather than pretending successful cleanup.
- Forced interpreter finalization/unload is excluded.

These close important parts of the original defect. They do **not** resolve R1’s missing source-owner mapping, so B2.1 cannot receive complete acceptance.

## D. B2.2 — INDEPENDENT HIGH-RISK FINDING

**Verdict: FAIL — BLOCKING rejected-attempt pin-lifetime incompleteness; also dependent on R1.**

### Finding R2: Precommit rejection does not close the P0 unwind contract

The package explicitly specifies P0 release for:

- Successful root release: after B4 publication and anchor-lease release.
- BF: after borrowed uses end and new P0 entry permanently closes.

See [CP §27](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-four-target-correction-comprehensive-continuation.md:326) and Appendix R2 §§22–23.

In contrast:

- The pin protocol defines acquisition conditions and CAS-based decrement machinery.
- B2 precommit rejection remains B2.
- Ancestry rejection specifies IR0 cleanup and synchronous **E1** release.
- Canonical rejection specifies ER0 cleanup, error preservation and “precommit release.”
- The generic pin oracle ends with `PIN_RELEASE`.

These do not establish a complete rejection-path mapping for the **P0 already acquired by the rejecting operation**. See [CP §§18–21](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-four-target-correction-comprehensive-continuation.md:220), [Appendix R2 §§18–20](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-four-target-correction-comprehensive-continuation.md:1863), and [PRE §40](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-independent-review-comprehensive-continuation.md:372).

The unresolved cases include:

1. P0 acquired, but E1 acquisition fails.
2. P0/E1 acquired, initial frame unavailable.
3. Ancestry match, depth rejection or ancestor-materialization failure.
4. T1 completed, then canonical semantic rejection.
5. Unexpected canonical helper error.
6. Pre-E3 invalidation while an operation owns P0.

The text does not connect each case to an exactly-once P0 relinquishment, its relationship to remaining borrowed/native uses and error restoration, and the final ownership state before Python resumes.

“B2 remains B2” does not resolve this: **phase and P0 occupancy are separate fields of the control word**. A returned rejected invocation retaining P0 would leave later admission busy without an admitted finalizer or specified cleanup owner. Prematurely releasing P0 while protected uses remain would violate the stated access contract.

This is an architecture omission, **not an observed runtime leak or use-after-free**. A safe unwind is possible, but choosing it is precisely the implementation decision the acceptance standard forbids leaving open.

### Positive B2.2 conclusions

The following are explicitly resolved:

- One canonical initialized-before-publication binding.
- First publication wins; repeated binding rejects rather than retrieves.
- A loser finding a binding allocates nothing.
- Prepublication root cleanup precedes private-handle destruction.
- Postpublication loss of the return value does not undo binding.
- A live handle lease exists before touching pin state.
- P0 is root-capable; P1 is native evidence-only.
- New P0 entry is forbidden after E3.
- Existing finalizer P0 remains counted through successful root release.
- P1 may retain native storage after root release.
- Last-lease destruction requires root NULL and all relevant leases/pins absent.

Publication before acquiring an *operational* P0 is not itself a defect: construction and handle/anchor leases already protect publication. P0 is required before operational root use.

## E. CS-R1-MAJOR-H1 — INDEPENDENT HIGH-RISK FINDING

**Verdict: NON-PASS for the integrated target because its lifetime/cleanup prerequisites remain incomplete. No additional primitive-whitelist defect was established.**

The exact canonical whitelist is materially stronger than the prior category-level proposal. [CP §28 and Appendix R2 §25](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-four-target-correction-comprehensive-continuation.md:348) constrain the actual operations:

| Operation family | Positive finding |
|---|---|
| `Py_TYPE`, pointer/singleton comparison | Scalar/borrowed operations on independently live inputs |
| `PyDict_Size`, `PyDict_Next` | Exact stable dictionaries; borrowed entries; no dispatched equality/hash lookup |
| Tuple macros | Exact tuple and bounds precede element access |
| Unicode accessors | Length/kind/data/code-point operations; no allocating UTF-8 conversion |
| `PyLong_AsLongAndOverflow` | Exact int excludes coercion; nonzero/overflow rejects |
| `PyType_GetDict` | Owning temporary, independently retained type/dictionary lifetime |
| `PyGen_GetCode` | Owning temporary, independently live generator/code ownership |
| Function getters | Borrowed fields; optional NULL is distinguished from required-value failure |
| `PyErr_Occurred` | Observation only |
| Specified `Py_DECREF` | Only independently proved non-last ER0 releases |
| Fixed-size `memcpy` | Borrowed pointer-representation load after S3/type/extent checks |
| Checked arithmetic, fixed storage and atomics | No dynamic allocation or Python ownership introduced |

I checked the pinned implementations of the dictionary, integer, function, generator and type-dictionary operations. For example, the generator getter increments the returned code reference, and the type-dictionary getter returns a new reference to the existing dictionary. These support the candidate’s ownership classifications. [CPython generator implementation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.14.5/Objects/genobject.c), [type implementation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.14.5/Objects/typeobject.c).

The allocation-free claim excludes both Python and native heap allocation on the admitted E1-B path. Cached allocation would still be allocation; stack-local scalars and fixed arrays are different. Callback-capable deallocation is separately excluded by ER0.

However, these are conditional guarantees: a closed operation list cannot establish the missing owner mapping or select the missing rejected-attempt P0 unwind. H1 therefore does not independently rescue R1/R2.

## F. CS-R1-MAJOR-MX — INDEPENDENT HIGH-RISK FINDING

**Verdict: FAIL — MAJOR target-reachability gap, with dependent ownership/unwind gaps.**

### Finding R3: The P0-busy oracle lacks a frozen admissible route to its target

Appendix R2 §39, row C2 specifies:

> P0 already active; contender acknowledges attempt → busy/closed result.

Appendix R2 §35 and its adversarial matrix similarly require synchronized P0 contention. See [the pin oracle](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-four-target-correction-comprehensive-continuation.md:2162) and [row C2](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-four-target-correction-comprehensive-continuation.md:2266).

But P0 requires the admitted main-thread/GIL context. During protected execution:

- Another Python thread cannot execute the operation.
- A foreign native thread does not satisfy P0’s context requirement.
- Same-thread Python callback/reentry is forbidden.
- Allowed instrumentation cannot silently introduce a new production-equivalent nested execution path.

The row does not select an admissible earlier window, a particular same-thread native path, or an explicitly narrower helper/negative-artifact route. It also does not freeze which gate such a contender reaches before context rejection.

**A rejection at a context gate cannot qualify the occupied-P0 gate.** A specially constructed helper may test the latter, but its narrower claim and equivalence must be specified rather than supplied by the implementer.

This is a matrix architecture defect, not proof that every possible P0-busy test is impossible.

R2 additionally leaves the matrix without a fully defined rejected-attempt P0-release oracle. R1 leaves equivalence of the source-owner representation unresolved.

### Positive MX conclusions

MX is a **qualification evidence/oracle contract**, not a second live semantic authority.

Its specified rules correctly require:

- Same lifetime/attempt/E1/P0 attribution.
- Positive target acknowledgement separate from disposition.
- IR0 completion before E1-B entry.
- E2 after successful A/B completion and cleanup.
- E3 separately witnessed after E2.
- FAIL for attributed forbidden behavior.
- INCONCLUSIVE for absent targets, invalid fixtures or missing attribution.
- No cleanup-based upgrade.
- No fixture-created canonical K/Q/U.
- No observer-owned reference supplying the lifetime proof.

Those rules remain valid. They do not make an underspecified target schedule executable or decisive.

## G. CROSS-TARGET LIFETIME / OWNERSHIP ANALYSIS

The architecture’s actual order differs from the checklist order: **bootstrap, binding and publication precede protected ancestry/canonical validation**.

| Boundary | Owners, roots and derivatives | Protection/observability | Cleanup or unresolved point |
