| Profile/S3 bootstrap | Native profile anchor acquires metadata owners; table derivatives depend on them | Before source responsibility; allocating setup is outside H1 | Partial bootstrap retains bounded ER2 bundle and permanently rejects |
| Private binding construction | Construction lease; native cell; extra Registry reference; private handle/type ownership | Main-thread GIL; no externally callable handle yet | Root ER0, private handle ER1, native disposal on unpublished failure |
| Binding publication | Association owns initialized handle; anchor and handle leases protect cell; cell owns Registry | Exact member store; no partial initialized handle escapes | Published binding is never rolled back to unbound |
| Source ownership before installation | R0 requires S-owned adapter/configuration/F/O | Must precede source effects | **R1: mapping to the no-Python-fields handle is unresolved** |
| Operational entry | Live handle first; P0 and E1 must exist before E1-A | GIL/profile/current binding required | **R2: failed-acquisition unwind not completely mapped** |
| Initial frame acquisition | Scanner obtains new frame reference; executing frame independently owns materialized object | E1/P0/GIL retained | NULL rejects; no empty-ancestry inference |
| Code comparison | New code reference; live executable owner remains | Exact identity comparison | Immediate IR0 code release |
| Back-frame acquisition | Current frame retained while successor is obtained | Same protected ancestry | Successor obtained before predecessor release |
| Clean terminal exhaustion | Final live frame remains independently owned | Clear error indicator required | T1 performs final non-last frame decrement |
| E1-A completion | No scanner-owned frame/code or locally owned inspection exception remains | Same lifetime/attempt/E1/P0/GIL | Direct transition; no Python return/reacquisition |
| E1-B raw/canonical reads | Cell-rooted Registry→map→Association graph; metadata owners; borrowed slots/containers | Exact H1; no allocation/Python/GC collection; writers excluded | Getter ER0 temporaries discharged; **R2 applies on rejection** |
| E2 | Immutable scalar observation published; root and P0 remain | Native evidence readers may observe E2 | No semantic authority from raw addresses |
| E3 | Same-attempt one-shot admission; original finalizer retains P0 | No protection release or ordinary Python resumption | New P0 forbidden |
| Enclosing restoration/resource work | R0 callable responsibilities remain distinct from Registry root | Same admitted native executor | **R1 prevents complete representation-level ownership proof** |
| Successful closure/root release | Sole finalizer; current reverse Registry edge proves non-last root release | Borrowed uses end before root clear/decrement | B4 → anchor lease drop → finalizer P0 release |
| BF | Anchor/root/metadata retained; executor P0 released after use ends | P0 permanently closed; P1 evidence-only remains | No retry, root diagnostics, recovery or COMPLETE |
| Rootless native lifetime | Live handle/P1 leases retain native evidence storage | No root/canonical Python access | Exactly one last-lease destruction claim |

A complete total ordering between unrelated safe setup operations is unnecessary. The missing ordering in R2 is material because it determines ownership on actual rejection exits.

## H. P1-B / T1 / E1-A / E1-B ANALYSIS

Historical evidence remains:

- **P1-B:** bounded frame materialization may allocate under the exact admitted profile without executing Python or running GC collection.
- **R3 — ORDERING NEVER FROZEN.**
- **R3-B — GENUINE ARCHITECTURE CHOICE REMAINED.**

**T1 is a new prospective choice.**

I found affirmative support for its local reference argument:

1. Compare current code.
2. Release returned code immediately.
3. Obtain successor before releasing predecessor.
4. Establish clean terminal NULL.
5. Release final scanner-owned frame under IR0.
6. Establish zero scanner ownership and clear error state.
7. Enter E1-B directly under unchanged E1/P0/GIL.

The pinned materializer installs the frame object in the executing interpreter frame. Frame clearing later removes that ownership; the protected scanner does not advance or unwind the executing frame. Public getters create additional inspection references. Consequently, releasing the scanner’s reference does not itself remove the independent executing-frame owner. [CPython frame ownership](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.14.5/Python/frame.c), [public frame getters](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.14.5/Objects/frameobject.c).

E1-B’s specified Registry graph and metadata roots are independent of scanner temporaries. **I found no direct T1-created dangling-reference window.**

This supports T1’s local ordering; it does not confer package PASS while R1/R2 remain unresolved. It also does not retrospectively change historical P1.

## I. RAW SLOT / ERROR / REFERENCE / CLEANUP ANALYSIS

### Raw-slot validity

The specified validity interval begins only after:

- An independently live handle/cell and root-capable P0 exist.
- S3/profile/type/role and object extent are authenticated.
- The current parent edge owns the containing object.
- GIL and applicable writer exclusion hold.

The load copies `sizeof(PyObject *)` bytes into a local pointer representation. It acquires **no ownership**. NULL rejects; non-NULL must pass its own representation checks before dependent use.

The last permitted use occurs before the relevant owner/protection ends. Successful root release explicitly ends all borrowed descendant uses first. P1 evidence readers receive no permission to dereference historical pointers after B4. See [CP §29](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-four-target-correction-comprehensive-continuation.md:358).

A copied address is not self-validating. T1 does not replace any of these requirements.

### Reference census

| Reference | Establishment | End/classification |
|---|---|---|
| Metadata/type/module/image references | Bootstrap-owned entries | ER2 process-lifetime retention |
| Extra Registry reference | Binding construction | Successful ER0 release; retained on BF |
| Handle’s native cell lease | Private handle construction | Handle deallocation |
| Association’s handle reference | Publication member assignment | Ordinary graph lifetime after permitted root release |
| Construction lease | Private construction | Publication return or unpublished disposal |
| P0 | Live-handle operational acquisition | Success/BF specified; rejected-attempt mapping incomplete |
| P1 | Acquisition before native reader use | Native release; delays storage destruction |
| Scanner frame/code references | Public getters return new references | IR0 traversal/rejection/T1 cleanup |
| Dict/tuple/Unicode/function/raw-slot results | Borrow from current protected owners | No decrement; dependent use ends before owner/protection |
| Generator-code/type-dict temporaries | Owning getters | ER0 non-last decrement |
| Inspection exception | Transfer from error indicator to local owner | Transfer back into empty indicator after rejection cleanup |
| R0 F/O/adapter/source references | Preserved source-owner contract | LR0 or explicit unresolved retention; representation gap R1 |

**IR0/ER0/ER1/ER2 are reference-release/retention classifications, not four interchangeable exception states.**

- **IR0:** scanner reference is provably non-last under live-ancestry ownership.
- **ER0:** canonical temporary/root decrement is provably non-last under its specified independent owner.
- **ER1:** exact private unpublished handle may undergo its bounded native destruction; its heap-type decrement is separately ER0.
- **ER2:** explicit metadata retention, with no per-cell teardown.

### Error contract and conceptual failures

E1-B entry requires a clear active error indicator. The valid path preserves that state. Unexpected helper error stops validation and preserves the resulting indicator through canonical ER0 cleanup; it is not permission to continue validating with an error.

P1 differs: ancestor failure transfers the inspection exception out, cleans IR0, releases E1 and restores the same exception into an empty destination. The empty-destination condition matters because `PyErr_SetRaisedException` decrements the old destination exception. [CPython error implementation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.14.5/Python/errors.c).

| Failure location | Specified result | Remaining issue |
|---|---|---|
| Wrong runtime/profile/live handle | Reject before unsafe dependent access | No arbitrary pointer recovery |
| Existing error before E1 | Preserve; no scan/reservation | If P0 was acquired earlier, R2 requires its mapped unwind |
| Allocation before root acquisition | Dispose private native allocation | No publication/source effect |
| Failure after root acquisition | End borrows; root ER0; private handle ER1; native disposal | R1 affects eventual full source-owner representation |
| Failure after publication | Retain B2; never pretend unbound | Binding alone does not authorize installation |
| Initial frame NULL | Ancestry unavailable; reject even if error was cleared | R2 |
| Ancestor materialization failure | Preserve exception by transfer; IR0 cleanup; no truncated success | R2 |
| Canonical match/depth rejection | IR0 cleanup; no E1-B/E2/E3 | R2 |
| Canonical type/shape/value rejection | Stop before dependent invalid access; canonical ER0 cleanup | R2 |
| Unexpected H1 helper error | Preserve error; no formatting/clearing/replacement; no E2/E3 | R2 |
| Admitted restoration/release-proof failure | BF; root retained; no clean completion | R1 representation; no recovery inferred |
| Corruption/uncertain native execution | SC; only observed evidence survives | No guaranteed cleanup |

No safe cleanup is inferred for arbitrary invalid pointers. No double exception restoration is admitted. The architecture’s exception-transfer sequence is useful, but it does not itself release an operational pin.

## K. WRITER / SIGNAL / GIL / P0 / E1 ANALYSIS

The protections have different jobs:

| Mechanism | What it establishes | What it does not establish |
|---|---|---|
| Registry/metadata ownership | Object and derivative lifetime | Writer exclusion |
| Live handle/native leases | Cell lifetime before/during pin operations | Canonical semantic validity |
| P0 | Root-capable operational entitlement | GIL ownership or native configuration exclusion by itself |
| GIL plus no Python execution | Stable Python ancestry/containers during protected work | Exclusion of unrelated native writers |
| E1 | Native configuration-writer exclusion and attempt ordering | Object ownership |
| P1 | Native evidence-storage lifetime | Root access or semantic admission |

Across successful E1-A→T1→E1-B→E2→E3, the text explicitly preserves these protections. T1 has no callback, GIL-drop or writer-reopening step.

Native signal recording remains possible; Python handler execution, signal checks and pending-call processing are forbidden inside the protected operation. Materialization may schedule future GC; scheduling is not collection. [CPython GC scheduling path](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.14.5/Python/gc.c).

For each main boundary, both questions matter:

- **Could another actor mutate it?** The successful path supplies GIL/E1/profile exclusion.
- **Could it die locally anyway?** IR0/ER0 and independent roots supply the separate non-last/lifetime proof.

R1 leaves part of the second answer incomplete. R2 leaves the protection-ending sequence incomplete on rejection. R3 concerns evidence that the intended contender actually reaches the claimed gate.

## L. ARTIFACT EQUIVALENCE

CP §32 requires equality of safety-relevant behavior, including:

- Ownership and GC representation.
- Binding setter and initialization.
- Pin/control-word/destruction behavior.
- Raw-load body and compiler expansion.
- Helper/error/reference contracts.
- T1 and E2/E3 order.
- Memory ordering and source effects.

Only fixed scalar evidence, native synchronization and parent markers are allowed differences. See [CP §32](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-four-target-correction-comprehensive-continuation.md:400).

This is a sound architectural equivalence requirement. It correctly rejects “same final value” as sufficient.

No future production/instrumented binary equivalence was verified: those artifacts do not exist yet. Their absence is a legitimate deferred qualification item.

Current obstacles are architectural:

- R1 leaves the representation whose equivalence must be checked unresolved.
- R2 leaves a safety-relevant cleanup path unspecified.
- R3 does not establish that its target-reaching instrumentation belongs to the permitted equivalence class.

A stronger observer root, retained frame, hidden pin or artificial reentry path cannot silently cure these gaps.

## M. E2 / E3 / CRASH / SC ANALYSIS

**E2** is immutable same-attempt observation after successful ancestry, complete IR0 cleanup, canonical validation and required canonical temporary cleanup. It is not source restoration, root release or invocation COMPLETE.

**E3** is separate one-shot finalization admission by the same owner/attempt under continuous protection. It forbids new P0 entry but retains the original finalizer’s P0.

Neither E2 nor E3 permits ordinary Python execution or automatically releases E1/GIL/root ownership. Successful root release occurs later, after restoration terminal publication, R0 discharge and resource terminal publication. See [CP §33](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-four-target-correction-comprehensive-continuation.md:408).

MX is not an additional production semantic gate that must execute a Python oracle before E2. Its evidence must faithfully correspond to the actual validation and publication.

Crash/SC treatment is appropriately bounded:

| Crash boundary | Permitted conclusion |
|---|---|
| Before/during T1 | Only already observed scan/release events |
| After IR0, before E1-B | No inferred canonical success/E2/E3 |
| During E1-B | No inferred cleanup or validation publication |
| During private binding | No assumed disposal or publication |
| After publication | A witnessed binding is not restoration/admission |
| After E2, before E3 | E2 may exist; admission must not be inferred |
| During closure/root release | Only completed observed transitions; no inferred B4/resource completion |
| After E3 | Existing admitted-failure/SC limits; no retry or recovery authority |

R2 concerns **intact-runtime rejection**, so SC cannot excuse it. Likewise, R1 cannot be deferred by declaring ordinary source ownership a crash concern.

## B. R1 — SELECTED CORRECTED ARCHITECTURE

**Preserved requirements.** R0 assigns source responsibility to a capability-retained SourceAuthority, including adapter/configuration ownership, explicit callable slots and same-invocation discharge. B2.1 separately requires a non-GC handle without Python-reference fields beyond its type reference. The external Registry root must survive caller loss. Sources: original R0; [CP §13](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-four-target-correction-comprehensive-continuation.md:154); [CP §§24–26](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-four-target-correction-comprehensive-continuation.md:288).

Before correction, the two specified graphs were:

```text
Capability → SourceAuthority S → adapter/configuration/F/O/R0 cell

Native anchor → CS-R1 cell → Registry → Association → published handle
                                      published handle → cell lease
```

The relationship between **S** and the published handle was missing. Equating them contradicted the handle’s reference-field restriction; merely naming them differently supplied no lifetime edge.

**NEW PROSPECTIVE CORRECTION — select distinct owner and handle.**

Use these unambiguous identities:

- **S — SourceOwner:** the preserved R0 SourceAuthority.
- **H — SourceHandle:** the published B2.1 non-GC handle.
- **D — CS-R1 cell:** the existing separately anchored native Registry-root/control/evidence cell.
- **C_R0 — R0 reference/result storage:** fixed native storage embedded in S, distinct from D.
- **A_src — SourceLifetimeAdapter:** acts for S. It is not the existing retaining adapter used by canonical Q observations.
- **Assoc:** the canonical capability/Association record.
- **Anchor:** the existing external native profile anchor.

The selected graph is:

```text
Anchor ──native lease──> D ──owning Registry reference──> Registry
                                                        │
                                                        v
                                                      Assoc
                                                    /       \
                                         owns S <──         ──> owns H
                                            │                     │
                                            ├── owns H             └──native lease──> D
                                            ├── owns A_src
                                            ├── owns canonical ledger reference
                                            ├── owns source configuration
                                            ├── owns per-source F/O slots
                                            └── contains C_R0 and its owning slots
```

**S is an exact private GC-aware native heap container. H remains the exact non-GC native heap type already selected.** Neither has caller-controlled construction, subclassing, an instance dictionary, weakrefs or a Python finalizer.

S has an explicit, traversed ownership edge to H. H has **no owning or dereferenceable backlink to S**. D records S’s bound identity for comparison, not as another Python owner.

The concrete capability-retention edge is the prospective `Assoc._source_owner` object-reference member. The existing prospective `Assoc._source_lifetime` member continues to publish **H**.

### Establishment and publication

**NEW PROSPECTIVE CORRECTION — freeze this establishment order.**

1. Canonical capability setup creates S once and stores it in `Assoc._source_owner`, before source responsibility or source mutation.
2. S initially has no handle, adapter, installation-lifetime callable references or temporary R0 references. Its fixed configuration/result storage is initialized.
3. The binding writer obtains S through the canonical Association; it does not accept a caller-supplied S.
4. It constructs D and H using the preserved B2.2 construction/anchor/root rules.
5. Before publication, it initializes D’s immutable S/lifetime identity and establishes `S.handle → H`.
6. Publication still linearizes at the initialized-H assignment to `Assoc._source_lifetime`.
7. Adapter establishment, callable retention and source installation remain separately governed steps after successful binding and their existing eligibility checks.

No observer can treat the preparatory `S.handle` store as publication. The native construction interval remains callback-free and non-yielding.

If construction fails before H publication:

- Source effects and R0 callable ownership have not begun.
- Preserved candidate-root ER0 disposal occurs first.
- The unpublished anchor lease is removed.
- `S.handle` is cleared while the constructor still independently owns H.
- The constructor releases H under the existing ER1 handle rule, then drops construction ownership.
- S remains the same capability-owned, unbound source owner.

The failure does not replace S or publish a partial source binding.

### Binding identity

**NEW PROSPECTIVE CORRECTION — freeze one-to-one identity.**

For the one admitted source lifetime:

```text
Assoc._source_owner is S
Assoc._source_lifetime is H
S.handle is H
H.cell is D
D.bound_source_identity denotes S for comparison only
```

There is one published H for S and one S for H. No alias-handle factory, shared handle across owners, retrieval-through-bind or rebinding is admitted.

Before P0, the operation may inspect H’s native lease and D’s immutable publication/control identity. This is **not** a current canonical graph proof. After P0/E1 and ancestry completion, E1-B traverses the rooted current graph and checks the identity relationship above using S3-authenticated members and existing H1 operations.

## C. R1 — OWNERSHIP / LIFETIME TABLE

**NEW PROSPECTIVE CORRECTION — the following ownership accounting is normative.**

| Entity or edge | Ownership and establishment | Lifetime/discharge |
|---|---|---|
| `Assoc → S` | Strong Python-reference member; established once by canonical capability setup | Retained through active responsibility, rejection and BF. Ordinary graph disposal only after lawful release of external responsibility rooting, or before responsibility ever begins. |
| `Assoc → H` | Strong member established by the existing publication store | Immutable during the published lifetime; ordinary graph cleanup after B4 may release it. |
| `S → H` | Strong, GC-traversed member established privately before H publication | Rolled back only on unpublished failure; otherwise retained with S until ordinary post-responsibility cleanup. |
| `H → D` | Existing single native handle lease | Ends in H’s bounded native destructor. Two Python references to H do not create two native handle leases. |
| `Anchor → D` | Existing native anchor lease | Retained through active responsibility/BF; dropped at the existing successful root-release point. |
| `D → Registry` | Existing extra owning Registry reference | Successful ER0 release only; retained on BF. |
| `S → A_src` | Explicit strong object-reference slot | Adapter established only for canonical S; retained with the source owner and later as ordinary structural/evidence state. |
| `S → ledger` | Strong reference to the existing canonical ledger | Preserves the inherited source/cut ownership relationship; it is not a copied ledger or second cut authority. |
| Source configuration | Fixed native record embedded in S | Holds source selectors, scalar configuration/progress and references to named callable-slot roles. It contains no hidden owning callable cache. |
| S’s installed-callable slots | Two explicit owning slots, one per registration, containing the selected shared F when populated | Cleared by the same admitted finalizer under R0/LR0 after verified handoff. |
| S’s original-callable slots | Two explicit owning slots containing the same authenticated O when populated | Cleared under R0/LR0 when restoration/evidence purposes end. |
| C_R0 | Fixed embedded native reference/result record; no separately escaping allocation or lease | Lifetime is S’s lifetime. All its Python-reference slots belong to S’s GC accounting. |
| C_R0 callable slots | Per-registration original reservation, original verification guard, expected-installed guard and displaced-registration slots | Each populated slot has exactly one recorded ownership purpose. Transfer/release/retention follows R0; unused slots remain empty. |
| R0 unresolved references | Remain in the same named S/C_R0 slots | No deferred list, replacement owner or hidden retention cache. |
| D’s S/Association/work identities | Non-owning comparison data | Never dereferenced as owner pointers; after B4 they are historical scalar identities only. |
| Metadata ownership | Existing Anchor ER2 bundle | Process-lifetime metadata ownership; no per-source owner added to that bundle. |

A_src does not cache owning F/O references or a persistent raw S pointer. An adapter operation obtains S through the canonical source association and borrows its slots only within the corresponding admitted owner lifetime.

S’s GC traversal covers **every owning Python field**, including H, A_src, ledger, callable arrays, populated C_R0 slots and applicable heap-type ownership. Embedding C_R0 does not hide its references from GC.

This follows the general container requirement for traversal and clearing; the consulted [Python 3.14 GC documentation](https://docs.python.org/3.14/c-api/gcsupport.html) supports that general rule. It is currently rendered for 3.14.7 and is **not substituted for the required exact 3.14.5 implementation audit**.

## D. R1 — SUCCESS / BF / DESTRUCTION / LR0 INTERACTION

**Preserved sequence.** Restoration terminal publication precedes R0 discharge, which precedes resource terminal publication. Registry-root release is later and separate. [CP §§13 and 27](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-four-target-correction-comprehensive-continuation.md:326).

**NEW PROSPECTIVE CORRECTION — apply that sequence to S explicitly.**

The same admitted synchronous native finalizer acts for S throughout:

1. Complete the separately governed restoration/coverage transaction.
2. Publish its verified restoration disposition.
3. Transfer or release C_R0’s temporary callable ownership under the exact R0 classifications.
4. Clear S’s eligible installation/original slots under LR0.
5. Establish that no successful-path source-instance callable obligation remains.
6. Publish the separate resource-discharge result.
7. Continue the preserved Closing/root-release/B4/anchor-drop/finalizer-P0 sequence.

Each release first removes ownership from its named slot, then decrements the saved reference exactly once under its independent-owner proof. Transferred reservation ownership is removed from C_R0 and becomes registration ownership; it is not also decremented.

The executable module continues owning F. Authenticated signal-module ownership and restored registration ownership support the applicable O decrements. Fixture aliases do not count.

S, A_src, the ledger and H are **not destroyed by LR0 discharge**. R0 explicitly preserves S after callable discharge. Their continued structural/evidence lifetime is not an unresolved installation-lifetime callable obligation.

### Caller loss

While responsibility remains, the surviving path is:

```text
Anchor → D → Registry → Assoc → S
```

It does not depend on the initiating frame, returned H or an observer. S’s H reference also prevents H’s destruction while S still needs D.

### BF

BF retains the existing Anchor/D/Registry graph and therefore S and every still-outstanding S/C_R0 reference.

Retention is phase-sensitive:

- Partial/uncertain restoration retains the references still required by that unresolved state.
- If LR0 already completed and a later root-release proof fails, BF retains S and its results/root graph; it does **not** recreate discharged callable references.
- No BF path clears an outstanding source obligation to obtain a clean result.
- New P0 remains permanently closed, with existing P1 evidence access only.

### Cycles and destruction

The mixed cycle through H’s native lease and D’s Registry root is intentional while responsibility remains. The existing successful root clear breaks its Python-retaining edge; the anchor lease then drops at its existing point.

After B4:

- H cannot reach S through an owning Python edge.
- D contains no Registry root and grants no source-owner dereference right.
- An externally retained H can preserve rootless native evidence without preserving S.
- Registry/Association/ledger/S cycles are ordinary GC-visible cycles.
- Once their ordinary owners disappear, they may be collected without requiring process exit.

S’s normal clearing/deallocation releases structural references only after source-instance obligations have already been discharged, or before they began. It performs no restoration, LR0 substitute, terminal publication or recovery. A reachable active/BF S cannot lawfully become a GC cleanup target because the external Registry root retains it.

C_R0 cannot outlive S independently and cannot be passed to P1 readers. D remains separately governed by its existing last-lease destruction conditions.

## E. R1 — S3 / REGISTRY ROOT / ARTIFACT EQUIVALENCE

**NEW PROSPECTIVE CORRECTION — extend the expected representation schema, not the historical PASS.**

The eventual independently authenticated expected schema must include:

- `Assoc._source_owner`, alongside the already prospective `_source_lifetime`.
- S’s exact type, base, GC flags, member descriptors and complete layout.
- S’s H, adapter, ledger and callable-reference members.
- C_R0’s complete embedded owning-slot inventory and native scalar layout.
- D’s immutable bound-source comparison identity.
- The exact establishment, publication, unpublished rollback, traversal, clearing and destruction implementations.
- The unchanged H representation, including absence of Python-reference fields beyond its type reference.

All layout-affecting fields are covered even when canonical validation does not read them. No offsets, loaded-type identities or binary hashes are invented here.

Current source has no implemented SourceOwner or SourceHandle. The [current Association declaration](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/src/orev3/execution/cancellation_capability.py:386) and [CP §35](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-four-target-correction-comprehensive-continuation.md:442) establish that these remain prospective.

S3’s methodology stays unchanged. Historical S3 PASS does not authenticate the new schema. Future schema/image authentication is a required implementation/qualification gate.

Canonical reads use the existing exact-type, pointer-comparison and S3/raw-member machinery. Owner establishment and binding writes remain outside E1-B; no GC, allocation or new setter is added to H1.

Production/instrumented equivalence must cover the graph above, including GC visibility and C_R0 accounting. An instrumented direct H→S owner, extra module-held S, hidden native root or observer-held adapter would defeat lifetime transfer.

## F. R2 — SELECTED EXACTLY-ONCE P0 OWNERSHIP RULE

**Preserved facts.** P0 occupancy, E1 reservation and B/E phase are distinct. Only one P0 exists; successful acquisition requires a live H and eligible native state. Success/BF have their own specified terminal release points. [CP §§26–27](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-four-target-correction-comprehensive-continuation.md:316).

**NEW PROSPECTIVE CORRECTION — each invocation owns an explicit obligation.**

An invocation begins with:

```text
P0_NOT_OWNED
E1_NOT_OWNED
```

Only the successful P0-acquisition CAS changes its local obligation to:

```text
P0_OWNED_BY_THIS_INVOCATION
```

The transition and local recording occur in the same uninterrupted native execution. No helper infers ownership from B2, E1, E2 or a nonzero occupancy bit.

For every intact-runtime precommit exit:

- Never acquired P0 → never release P0.
- Acquired P0 → relinquish it exactly once before Python resumes.
- E3 lawfully committed → retain the same P0 into the existing finalizer contract.

There is no new transfer to a cleanup owner. E3 changes the invocation’s role; it does not transfer P0 to another executor.

Only the invocation’s terminal unwind releases rejected-attempt P0. Scanner and canonical cleanup helpers cannot release P0 or E1.

P0 release uses the existing manifest-bound native atomic transition, preserving unrelated phase, lease and P1 fields. CAS interference from legitimate P1 activity is retried; a failed CAS is not counted as release. After the successful transition, the invocation’s local obligation becomes `P0_NOT_OWNED`.

An ownership inconsistency is not repaired by clearing someone else’s occupancy. Crash/corruption remains SC, not a claimed successful unwind.

## G. R2 — REJECTION-CLASS MATRIX

**NEW PROSPECTIVE CORRECTION — this matrix covers admitted intact-runtime rejection exits.**

Notation:

- **I:** scanner-owned IR0 slots.
- **C:** canonical owning ER0 temporaries.
- **X:** locally owned transferred inspection exception.
- Progress is **E1-A / T1 / E1-B / E2 / E3**.
- “Possible” means determined by the invocation’s actual owning slots, never guessed from phase.

| Class | Ownership at rejection | Progress | Required cleanup and P0 disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| **R2-A: P0 acquisition rejects** | P0 no; E1 no; I/C/X none | No / No / No / No / No | Native reason; no root use; no P0 or E1 release. Preserve any entry error. |
| **R2-B: E1 acquisition rejects after P0** | P0 yes; E1 no; I/C/X none | No / No / No / No / No | End native acquisition inspection; release this P0 once. No E1 release. |
| **R2-C: pre-scan precondition/invalidation rejection** | P0/E1 yes; I/C/X none | No / No / No / No / No | End checks; release E1, then P0 once. |
| **R2-D: initial frame unavailable** | P0/E1 yes; no returned frame; C none; X only if an error is actually present | No / No / No / No / No | Reject unavailable ancestry. Preserve an actual inspection error using the transfer order below; otherwise retain clear indicator. Release E1 then P0. |
| **R2-E: ancestry identity/depth rejection** | P0/E1 yes; I possible; C/X none on ordinary rejection | Incomplete / No / No / No / No | Stop scan; discharge all I under IR0; release E1 then P0. |
| **R2-F: frame/getter/materialization error** | P0/E1 yes; I possible; C none; X acquired from indicator | Incomplete / No / No / No / No | Save X; complete IR0 cleanup; release E1; release P0; restore X. |
| **R2-G: clean ancestry, transition rejection** | P0/E1 yes; I/C/X none | Yes / Yes / No / No / No | No scanner cleanup remains. End transition checks; release E1 then P0. |
| **R2-H: ordinary canonical rejection** | P0/E1 yes; I/X none; C possible | Yes / Yes / Entered / No / No | End dependent reads; ER0 cleanup; clear indicator remains clear; release E1 then P0. |
| **R2-I: unexpected canonical helper error** | P0/E1 yes; I/X none; C possible; error remains in indicator | Yes / Yes / Entered / No / No | Preserve active error; end reads; ER0 cleanup; release E1 then P0 without error transfer/clearing. |
| **R2-J: late invalidation before E2** | P0/E1 yes; I/C/X reflect actual subregion | E2 not published; E3 no | Apply the corresponding scanner/canonical cleanup row, then release E1/P0. No E2/E3 inferred. |
| **R2-K1: rejection after valid E2, before E3** | P0/E1 yes; I/C/X none | Yes / Yes / Complete / Yes / No | Retain E2 as historical same-attempt observation; reject admission; release E1 then P0. |
| **R2-K2: unexpected inspection result without error** | P0/E1 yes; I possible; C/X none | Scanner incomplete | Fixed rejection reason; IR0 cleanup; release E1 then P0. No truncated-scan acceptance. |
| **R2-K3: finite counter/evidence capacity unavailable** | Determined at the first applicable native check | No E3 | Before acquisition: R2-A. If already owned: clean applicable slots and release E1/P0. Never wrap or manufacture another evidence slot. |
| **R2-K4: source-owner/binding/current-edge mismatch in E1-B** | P0/E1 yes; I/X none; C possible | Yes / Yes / Entered / No / No | Stop before dependent invalid access; canonical ER0 cleanup; release E1 then P0. |

Earlier profile/context/entry-error rejection is R2-A with no P0 acquisition attempt when the earlier gate fails.

Binding-construction failures are **not** operational P0-owned paths: they retain their construction-lease/root/ER1 disposal rules. Successful E3, BF and SC are likewise not folded into this table.

For any intact rejection discovered at an existing operation, the actual ownership vector selects one row. There is no catch-all return that leaves the vector unexamined.

## H. R2 — UNWIND PARTIAL ORDER AND ERROR CONTRACT

**NEW PROSPECTIVE CORRECTION — select this common order.**

For a P0-owned rejection:

```text
stop new dependent work
    ↓
save inspection exception, only when applicable
    ↓
finish IR0 cleanup
    ↓
finish ER0 canonical cleanup
    ↓
finish other admitted non-callback local cleanup and borrowed uses
    ↓
release owned E1
    ↓
release owned P0 exactly once
    ↓
restore transferred inspection exception, only when applicable
    ↓
return/resume Python
```

IR0 and canonical ER0 owning temporaries do not coexist on a valid T1 path. Listing both does not create permission to carry scanner ownership into E1-B.

### End-of-use boundary

The final ordinary dependent operations are scanner cleanup, canonical cleanup and bounded rejection/evidence preparation. Any borrowed Registry, S, adapter, frame, code, container or raw-slot use ends before E1 release.

The remaining protected operations are terminal control operations:

1. Release the invocation’s E1 reservation.
2. Relinquish the invocation’s P0.

**P0 release is the last operation requiring that invocation’s P0.** Afterward, the invocation uses only its local scalar result and, if present, its independently owned X. It performs no further root traversal, source-owner access or cell-evidence dereference under the released P0.

Qualification exit markers after release use separate preallocated scalar observation storage. They cannot justify a post-release access to D. P1 observers retain their separately acquired evidence-only rights.

### E1 acquisition failure

**NEW PROSPECTIVE CORRECTION — R2-B has this exact behavior.**

Before E1 acquisition, the operation has performed only admitted entry checks, live-H/native-publication checks and P0 acquisition. It has performed **no Registry-root traversal, scanner acquisition, S-slot borrow or canonical owning getter**.

E1 acquisition is a native reservation transition:

- Success owns E1.
- Failure owns no E1 and does not alter the winner’s reservation.
- It creates no Python-reference cleanup obligation.
- It does not alter the Python error indicator.

On failure, the invocation records the native rejection disposition and immediately releases its own P0. It does not run an E1-release operation.

### Inspection exception

The inherited transfer protocol is preserved; [CP Appendix R1’s error contract](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-four-target-correction-comprehensive-continuation.md:1408) already places IR0 cleanup before reservation release and restoration afterward.

**NEW PROSPECTIVE CORRECTION — insert P0 release before restoration.**

1. `PyErr_GetRaisedException` transfers the actual inspection exception into invocation-local X, leaving the active indicator empty.
2. Clean every scanner owning slot under IR0 while E1/P0/GIL remain held.
3. Finish remaining admitted local uses.
4. Release E1.
5. Release P0.
6. `PyErr_SetRaisedException` transfers X into the still-empty indicator.
7. Clear local X ownership by transfer; return with the same exception.

No step formats, normalizes, substitutes or destructively releases X. Native reservation/P0 operations perform scalar/atomic work only, with no Python API, allocation, callback or deallocation. A live H lease remains throughout; rejected-attempt P0 release cannot be the cell’s last lifetime protection.

Consequently those releases cannot disturb the error indicator. Restoration after both releases preserves the inherited reservation ordering without requiring P0 for an exception owned locally.

Initial-frame NULL with a cleared indicator remains “ancestry unavailable”; no MemoryError is reconstructed.

### Canonical helper error

The error stays in the active indicator. There is no X transfer on this path.

ER0 decrements remain proven non-last. E1/P0 releases are error-neutral native operations. The invocation returns with the same unexpected error. Ordinary semantic rejection instead preserves a clear indicator.

Python diagnostics, if the surrounding interface constructs them, occur only after both obligations are released and cannot replace an error that must be preserved.

### E2/E3

An already-published E2 is not erased by R2-K1. The rejection ends that attempt’s eligibility to commit E3; E2 cannot be presented by a later invocation as admission authority.

The existing immutable evidence-storage and finite-counter limits remain binding. Unwind does not reset, overwrite or allocate replacement E2 storage. If another publication cannot fit those existing bounds, a later attempt rejects for capacity rather than reusing the old observation.

Uncertain publication or uncertain E3 execution is SC, not an invented intact-runtime rejection.

## I. R2 — T1 / E1-A / E1-B / H1 INTERACTION

**Preserved architecture.** [CP §§18–21](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-four-target-correction-comprehensive-continuation.md:216) and [Appendix R5](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-four-target-correction-comprehensive-continuation.md:3390) supply P1-B, T1 and the split.

**NEW PROSPECTIVE CORRECTION — impose these rejection invariants.**

- E1-A owns scanner cleanup. Its successful endpoint has no scanner frame/code ownership or local inspection exception.
- T1 does not release E1 or P0.
- A successful transition into E1-B preserves the same lifetime, attempt, E1, P0, GIL and excluded-writer protection.
- E1-B rejection cannot resurrect a frame/code reference.
- E1-B owns only its permitted canonical ER0 temporaries and borrowed values.
- Rejection cleanup is terminal control flow, not an expanded H1 success whitelist.
- No S/H ownership decrement or source-callable LR0 operation is added to canonical rejection.
- E2 requires both successful regions and their cleanup. E3 remains a separate same-attempt admission under its inherited enclosing prerequisites.

The successful finalizer continues holding P0 through the frozen B4/anchor-drop sequence. BF releases the executor’s P0 only after required uses end and new P0 is permanently closed.

**All E1→E2 is not allocation-free.** E1-A retains bounded P1-B materialization allocation; only the admitted E1-B path carries the allocation-free claim.

## J. R3-MX — SELECTED ADMISSIBLE QUALIFICATION ARCHITECTURE

**Preserved requirements.** Existing matrix row C2 and the pin oracle require occupied-state rejection, while the review identifies missing production reachability. [CP Appendix R2 §35](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-four-target-correction-comprehensive-continuation.md:2162); [row C2](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-four-target-correction-comprehensive-continuation.md:2266).

**NEW PROSPECTIVE CORRECTION — replace the ambiguous contention row with two distinct obligations:**

1. **Production path/exclusion proof:** establish from the admitted executable topology and entry-gate implementation that ordinary actors cannot produce the alleged protected-interval contender.
2. **Direct helper-scoped occupied-state qualification:** execute the exact production P0 acquisition primitive against standalone native control-word storage containing the occupied-state precondition.

No dynamic demonstration of impossible production concurrency is required or claimed.

### Production safety property

The P0 primitive must never grant another root-capable occupancy when the word already records one. Rejection must not clear, steal or transfer the incumbent occupancy.

This remains a required local property even when closed-profile actor exclusion prevents ordinary simultaneous entry.

### Frozen production gate order

**NEW PROSPECTIVE CORRECTION — the operational path is:**

1. Safe runtime/profile and native execution-context admission.
2. Bound main-thread/GIL eligibility before any context-dependent Python-object operation.
3. Existing-error rejection and independently live exact H.
4. H lease/D immutable publication/profile/lifetime checks.
5. Native phase eligibility and P0 acquisition decision.
6. E1 acquisition.
7. Pre-scan native preconditions.
8. E1-A.
9. E1-B, including current canonical binding/owner checks.
10. E2, then separately eligible E3.

The pre-P0 native binding checks do not pretend to traverse the current Registry graph. Current graph authority is established only within the protected canonical region.

An inadmissible foreign native context stops before context-dependent object access and before P0. Another Python thread cannot execute the protected operation while the owner retains the admitted GIL; its native GIL-acquisition precursor is not a P0 attempt. Same-thread Python reentry and unreviewed native nested entry remain excluded by the admitted call graph.

### Selected helper route

**NEW PROSPECTIVE CORRECTION — define an internal `P0_TRY_ACQUIRE` primitive.**

This is the production implementation’s native scalar/atomic P0 acquisition operation, not a new public API or H1 canonical helper.

Its local input is a valid, aligned, live native control word with the manifest-bound representation. It performs no Python-object or root access. Production obtains that storage through H’s existing lease and preceding gates.

The qualification driver:

- Runs in the admitted main-thread/GIL context.
- Owns standalone native control-word storage.
- Initializes the relevant eligible-B2, occupied-P0 bit pattern.
- Invokes the **same production primitive** directly.
- Does not create a Registry, S, H, canonical cell or semantic K/Q/U state.
- Does not acquire an actual production P0 or start E1.
- Does not run inside another production protected invocation.

The fixture’s occupied bit is explicitly synthetic helper input. It is not evidence that production concurrency occurred or that an actual source owner survived.

## K. R3-MX — TARGET ACKNOWLEDGEMENT / DISPOSITION / EVIDENCE CLASS

**NEW PROSPECTIVE CORRECTION — freeze the occupied branch.**

Within `P0_TRY_ACQUIRE`:

1. Load the control word with its admitted atomic ordering.
2. Reject an ineligible phase at a distinct phase gate.
3. For eligible B2 with P0 occupied, execute the occupied-P0 rejection branch.
4. Return a unique native gate/result pair:

```text
gate = P0_OCCUPIED
disposition = REJECT_NO_ACQUISITION
```

5. Perform no successful-acquisition CAS, occupancy decrement, E1 operation or root access on this branch.

The gate tag has exactly one producing branch in the audited primitive. The qualification driver cannot inject or substitute it.

The helper evidence sequence is:

```text
FIXTURE_CONTROL_WORD_READY
→ EXACT_PRIMITIVE_CALL
→ RETURNED_GATE_P0_OCCUPIED
→ REJECT_NO_ACQUISITION
→ CONTROL_WORD_UNCHANGED
→ NO_RELEASE_OBLIGATION
```

The driver records the input and resulting word plus returned gate/disposition in preallocated scalar storage. Artifact inspection must establish that the busy branch performs no write; equality of endpoint words alone is insufficient to exclude an intervening illicit mutation.

| Target | Evidence class | Permitted conclusion |
|---|---|---|
| Occupied branch acknowledged; rejection; no mutation/acquisition/release | Helper-scoped direct primitive qualification | Exact occupied-state decision rejects without changing the incumbent word. |
| Earlier production context gate acknowledged | Negative/interface evidence for that gate | That actor/path was excluded there. No occupied-P0 conclusion. |
| Native Python-thread precursor attempts GIL acquisition during protection | Existing exclusion evidence | No Python entry before GIL availability; not a P0 contender. |
| Source/artifact closure excludes same-thread callback or nested native entry | Static admitted-profile proof | No such production route exists within the audited topology. |
| Missing occupied tag, wrong phase, fixture interference or missing attribution | INCONCLUSIVE for occupied-P0 | No branch qualification. |
| Occupied branch grants acquisition, mutates occupancy or releases it | FAIL | Violates the occupied-state contract. |

The failed helper call owns no P0 obligation. The synthetic incumbent bit is never passed to the R2 release path. Disposing of standalone fixture storage is fixture teardown, not `PIN_RELEASE` or a native-cell destruction qualification.

## L. R3-MX — ARTIFACT EQUIVALENCE AND CLAIM CEILING

**NEW PROSPECTIVE CORRECTION — require exact primitive transfer.**

The production and helper artifacts must contain the same compiled `P0_TRY_ACQUIRE` implementation, with identical:

- Control-word representation and masks.
- Phase-before-occupied gate order.
- Atomic loads/CAS and memory ordering.
- Gate/result encoding.
- Occupied-branch no-write behavior.
- Acquisition success behavior relevant to distinguishing success from rejection.

The driver invokes that implementation directly. No mock primitive, replaced atomic, patched return, alternate “test busy” branch or fixture-only production entry point is permitted.

Production integration must separately show that:

- Its entry gates dominate the primitive call.
- A rejection result cannot fall into acquisition-success handling.
- R2-A does not release P0.
- E1 and root access are unreachable after acquisition rejection.

Allowed helper differences are standalone scalar storage, native driver call scaffolding and preallocated scalar evidence. These differences supply input-storage lifetime **only for the helper claim**. They supply no production source/root lifetime proof.

The resulting claim is deliberately limited:

> The exact production acquisition primitive, when presented with its valid local occupied-state input, rejects without modifying or acquiring that occupancy.

It does not qualify production contention scheduling, SourceOwner lifetime, E1 exclusion, canonical validation, K/Q/U, E2/E3 or finalization.

## M. CROSS-CORRECTION CONSISTENCY

**NEW PROSPECTIVE CORRECTION — require these integrated invariants.**

| Interaction | Required result |
|---|---|
| **R1 × R2** | S owns source responsibility; the executing invocation owns P0/E1. Rejection does not discharge S’s R0 slots or destroy S/H. |
| **R1 × R3-MX** | The occupied helper uses no S/H/root graph. It cannot supply a stronger source owner than production or count as a lifetime test. |
| **R2 × R3-MX** | Occupied rejection is R2-A: no acquisition, no release. It cannot release the incumbent’s occupancy. |
| **R1 × BF** | The preserved root graph retains S and every outstanding obligation. Discharged slots are not recreated. |
| **R2 × E2** | Rejected attempts relinquish P0 even after E2; retained observation does not preserve admission entitlement. |
| **R1 × equivalence** | Owner/handle fields, GC traversal, publication and root paths must match. |
| **R2 × equivalence** | Ownership recording, cleanup, E1/P0 release and error restoration paths must match. |
| **R3-MX × equivalence** | Exact primitive behavior transfers; standalone fixture scheduling/lifetime does not. |

No correction creates another claimant, cleanup executor, semantic mirror or source owner.

## N. ANTI-REGRESSION CHECK

The following are architectural self-checks, not executed qualification.

| Adversarial question | Candidate response |

# C. R1 — INDEPENDENT HIGH-RISK FINDING

**Verdict: PASS — the owner/handle representation defect is closed.**

The acceptance is supported by explicit ownership, publication, rollback and responsibility boundaries in CAND §§B–E, read with R0 and CP §§13, 24–27.

## 1. Scope and identity

The S/H split is authorized.

The broader architecture assigns source responsibility to a capability-retained SourceAuthority and distinguishes architectural roles from experimental class names. R0 expressly treats concrete representation as a constrained prospective choice. The correction authorization explicitly permits distinct owner and published-handle entities.

The former B2.1 “SourceAuthority handle” terminology did not independently establish that its non-GC wrapper must directly contain every R0 reference. That unresolved relationship was precisely PR’s R1 finding.

The correction now assigns:

- **S:** the preserved semantic SourceAuthority.
- **H:** the published non-GC representation handle.
- **D:** the separately anchored native cell.
- **C_R0:** S’s embedded R0 storage.

Adding prospective `Assoc._source_owner` does change the eventual schema. It does not change the frozen current implementation or inherit its layout authentication. CAND §E explicitly requires new schema/image authentication. I found no required scope escalation.

## 2. Ownership reconstruction

| Edge/entity | Ownership and visibility | Establishment and termination |
|---|---|---|
| Anchor → D | Native lease outside Python GC | Construction; removed after successful root release, or unpublished rollback |
| D → Registry | One owning Python reference held by the separately anchored native cell | Construction; explicit ER0 root release; retained on BF |
| Registry → Assoc | Existing canonical strong map ownership, GC-visible | Canonical reservation; ordinary graph lifetime |
| Assoc → S | Strong, GC-visible member | Canonical setup once; ordinary disposal only before responsibility or after lawful responsibility-root release |
| Assoc → H | Strong, GC-visible member | Publication store; immutable through the published lifetime |
| S → H | Strong, traversed member | Before publication; unpublished rollback or ordinary post-responsibility clearing |
| H → D | One native lease | H construction through H destruction |
| S → A_src | Strong, traversed reference | Adapter establishment for canonical S; later structural/evidence lifetime |
| S → ledger | Strong, traversed reference to the existing ledger | Preserves canonical source/cut ownership; no copied ledger |
| Configuration | Embedded native record | Scalars and named callable-slot roles; no hidden owning callable cache |
| S’s F/O slots | Two installed and two original owning slots when populated | Explicit retention; same-invocation LR0 discharge or named unresolved retention |
| C_R0 | Embedded storage, not an independently leased object | Cannot outlive S |
| C_R0 reference slots | Individually owned and traversed | Reservation, verification, expected-installed and displaced-reference purposes; exact R0 transfer/release rules |
| D’s S/Association/work identities | Non-owning comparison data | No owner dereference; historical scalars after B4 |
| Metadata | Existing explicit ER2 ownership | Process-lifetime anchor bundle; not per-source retention |

Two Python references to H do not create two native H leases. C_R0 embedding does not hide its Python references.

A_src is the already-required source-lifetime adapter role, not the canonical retaining adapter used for Q. Its access to callable slots is borrowed. The candidate’s graph and prohibition on hidden owners/caches do not permit adding an adapter-owned S or F/O cache.

## 3. Uniqueness, publication and rollback

The one-to-one relationship is selected, not deferred:

```text
Assoc._source_owner = S
Assoc._source_lifetime = H
S.handle = H
H.cell = D
D.bound_source_identity = comparison identity of S
```

Canonical setup creates S once. The binding writer obtains that S from the current Association. Alias factories, shared handles, replacement and retrieval-through-bind are excluded.

The inherited callback-free construction interval and first-publication-wins rule prevent a second publication during private initialization. Pre-native reentry can win first; the outer binding then rejects before allocation. These rules are preserved in [CP §25 and Appendix R2 §§11–16](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-four-target-correction-comprehensive-continuation.md:306).

Failure accounting is complete for the affected construction edges:

| Failure boundary | Required state/result |
|---|---|
| S allocation or storing S | No source effect, H or R0 callable obligation; ordinary disposal is safe; no replacement of an already established S |
| D allocation, before root | Dispose private native candidate |
| After root acquisition | End borrows; clear/decrement root using independently live Registry argument |
| H allocation or later private initialization | Root disposal; remove unpublished anchor; dispose any acquired H/construction ownership |
| After S → H, before publication | Clear S.handle while constructor independently owns H; then ER1 H release and construction-lease release |
| Publication completed | B2 persists; losing the return value does not undo publication |
| Crash/uncertain execution | SC; no inferred rollback or publication |

Clearing S.handle during rollback cannot be H’s last decrement because the constructor still owns H. Removing the root first cannot destroy Registry because the binding argument independently owns it.

Rollback returns the existing S to its unbound state; it does not create another source owner.

## 4. Published but not installed

This interval is safe within the specified architecture.

H publication establishes a native binding and root, not installed-source status. At this point S may have no adapter or callable slots populated. R0’s L1–L4 sequence establishes S, then A_src, then owned callable references, all before source mutation.

An observer can see the published binding. **P0 acquisition is not categorically forbidden merely because installation has not occurred:** eligible B2 admits a root-capable inspection. P0 does not itself authorize restoration, installation or E3.

The relevant safety boundaries are already present:

- Installation independently checks current binding/profile/source prerequisites.
- Finalization retains its Phase-A and enclosing semantic/handoff prerequisites.
- Current graph access must validate representation before dependent use.
- Binding alone supplies none of those semantic facts.

Consequently, accepting H or acquiring P0 cannot legitimately make empty source slots actionable as installed-source authority.

A failure after publication retains B2 and the same S/H graph. It does not justify rollback, rebinding, BF by inference or source-effect replay. Pre-E3 invalidation preserves ownership while forbidding admission. The future activation implementation remains separately unqualified; this correction does not invent an activation-recovery route.

## 5. GC, mixed cycles and caller loss

The ownership cycle is real:

```text
D → Registry → Assoc → S → H → D
```

Its D → Registry edge is externally rooted, so active/BF responsibility is not an isolated collectible Python cycle.

S traverses its Python fields, including embedded C_R0 references. The native lease from H does not pretend to be a Python ownership edge. On success, explicit D → Registry root removal breaks the retaining cycle before ordinary collection becomes permissible.

The relevant CPython GC mechanism distinguishes external references from references within the collected containers and invokes clearing on the resulting unreachable set. This supports the external-root argument; it does not permit omitting S’s own references from traversal. [CPython 3.14.5 GC implementation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.14.5/Python/gc.c)

The safety predicate is supplied by the lifecycle:

- **Before responsibility:** source-instance obligations have not begun.
- **Active/BF:** external rooting retains S; ordinary clearing cannot discharge responsibility.
- **After lawful B4:** restoration/resource prerequisites have completed and required callable obligations have ended before root release.

A second, invented GC “permission bit” is unnecessary. An implementation that clears active/BF S or uses `tp_clear` for restoration would violate the selected contract.

Caller loss cannot remove Anchor → D → Registry → Assoc → S. S also keeps H alive while that relationship is required.

After B4:

- Retained H can keep rootless D alive without keeping S.
- D cannot expose C_R0 or dereference S.
- Its comparison identities remain historical scalar data.
- Metadata remains independently ER2-owned.
- Last-lease destruction remains governed by root NULL, terminal state, absent anchor/H/P0 and zero P1.

A pointer-shaped representation is not permission to dereference the historical identity. The exact representation and all uses must satisfy the candidate’s scalar-only contract and future artifact audit.

S can exist without D before binding or after its H edge is cleared during lawful ordinary teardown. The candidate does not permit active S to lose the H/D lifetime it requires.

## 6. R0 references, LR0 and BF

The named categories match original R0. Separate populated slots denote separate owned references even when they contain the same F or O object.

The decisive accounting rules are:

- Reserved O ownership transfers once to registration.
- Displaced registration ownership transfers once into C_R0.
- Guards and remaining temporaries receive their specified non-last release.
- S’s installation/original slots are separate source-lifetime references.
- Transferred ownership is removed from its old slot and is not decremented again.
- Unused slots remain empty; unresolved references stay in named slots.

I examined CAND §D’s “transfer or release” wording after restoration publication. It does **not** authorize delaying registration replacement’s ownership transfer until then: §D first requires completion of the separately governed transaction, and §C expressly retains R0’s transfer rules. A reservation already transferred by that transaction is no longer C_R0-owned and cannot be transferred again by the later discharge step. No alternative transfer sequence is accepted here.

Successful ordering remains:

```text
restoration/coverage transaction and its registration transfers
→ restoration disposition
→ remaining temporary discharge
→ eligible S callable-slot discharge
→ resource-discharge publication
→ Closing/root release/B4/anchor drop/finalizer-P0 release
```

F’s executable-module owner and O’s authenticated module/restored-registration owners supply the required non-last proofs. Fixture aliases do not.

BF remains phase-sensitive:

| BF point | Retention |
|---|---|
| Before callable discharge completes | Required S/C_R0 references and unresolved obligations remain rooted |
| After callable discharge, before successful root release | Discharged references stay discharged; structural graph and failure/results remain |

Neither case permits GC to erase unresolved responsibility, new P0 entry, another finalization attempt or COMPLETE. Retaining the existing finite Registry graph is inherited failure responsibility, not a claim of healthy cleanup.

## 7. A_src, S3 and equivalence

A_src obtains S through the canonical Association and borrows only within the admitted owner lifetime. Its lookup does not depend on an H → S backlink: the root-capable path reaches S through Registry/Assoc. Ordinary canonical ownership outside that protected path remains separately required. No standalone adapter invocation acquires source authority merely by possessing A_src.

After B4 or during BF, retaining A_src as structural/evidence state does not grant another installation/restoration operation.

CAND §E covers the new Association member, S’s complete layout/GC fields, embedded references, D identity representation and establishment/publication/rollback/traversal/clearing/destruction implementations. Historical S3 PASS is not used as evidence for these future bytes.

Equivalence also covers the source-owner graph and reference accounting. Additional fixture S/H owners, D leases, adapter retention or Registry roots cannot supply a production lifetime proof.

**R1 conclusion:** all fifteen requested R1 decision points are resolved within the bounded architecture. No remaining ownership, GC, rollback, BF, LR0, adapter-lookup or equivalence choice requires the implementer to select safety semantics.

# D. R2 — INDEPENDENT HIGH-RISK FINDING

**Verdict: PASS — the rejected-attempt P0 unwind defect is closed.**

The decisive material is CAND §§F–I and the inherited [live-handle/pin model](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/docs/project-checkpoints/ore-v3-stage3c-post-csr1-four-target-correction-comprehensive-continuation.md:316).

## 1. Acquisition, release and interference

Successful acquisition CAS is the only event creating invocation-local P0 ownership. Recording that obligation belongs to the same uninterrupted native execution. No admitted Python exception, callback or signal-processing boundary occurs between those steps. Crash/corruption is separately SC.

An implementation that returns from a successful acquisition without recording ownership would violate the contract; choosing a variable, flag or equivalent compiled representation does not change the selected semantics.

P0 occupancy is separate from phase, E1 and P1 count. Release requires this invocation’s recorded obligation and clears that occupancy through the existing atomic transition while preserving unrelated fields.

Legitimate P1 interference may require retry. A failed CAS is not release. An ownership inconsistency does not authorize clearing occupancy.

There is no legal P0 ABA handoff during those retries:

1. Only this invocation may release its acquired P0.
2. Another acquisition requires P0 absent.
3. P1 changes its own count, not P0 ownership.
4. Another main-thread protected invocation cannot intervene while the current native call retains the admitted execution context.

E1 release may allow a configuration writer to proceed, but it does not transfer P0 or permit that writer to release it. Invalidation does not erase the rejecting invocation’s obligation.

This establishes safety, not a new wait-free progress guarantee. The inherited lock-free design and qualification timeout discipline remain; a timeout is not successful release evidence.

## 2. The H-lease question

**There is no omitted invocation-owned H lease to release.**

CP Appendix R2 §18 selects an independently live H passed through the ordinary Python/C call boundary. H owns one native cell lease for H’s lifetime. CAND §C explicitly preserves that single lease, and §I adds no S/H ownership decrement to rejection.

Thus:

- P0 cannot be acquired before live H exists.
- H’s lease protects D throughout release.
- Dropping rejected-attempt P0 cannot be D’s last lifetime protection.
- Returning from the native invocation does not require releasing an invented extra handle lease.
- Construction ownership exists only on the distinct binding path.

In B2, canonical Assoc/S references also retain published H. Ordinary argument/reference cleanup therefore cannot destroy H on the acquired-P0 rejection paths at issue. A stale live H rejected before acquisition retains the inherited bounded H destructor contract; that is not P0 unwind or source cleanup.

## 3. Rejection coverage

Here **I** means scanner ownership, **C** canonical owning temporaries, and **X** transferred inspection exception.

| Class | Ownership/cleanup determination |
|---|---|
| R2-A: no acquisition | No P0/E1 release; context, phase, occupied or capacity rejection cannot release an incumbent |
| R2-B: E1 acquisition fails | P0 only; end native checks and release P0; no E1 cleanup |
| R2-C: pre-scan rejection | P0/E1, no scanner/canonical ownership; end checks, release E1 then P0 |
| R2-D: initial frame unavailable | No returned frame; preserve clear state or actual error, then terminal release |
| R2-E: ancestry/depth mismatch | Discharge every populated current/next/code IR0 slot before release |
| R2-F: materialization/getter error | Transfer X, discharge I, release E1/P0, restore X |
| R2-G: post-T1 transition rejection | I/C/X empty; end applicable checks and release |
| R2-H: ordinary canonical rejection | End reads, discharge C under ER0, preserve clear indicator, release |
| R2-I: unexpected canonical error | Same C cleanup while preserving active error; no X transfer |
| R2-J: pre-E2 invalidation | Actual scanner/canonical subregion determines ownership; no phase-based guess |
| R2-K1: post-E2/pre-E3 rejection | I/C/X empty; retain historical E2, end attempt eligibility, release |
| R2-K2: unexpected inspection result without error | Clean actual I slots; fixed rejection; no fabricated exception |
| R2-K3: finite capacity failure | Ownership at the check determines unwind; no wrap, overwrite or replacement slot |
| R2-K4: owner/binding mismatch | Validate before dependent access; end borrows, discharge C and release |

The scanner ownership vector is bounded to current frame, successor frame and code temporary. “I possible” therefore names actual populated slots, not an unspecified cleanup strategy.

R2-G does not create a new gate or reopen a writer interval. Where no rejecting operation exists at that boundary, that row is unused. It still supplies the disposition if an already-admitted transition check rejects.

For the pinned initial getter, materialization failure can clear its error and return NULL. R2-D correctly refuses to reconstruct MemoryError. Its actual-error branch does not claim that such an error must occur on this pinned path. [CPython 3.14.5 `PyThreadState_GetFrame`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.14.5/Python/pystate.c)

## 4. Complete rejected-invocation tail

The selected partial order is valid:

```text
end dependent work
→ transfer actual inspection exception, where applicable
→ complete IR0
→ complete ER0
→ end remaining admitted cleanup/borrows
→ release owned E1
→ release owned P0
→ restore X, where applicable
→ return/resume Python
```

IR0 and canonical ER0 do not coexist on a valid post-T1 path. The sequence does not require executing inapplicable cleanup.

All raw-slot, Registry/Association/S, container and metadata-derived uses end before E1 release. Rejection evidence derived from those values must already have been copied into admitted scalar storage. Post-P0 markers use separate storage and do not reread D.

E1-before-P0 is safe because only terminal native control work remains after writer exclusion ends. That work requires lifetime and P0 ownership, not continued source-configuration stability.

The remaining resources are accounted for:

| Resource | Terminal disposition |
|---|---|
| Scanner references | IR0 before E1 release |
| Canonical owning getters | ER0 before E1 release |
| Root/source/container borrows | End before E1 release |
| E1 | Release only if acquired |
| P0 | Exactly one release if acquired and not committed into finalization |
| H cell lease | Remains owned by H, not the invocation |
| Construction lease | Not present on operational rejection |
| Native local buffers/scalar result | No Python ownership or deferred source cleanup |
| X | Transferred back exactly once |
| Evidence | Fixed lifetime-owned storage; not an invocation-owned allocated slot |
| S/H published graph | Intentionally remains canonically owned |

The inherited evidence model is one immutable E2/result area plus bounded diagnostic storage and checked counters. It does not introduce a per-attempt heap reservation needing a hidden release. Capacity exhaustion can reject later attempts permanently; that is the specified finite-storage consequence, not healthy reuse or a leaked P0.

## 5. Error preservation

The inspection transfer is sound under the selected constraints. The pinned getter removes the exception from the indicator without destroying it. Restoration installs that owned exception and decrements the old destination; the required empty destination avoids a callback-bearing replacement. E1/P0 operations are explicitly prohibited from Python API calls, allocation, decref, callbacks or deallocation. [CPython 3.14.5 exception implementation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.14.5/Python/errors.c)

No invocation-owned H decrement remains after restoration. X therefore survives independently after P0 release, and no omitted terminal cleanup clobbers it.

For canonical errors, the active indicator remains in place. The owning getter temporaries have independent owners: the live generator retains its code, and the retained type owns its dictionary. Their matching non-last decrements do not invoke destruction. [Generator getter](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.14.5/Objects/genobject.c), [type-dictionary getter](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.14.5/Objects/typeobject.c)

Ordinary rejection retains a scalar/native disposition. Any Python diagnostic construction occurs after both obligations end and cannot replace an error that must be preserved.

## 6. E2, E3, BF and actors

E2 is immutable and bound to lifetime/attempt/profile/table/Association. R2-K1 terminates that attempt’s admission eligibility; later calls cannot submit old E2 as authority. Capacity constraints prevent overwriting it to disguise reuse.

E3 is the separate one-shot commit. It changes the same invocation’s role to admitted finalizer. It does not run the rejected-attempt epilogue or transfer P0 to another executor. Uncertain E3 execution remains SC.

Success preserves P0 through B4 and anchor drop. BF closes new P0, ends required uses, releases the executor’s pin and retains the responsibility graph. Neither is folded into ordinary rejection.

Actor boundaries remain distinct:

- Native configuration writers may proceed after E1 release.
- Another P0 acquisition requires occupancy release and admitted context.
- Python callbacks/signal processing remain excluded until protected return.
- P1 may overlap but receives only scalar evidence rights.
- Native signal recording may continue without Python handler execution.

**R2 conclusion:** acquisition, every admitted rejection class, terminal ownership, errors and success/BF separation are architecturally complete. No hidden H/evidence cleanup or unresolved release-order choice remains.

# E. R3-MX — INDEPENDENT HIGH-RISK FINDING

**Verdict: PASS — the occupied-P0 target/evidence defect is closed.**

## 1. Required property and authority for the split

The inherited requirement is exclusive root-capable occupancy: a failed contender must not gain, clear or transfer the incumbent’s P0.

PR identified an unspecified route to the occupied branch; it did not establish that impossible production scheduling must be demonstrated. CP already distinguishes helper, negative-artifact and production claims. The [correction authorization, §§50–55](/Users/erale/.codex/sessions/2026/09/14/rollout-2026-09-14T14-47-53-01a0a1e4-100b-7851-932a-b7774b16a8ff.jsonl:77) expressly allows distinct evidence contracts while retaining the safety property.

CAND §§J–L select the required combination:

1. Production actor/call-graph exclusion evidence.
2. Direct occupied-state execution of the exact production primitive.
3. Production integration/equivalence evidence.

Neither half substitutes for the other.

## 2. Gate order and actors

The selected order safely puts runtime/native-context and main-thread/GIL admission before context-dependent object access; live H precedes D access; P0 precedes E1 and root traversal. Pre-P0 D reads are immutable publication/control checks, not current canonical graph validation.

| Actor | Admitted reachability |
|---|---|
| Main Python thread, ordinary entry | May reach acquisition after earlier gates; no concurrent protected invocation |
| Another Python thread | Must obtain GIL before Python operation entry; a precursor may witness that wait, not access P0 |
| Foreign native thread | Rejected by context requirements before object/P0 access |
| Same-thread Python callback/reentry | Excluded during the protected interval by the closed operation/error/cleanup contracts |
| Native nested entry | No selected production route admits protected-interval nesting; an added route would require renewed call-graph review |
| Unreviewed native entry | Outside the admitted executable graph |
| Signal producer | Native recording may occur; Python handler execution does not enter the protected interval |
| Qualification driver | Separate admitted helper route; does not prove production contention |

The production proof must bind the eventual executable call graph. An inaccessible branch in ordinary production does not make its local occupied-state safety condition optional.

## 3. Primitive and fixture

`P0_TRY_ACQUIRE` decomposes the existing native acquisition operation. It is internal, does not create another semantic admission authority and performs no Python/root access.

The input requirement is a valid, aligned, live word with the complete manifest-bound representation—not merely two arbitrary bits. Eligible B2 and occupied P0 select the target case; all other fields must also satisfy the inherited valid-combination constraints.

Numerical masks and layout remain implementation-local. A fixture with invalid lease/count/state combinations would fail the stated precondition and could not qualify the branch.

Standalone storage supplies only local memory lifetime. It supplies no Registry, Assoc, S, H, canonical D, source responsibility, E1 or E2/E3. Its synthetic occupancy is not an acquired production P0.

## 4. Occupied branch and attribution

For eligible occupied input, CAND §K freezes:

```text
gate = P0_OCCUPIED
disposition = REJECT_NO_ACQUISITION
```

The branch performs no write, successful-acquisition CAS, decrement, E1 operation or root access. The broader no-write requirement excludes changes to phase, P1, attempts and other word fields.

The tag has one producing branch. The driver cannot inject it. Therefore phase/context rejection, CAS interference and fixture invalidity cannot legitimately masquerade as occupied acknowledgement.

Endpoint equality is insufficient by itself. The proposed proof also requires exact artifact inspection establishing no occupied-branch write and attributed execution of that same implementation. Fixture interference invalidates the claim.

No release obligation exists. Standalone storage teardown is not `PIN_RELEASE`.

## 5. Integration and equivalence

CAND §L requires the same compiled production primitive, including masks, ordering, atomic operations, encoding and occupied-branch behavior.

Production integration must establish:

- Earlier gates dominate its call.
- Rejection cannot enter success handling.
- R2-A performs no release.
- E1/root access is unreachable after acquisition rejection.

The selected primitive is the production acquisition operation; an alternate acquisition route bypassing those constraints would violate the specified call-graph/integration contract.

Allowed driver/storage/evidence differences do not supply the no-write or no-acquisition property. They supply valid local input and observation only.

The accepted claim is exactly the bounded one: **the production primitive rejects a valid occupied local state without modifying or acquiring it.** It does not qualify production contention scheduling or integrated source/root lifetime.

Wrong artifact, incomplete equivalence, invalid fixture or missing target cannot produce a production-transfer PASS. Missing occupied acknowledgement remains INCONCLUSIVE for that target; independently attributed forbidden behavior can still establish FAIL.

**R3-MX conclusion:** actor route, gate order, branch behavior, attribution, no-release rule, evidence class and equivalence are selected. No safety-consequential choice remains.

**Original callable profile**

For this initial bounded proposal, both originals must be the **same authenticated `signal.default_int_handler` object**. Other Python callables, callable instances, `SIG_DFL`, `SIG_IGN` and foreign native originals are unsupported admission cases: reject before installation.
