````````text
The authenticated source-close candidate independently establishes its own two-original setup. It does not prove V4 construction.

In CPython 3.14.5, `default_int_handler` is a module-exposed built-in callable that raises `KeyboardInterrupt`, distinct from `SIG_DFL`. The signal implementation obtains it from the module dictionary when installing Python’s default SIGINT behavior. The installed `signal.py` imports `_signal`’s exports. These provide module references, not an unconditional immortality guarantee. Module replacement or interpreter teardown must not be assumed harmless. [CPython 3.14.5 signal implementation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.14.5/Modules/signalmodule.c)

Therefore admission and finalization must authenticate the exact original object and surviving module anchors. No ambient reference-count guess is sufficient.

Strong ownership is:

- Signal module dictionaries → **O**.

- **F** has no direct captured-owner edge. Its globals belong to the ordinary module environment; canonical registry retention remains separately accounted.

A shared module binding is **not** a renamed temporary guard. Its independent purpose is executable-code availability before and after any particular source lifetime. No per-capability list, permanent guard cache or extra module binding is permitted.

Source-instance resource completion does not require unloading the implementation module or destroying Python’s signal functions. Ordinary module/interpreter teardown is outside this bounded source-finalization claim; it cannot be used to discharge an outstanding source-instance obligation.

**Last-release analysis**

All callable decrements required by the successful source-finalization contract are **LR0**:

|---|---|
| C’s original guards | S’s original slots and authenticated signal-module references |

No LR1 argument is needed. The contract must not depend on “a last DECREF probably has no effects.”

A Python function’s actual last destruction can notify function watchers, clear weakrefs and release transitive references. A built-in callable’s deallocator also clears weakrefs and releases referenced objects. Thus generic last destruction is not assumed callback-free. | S3-2 Actual loaded class identities | Required independently of names and metadata agreement |
````````
````````text
| S3-3 No instance-read bootstrap | Satisfied |
| S3-4 Complete layout, including unread slots | Satisfied |
| S3-5 Defining-class inheritance binding | Satisfied |
| S3-6 Member and layout authentication | Satisfied |
| S3-7 Layout versus semantic image | Correctly separated |
| S3-8 No caller-selected table entries | Satisfied |
| S3-9 Publication before responsibility | Explicit and necessary |
| S3-10 Monotonic invalidation | Preserved |
| S3-11 Future slot treated prospectively | Satisfied |
| S3-12 Runtime/native/ABI binding | Satisfied as a mandatory future manifest gate |

This does not approve the lifetime of a published table or its native owner; those belong to BLOCKING 2.

### 6. Bootstrap-circularity review

| Attack | Independent binding required by the candidate | Result |
|---|---|---|
| Schema generated solely from loaded classes | Reviewed source-derived expected schema exists independently | Rejected |
| Old schema/native artifact with new module | Repository source, loaded module and native manifest must all agree | Rejected |
| Internally consistent stale module/schema | Current authenticated authority identity remains an independent input | Rejected |
| Unauthenticated descriptor | Exact descriptor category, defining class and member-entry association | Rejected before descriptor metadata use |
| Instance probes justify offsets | Explicitly forbidden | No circular proof admitted |
| Semantic mutation preserves layout | Separate C1/image eligibility | Layout cannot rescue it |
| Future slot omitted | Complete future schema must include it | Rejected |
| Instrumented table reused by production | Separate artifact identities and explicit mapping required | No automatic transfer |

The schema cannot be accepted merely because the loaded classes produce it.

### 7. Metadata API classification/audit

The candidate correctly adds a **bootstrap metadata boundary**, not merely public APIs.

| Operation/representation | Classification | Ownership and valid-path behavior |
|---|---|---|
| `PyType_GetSlot` | Public, Stable ABI | Fixed slot ID; borrowed metadata pointer |
| `Py_tp_members` | Public slot identifier | Result interpreted as member-table metadata, not a callable |
| `Py_tp_base`, `Py_tp_bases` | Public slot identifiers | Borrowed base/type-tuple metadata |
| `PyType_GetFlags` | Public, Stable ABI | Scalar |
| `PyType_GetDict` | Public full API; not the selected Stable-ABI surface | New reference; dictionary treated read-only |
| `PyMemberDef` | Public structure represented in Stable ABI | Fields themselves have defined representation |
| `PyDescr_TYPE`, `PyDescr_NAME`, member-descriptor layout | CPython-specific, non-Limited layout access | Borrowed fields of an already authenticated descriptor |
| `tp_basicsize`, `tp_itemsize`, dictionary/weakref offsets | CPython type-layout access | Explicit bootstrap-only dependency |
| Exact dictionary/tuple operations | Public API/macros according to selection | Borrowed contents; no protocol dispatch |
| Native table allocation | Native bootstrap operation | May fail; no publication on failure |

Stable classification of a metadata carrier does not make arbitrary pointers to that carrier valid. The selected type/descriptor instances must already be authenticated. [Pinned Stable ABI inventory](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.14.5/Misc/stable_abi.toml).

On admitted exact objects and fixed valid arguments, the inspected metadata getters do not invoke descriptors, execute Python, release the GIL or check signals. Invalid arguments can enter Python-error construction paths and must be excluded before calling them. Bootstrap allocation and reference disposal remain outside canonical responsibility.

### 8. Slot-construction review

The pinned implementation supports the proposed cross-check for these classes:

`base basicsize + processed-local-slot rank × sizeof(PyObject *)`

It sorts processed local names, places reference members after the base layout and advances by pointer size. The admitted names require no private-name mangling; there are no `__dict__`/`__weakref__` entries, variable-size bases or multiple inheritance. Loaded metadata remains authoritative, with the formula an independent consistency check. [Pinned slot construction](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.14.5/Objects/typeobject.c).

The schema must require exact ordinary member flags, including their numeric expected value, rather than accepting arbitrary flags as “ordinary.” For the inspected construction path, that value is zero.

Unsupported bases, added slots or layout flags reject before instance access.

The candidate’s complete current slot census matches the source.

| `_Owned` | `_authority`, `_faults` |

`_Owned` implicitly derives from `object`; the other listed classes directly derive from `_Owned`. All use ordinary `type`.

No numerical future offsets are authenticated by this table.

### 10. Future `_Association._source_lifetime` consequence

A current canonical edge to the prospective SourceAuthority is justified. Placing it on the permanent association is consistent with existing ownership.

However:

- The future complete schema must contain the added slot.
- Current source hashes cease to identify the modified implementation.
- Its defining-class/member metadata must be rederived and authenticated.
- The edge must bind the exact SourceAuthority/native-cell identity.
- Unbound, publication, repeated-bind, failed-publication and closed-lifetime histories require qualification.
- Existing writer/image controls must explicitly accommodate the new canonical operation.

The slot placement is reasonable; the publication transition is not yet complete.

### 11. Defining-class/inherited-role review

| Inherited slot | Necessary roles |
|---|---|
| `_authority` | Invocation, ledger, current/cut episode, binding, adapter, bridge |
| `_faults` | Registry, selected association, invocation, ledger, current/cut episode, binding, adapter, bridge |

The defining class is `_Owned` in every case. Exact derived type is checked before using its role entry.

Registry and Association `_authority` reads are unnecessary. The root map’s peer associations need only their `registry` and `work` reverse-edge reads, not the selected association’s entire semantic traversal. That distinction should be explicit in the executable role enumeration; it does not require another slot or semantic check.

### 12. Role-table authentication/publication/invalidation

Before publication require:

1. Independently bound runtime, native artifact and project image.
2. Exact classes, metaclass and base chain.
3. Complete local-member schema.
4. Exact descriptor category and defining-class/member association.
5. Exact names, types, flags and bounded terminator.
6. Nonnegative, aligned offsets with subtraction-based bounds checks.
7. No duplicated or ambiguous defining entries.
8. Exact finite per-role permission subset.
9. No canonical responsibility or source mutation.
10. One immutable release publication.

Later physical-layout, semantic-image, executable-profile and lifetime validity remain distinct.

- Different build/layout requires requalification.
- Relevant in-process semantic/profile violation permanently invalidates the lifetime.
- Stale associations receive ordinary pre-entry rejection.
- No refresh, reseal or endpoint restoration erases invalidation.

### 13. Metadata-reference lifetime

The claimed ownership chain is plausible but incomplete:

`cell → exact heap type → defining extension module → metadata bundle`

An actual module association can keep a defining module referenced through a heap type. Merely sharing a module name cannot. The pinned API documents this association specifically for module-associated type creation. [Pinned type/module contract](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.14.5/Doc/c-api/type.rst).

The candidate still needs to specify:

- The actual cell/type/module ownership mechanism.
- Traversal and clearing of metadata-held Python references.
- Native reader pins that dominate table use.
- Whether module clearing is possible before ordinary cell destruction.
- The precise boundary beyond which interpreter shutdown is unsupported.

“Metadata teardown may not occur while a cell can use it” is an invariant, not its enforcement mechanism. Its ER2 classification is therefore not independently closed.

### 14. ROOT-A independent selection result

ROOT-A is sufficient and reasonably minimal for the selected design.

ROOT-B could also reach Registry through `association.registry`, then validate current membership. It would not eliminate the Registry/map/reverse-edge checks. Therefore ROOT-A is justified, although not uniquely necessary as a matter of graph theory.

A retained Registry alone is not authority for a particular association: native lifetime/work binding plus current map traversal remains mandatory.

### 15. Prospective source-lifetime binding review

**A blocking publication gap remains.**

The candidate specifies one binding and forbids replacement, but leaves:

> “Repeated binding returns/rejects…”

It does not freeze the exact publication/exclusion operation that makes the unbound check and attachment one canonical transition.

A required adverse schedule is:

1. Outer binding authenticates an unbound association.
2. Construction or another permitted prepublication boundary allows reentry.
3. Inner binding publishes a fully initialized binding.
4. Outer binding resumes.

The contract must prescribe how the outer operation detects the winner, handles its unpublished cell/root reference and returns or rejects without replacement. No implementation exists, so this is an architectural under-specification—not a reproduced overwrite.

Publication of SourceAuthority before native-cell initialization is forbidden, but the candidate has not established the mechanism making it impossible.

### 16. Native root-ownership/cycle analysis

| Edge | Proposed ownership class |
|---|---|
| Registry → association map | Python-GC-visible strong |
| Map → Association | Python-GC-visible strong |
| Association → Registry | Python-GC-visible strong |
| Association → SourceAuthority | Prospective Python strong; GC behavior must be specified |
| SourceAuthority → cell | Prospective strong reference |
| Cell → Registry | Python reference held natively; deliberately excluded from clearing |
| Cell → heap type | Native-object/type ownership |
| Type → module → metadata | Claimed strong ownership; exact implementation contract incomplete |
| Operation pin → cell/storage | Native lifetime mechanism, not automatically a Python reference |

The candidate deliberately depends on one edge not being collected. That deserves an explicit implementation model.

CPython’s container contract requires GC support for types holding potentially cyclic container references, including traversal and, for mutable containers, clearing. The proposed cell holds and later clears a Registry reference. Simply omitting that edge from an otherwise ordinary container’s GC contract is not an independently justified lifetime mechanism. [Pinned GC contract](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.14.5/Doc/c-api/gcsupport.rst).

Conversely, exposing the entire isolated cycle to ordinary collection can permit collection before E1, after caller loss. A separately specified native anchor might solve this, but adopting it changes a material lifetime assumption and requires re-review.

This is **not** a claim that every native-held reference must be collectible, or that deliberate finite retention is inherently wrong.

### 17. Root lifetime/use-after-free analysis

Once a valid owning root reference and continuous exclusion exist, descendant lifetime is straightforward. The unresolved part precedes that proof:

- What owns a cell before a pin is acquired?
- Which atomic event excludes destruction?
- Which state closes root-capable entry?
- Can a diagnostic pin coexist with root release?
- Who keeps the metadata owner alive?

A pin counter cannot make a stale address safe to increment. Entry must begin with an independently live handle, and destruction must participate in the same defined lifetime protocol.

The candidate states these requirements but does not fully specify that protocol.

### 18. Borrowed-descendant/reference review

| Reference | Independent owner | Review |
|---|---|---|
| Map entries | Live stable exact map | Borrowing valid |
| Association role slots | Live stable association | Borrowing valid |
| Ledger/episode/binding slots | Live stable containing object | Borrowing valid |
| Composition entries | Live stable composition dictionary | Borrowing valid |
| Tuple elements | Live exact tuple | Borrowing valid |
| Function getter results | Retained exact function/image bundle | Borrowed |
| `PyGen_GetCode` result | Live generator and authenticated code image | New reference; ER0 release |
| `PyType_GetDict` result | Live retained type | New reference; ER0 release |
| Error-indicator inspection | Thread state | No owning exception reference needed |
| Root reference | Native lifetime mechanism | BLOCKING 2 remains open |
| Metadata bundle ownership | Module/type/native lifetime mechanism | ER2 not fully closed |

There is no need to INCREF descendants merely to record an address.

The generator getter explicitly increments the returned code reference; the function getters return their stored objects. [Pinned generator getter](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.14.5/Objects/genobject.c), [pinned function getters](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.14.5/Objects/funcobject.c).

### 19. Successful root-release/ER review

The **local ER0 arithmetic is sound under its stated prerequisites**:

- Registry map still owns Association.
- Association’s `registry` slot still owns Registry.
- No collector, writer or teardown has cleared those edges.
- The native root reference is an additional reference.

Removing that additional reference cannot immediately make Registry’s reference count zero.

The problem is not circular arithmetic. The problem is completing the lifetime/GC/pin contract that guarantees the intact graph at that moment.

Read closure must precede clearing the root pointer. The finalizer’s own last use must finish, and no other root-capable pin may survive. Diagnostic/evidence-only pins must be distinguished from root-capable pins.

### 20. Unresolved-failure retention

Retaining failure is semantically appropriate; it is not recovery or cleanup.

For one fixed lifetime, the retained graph can include:

- The Registry and all associations it already owns.
- The selected invocation, ledger, binding and current/retained episodes.
- Adapter/bridge and their retained continuation state.
- SourceAuthority/native cell.
- R0 failure-retained callable references.
- Fixed native evidence storage.
- Shared metadata.

It is not necessarily “one association’s worth” of memory because the root is Registry.

There is no intrinsic need for unbounded growth merely from repeated observation or coalesced retained requests. That finite claim depends on the closed profile forbidding new work/rebinding and on bounded native attempt/evidence storage. It does not extend to arbitrary future work added to the Registry.

Process termination eventually reclaims process memory; it is not healthy lifetime completion.

### 21. Native-cell destruction/lifetime

The candidate correctly forbids a destructor from restoring sources, repairing state, replaying work or creating COMPLETE.

The following need correction before destruction is closed:

- Explicit unpublished-versus-published cell states.
- Disposal of a losing or interrupted unpublished binding.
- Pin acquisition and destruction exclusion.
- GC traversal/clearing versus the deliberate native anchor.
- Metadata ownership and ordinary module teardown.
- Read closure before root clearing.

After successful root release, Association may still own SourceAuthority, and SourceAuthority may still own a rootless cell containing immutable results. That is legitimate continuing lifetime, not proof that those objects were destroyed.

### 24. Function/code-image review

The getter-based approach reproduces the current identity comparisons without generator/frame internal reads.

The complete image set includes:

- `_CLASS_IMAGES`, including its enum classes.
- `_FUNCTION_IMAGES`, including property getters.
- The separate canonical action image.
- `_EXECUTOR_CODE`.
- Required C1 module-global bindings and P1 census identities.

A module/class name is insufficient provenance.

`PyGen_GetCode` returns a new reference. Function code/default/keyword-default/closure getters return borrowed values. Absent defaults or closure can legitimately be NULL; that is not the same as a required project slot being NULL.

The getters’ exact-type preconditions must be established before calling them.

### 25. Cut/settlement minimization

The source establishes:

1. `_cut` invokes admission and `_runner`.
2. It captures the current epoch and checks expected identity.
3. It checks adapter pending state.
4. It stores `cut_attempted` and `_cut_epoch`.
5. It arbitrates using `first.setdefault(0, _CUT)`.

Only the winning discriminator establishes K. The first two stores do not.

A receipt winner prevents a cut winner in the same cell. A later admitted settlement is blocked by CLOSED. Replacement epochs arise through `_settle` only after coherent action/accounting checks. Therefore old episode history need not be reconstructed at E2.

Winning cut does not prove normal return, restoration or COMPLETE.

### 26. Retaining-adapter minimization

The predicate survives:

`absent OR no attempt OR dequeued`

with exact role, type, cell representation, authority and fault checks.

- Attempted before receipt completion: pending remains; reject.
- Retained but not dequeued: reject.
- Dequeue interrupted before its first-winner store: reject.
- Dequeue store completed, return interrupted: continuation disposition is already published; other owner/activity gates remain separate.
- Dequeued earlier epoch followed by settlement/cut: valid.
- Foreign adapter or fault: reject.

Requiring `_epoch is final_cut_epoch` would incorrectly reject a qualified history. Its omission is justified.

### 27. Acquisition-bridge minimization

The predicate survives for the assigned terminal observation:

`absent OR (_released is True AND exact _errors dictionary is empty)`

with current composition, type, authority and fault checks.

Earlier `_entered`, `_entry_result`, `_stage`, `_finish_claim`, `_published`, captured epoch and establishment fields are not needed on this terminal path.

This does not authorize reusing the F2 policy for new work after release. Such work remains outside the admitted closed lifetime.

### 28. F2 terminal-evidence proof

The bridge’s `_finish` checks, before setting release:

- Active state and running owner.
- First finish claim.
- Publication and current epoch.
- Exact F2 policy/image.
- Zero depth.
- No delivery or pending representative.
- No F2 failed state.
- Completed protection scopes.
- Every recorded signal restoration marked restored.
- Staged receipt materialization, if needed.

An interruption before release leaves release false. An interruption after release can retain an error; the candidate rejects nonempty errors. If error-detail publication itself is interrupted after release, the preceding required effects have already happened; normal return is not part of this terminal fact.

The authenticated Case-F trace showed unreleased bridge, retained stage and failed/unresolved F2 restoration before ambient cleanup. That supports the distinction; it was not rerun.

### 29. Fault/invalidation equivalence

Registry and association fault cells are separate authorities. Both must be exact and empty.

Required sharing:

`association faults = invocation faults = ledger faults = current faults = binding faults = present composition faults`

The extra binding checks preserve constructor-established sharing. Cut faults need no separate read when cut/current identity is established.

A supported canonical fault cannot be replaced by an empty diagnostic copy. Native validation must not call `_invalidate` or clear a fault.

### 30. Final role-table review

| Role | Necessary read subset | Review |
|---|---|---|
| Registry | `_associations`, `_faults` | Sufficient |
| Registry-map association entry | `registry`, `work` | Needed for complete storage validation |
| Selected Association | Seven current role slots, `_faults`, future `_source_lifetime` | Existing checks sufficient; future binding gated |
| Invocation | `_ledger`, `_executor`, `_entry`, `_complete`, authority/faults | Sufficient |
| Ledger | Ten local slots, authority/faults | Sufficient for selected admission profile |
| Current/cut Episode | `first`, `claim`, authority/faults | Sufficient with causal minimization |
| ActionBinding | `profile`, `outcome`, `action`, authority/faults | Sufficient |
| Adapter | `_attempt`, `_dequeued`, authority/faults | Sufficient |
| Bridge | `_released`, `_errors`, authority/faults | Sufficient |

Omissions of invocation diagnostics, old accounting/history, adapter epoch and earlier bridge/F2 state are justified under the stated terminal and continuity predicates.

All slot values are object references. Boolean roles require exact singleton identity, not a C scalar interpretation.

### 31. K observation boundary

CS-R1 can observe K through:

`live root → current map member → selected association → current ledger → current/cut episode → canonical first cell → _CUT`

Required:

- Current graph and faults valid.
- Current and cut epoch identical.
- Exact first cell with the authenticated winner.
- No receipt won that cell.

`cut_attempted=True` is consistent representation/history evidence, not the winning authority.

### 32. Q observation boundary

CS-R1’s assigned Q portion covers:

- Prior accounting consumed through lawful settlement/cut causality.
- Adapter continuation discharged or absent.
- Bridge/F2 terminal continuation discharged or absent.

It does **not** independently establish other-thread mask discharge, physical source coverage or native configuration ownership. The later seam must combine those external prerequisites.

### 33. U observation boundary

CS-R1 can reject:

- Incomplete current receipt/claim/accounting combinations inconsistent with winning K.
- Unresolved adapter continuation.
- Unreleased/error-bearing bridge.
- Canonical graph/configuration/fault invalidity.

It does not prove that future restoration effects will be certain. Post-E3 physical uncertainty and external native/mask uncertainty have separate authority.

### 34. CS-R1 / R1 responsibility split

| R1 term | Supplier |
|---|---|
| A | Canonical SourceAuthority/native association plus Phase-A identity |
| K | CS-R1 current cut observation |
| Q | CS-R1 participating obligations **plus** external native/thread/source obligations |
| I | P1 live-ingress admission |
| U | CS-R1 canonical uncertainty **plus** applicable external uncertainty |
| C | Source continuous-coverage contract |
| X | Source/native writer admission and exclusion |

CS-R1 is not a substitute for the complete conjunction.

### 35. Callback-free helper whitelist

**The candidate does not yet provide a complete executable whitelist.** The following named subset is auditable; generic categories are not enough to finish the contract.

All permitted entries require the GIL, intact runtime, exact live objects and continuous writer exclusion. None may release the GIL or process signals/pending calls.

| Symbol/operation | Purpose and precondition | Ownership/allocation/error treatment |
|---|---|---|
| `Py_TYPE`/exact type comparison | Already-live object | No new reference/allocation |
| Pointer/singleton comparison | Authenticated references | Native comparison only |
| `PyDict_Size` | Exact dict established first | Scalar; invalid-argument error branch forbidden |
| `PyDict_Next` | Exact stable dict, valid position | Borrowed entries; no hash/equality dispatch |
| `PyTuple_GET_SIZE`, `PyTuple_GET_ITEM` | Exact tuple; bounds checked | Borrowed; no allocation |
| `PyType_GetDict` | Exact retained live type | New reference; release ER0 |
| `PyGen_GetCode` | Exact retained generator | New reference; release ER0 |
| Four selected `PyFunction_Get*` getters | Exact retained function | Borrowed; absent optional fields handled explicitly |
| `PyErr_Occurred` | Current thread state | Borrowed observation; no fetch/normalization |
| `Py_DECREF` for the two getter temporaries | Independent owner established | ER0 only |
| Fixed native evidence writes | Preallocated record | No semantic state mutation |
| Native atomic operations | Exact fields/order must be frozen | No Python interaction |
| Raw slot-load helper | **Not concretely frozen in candidate** | See §36 |
| Integer/string validators | **Exact helpers/error paths not frozen** | Must not coerce or allocate |

For example, exact-string type checking alone does not make UTF-8 conversion allocation-free: `PyUnicode_AsUTF8AndSize` can populate a cache. Exact integer checking must precede any API with a coercion fallback. [Pinned Unicode implementation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.14.5/Objects/unicodeobject.c), [pinned integer implementation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.14.5/Objects/longobject.c).

Selecting and auditing these remaining helpers is a required correction, not permission to use the examples here.

### 36. Raw slot-load primitive review

A sufficient architecture contract must specify:

- Already-live exact containing object.
- Internal fixed role entry.
- Authenticated object-reference slot, bounds and alignment.
- Overflow-safe address calculation.
- One pointer-representation load into a local value.
- No automatic INCREF.
- NULL rejection before nested use.
- No descriptor or arbitrary address interface.
- Exact compiler aliasing/load assumptions.

An ordinary load is sufficient under genuine writer exclusion; atomics cannot repair a missing lifetime proof.

The candidate’s “fixed-table slot loads” does not select the load/aliasing contract. A fixed-size copy into a pointer local is one possible correction, but adopting that correction here would exceed independent review. This contributes to the new helper-whitelist finding.

### 37. Memory-ordering review

| State/data | Required relationship | Candidate result |
|---|---|---|
| Python slots/containers | GIL plus no legitimate writer | No extra atomic slot access required |
| S3 table | Initializer release; users acquire | Specified |
| E1 reservation | Exclusive admission; acquire on success, release on rejection | Existing P1 input |
| Operation pins | Pin acquisition versus destruction must be one safe protocol | Incomplete |
| Read-closed/root-held | Closure excludes new root users before pointer clearing | Incomplete ordering |
| E2 payload/commit | Payload before release commit; observers acquire | Specified |
| E3 | Same attempt’s one-shot acquire-release transition | Specified |
| Root-released witness | After pointer clear and corresponding decrement | Needs integration with pin/closure protocol |
| Instrumentation | Fixed evidence release/acquire, no ownership | Required, exact mapping pending |

Native preemption does not invalidate Python observations if neither GIL nor exclusion is released.

### 38. Error-state review

The safe required policy is:

- Inspect an existing Python error before semantic reads.
- Reject without fetching, normalizing, formatting or replacing it.
- Retain a separate native rejection reason.
- Release E1 before Python-facing diagnostics.
- Preserve the original error when one already exists.

The candidate mentions error inspection but does not select this policy precisely enough for a complete whitelist.

Wrong exact types and NULL required slots must be handled by native reason codes before calling helpers whose invalid-argument paths construct exceptions.

### 39. Precommit rejection review

The intended rejection contract is sound:

- No source-finalization effect.
- No canonical repair/mutation.
- No E3 consumption.
- Root remains owned through all borrowed uses.
- Temporary getter references released ER0.
- Bounded native reason committed.
- E1 released before Python resumes.
- No invocation FAILED manufactured.

Its implementation-level completeness remains blocked by the cell lifetime and helper issues. A crash or corrupted pointer is SC, never this clean path.

### 40. E2 witness review

E2 may contain:

- Opaque lifetime and attempt identity.
- Role-table/profile identity.
- Canonical association/epoch observation IDs.
- Graph/qualification and assigned K/Q/U outcomes.
- Validation completion sequence and commit marker.

Raw addresses are optional audit data only. They cannot establish identity across allocation reuse or authorize later dereference.

E2 is not accepted from callers and cannot be reused after rejection or for another lifetime.

### 41. E2 reader model

| Reader | Permission |
|---|---|
| Same native executor | Consume same-attempt E2 for immediate E3 |
| Native observer/instrumentation | Acquire-read committed immutable evidence |
| Python diagnostics | Read/project after protected return with GIL |
| Later seam composition | Use the reviewed observation mechanism within the same reserved attempt |
| Unrelated caller | Diagnostics only; no semantic certificate authority |

Reader pins protect record memory, not K/Q/U authority. Their lifetime must be distinguished from root-capable operation pins.

### 42. E2→E3 handoff review

The same-call handoff survives as a logical protocol:

`continuous E1 + completed E2 release publication → same-attempt E3 one-shot commit`

No Python gap, writer release or GIL release is allowed. Native observers may see E2 without E3. A crash between them establishes neither admission nor restoration.

The unresolved cell/pin machinery must enforce this protocol; E2 cannot compensate for it.

### 43. Successful CS-R1 path

The smallest intended path is:

`eligible profile/table + valid published association + live cell/root + E1/P1`

→ current root traversal  
→ graph and qualification checks  
→ assigned K/Q/U observations  
→ cross-field coherence  
→ ER0 temporary cleanup  
→ immutable E2  
→ immediate E3.

Physical restoration is outside CS-R1’s observation operation. Later root release depends on the successful enclosing lifetime contract.

This path is not approved for seam use yet.

### 44. CS-R1 rejection path

`E1 → failed gate/read/check → bounded native reason → safe temporary cleanup → E1 release → Python`

No E2-success witness, E3 or source-finalization effect.

A pre-E1 rejection has no E1-acquired witness and cannot satisfy a test specifically targeting E1 cleanup.

Crash/corruption remains SC.

### 45. Successful later root-release sequence

The candidate needs the following order made explicit:

1. E3 has committed.
2. Restoration and its observation complete.
3. Restoration terminal publication.
4. R0 callable discharge.
5. Resource terminal publication.
6. Permanently close **new root-capable entry**.
7. Establish no other root-capable operation can remain.
8. Finish the final current-edge/ER0 proof using the finalizer’s existing ownership.
9. End all borrowed root uses.
10. Clear the native root slot exactly once.
11. DECREF that former reference exactly once under ER0.
12. Publish root-released state.
13. Permit protected exit.

Read closure cannot prohibit the finalizer from completing its already-owned final proof; it prohibits new users. This distinction and its pin ordering are not fully frozen in the candidate.

A failed release proof after E3 is not a clean precommit rejection.

### 46. Qualification-matrix review

Common requirements remain: isolated history, exact artifacts/profile, **12 seconds**, positive target acknowledgement, semantic/current-edge attribution, candidate evidence before cleanup.

Setup classes:

- **C:** canonical history.
- **D:** safe decoy.
- **N:** separately built negative artifact.
- **I:** native instrumentation.
- **O:** external/parent observation.

Every row additionally needs its expected candidate disposition. Missing target occurrence is INCONCLUSIVE. Unsafe access, false acceptance or fabricated evidence is FAIL.

**Material matrix defects:**

- Several decoy rows do not yet specify a route to the intended internal check. A decoy rejected at the public gate does not qualify graph validation.
- R6/R9 lack a frozen producer/pin protocol.
- R10 lacks a complete non-perturbing ownership/decrement oracle.
- Artifact equivalence is a requirement, not yet an explicit property map.

These are **REPAIRABLE MATRIX DEFECTS**, and collectively **BLOCKING BEFORE IMPLEMENTATION/seam approval** for the corresponding claims. Numeric future artifact hashes are appropriately deferred. No deliberate memory corruption is necessary.

The future binding also needs all A–L histories requested in §80: single publication, repeated-bind behavior, foreign/stale rejection, unpublished failure/interruption, initialized publication, installation gate, no active replacement, retained failure, successful closure and stale-wrapper rejection.

### 47. Production/instrumented artifact mapping

Evidence transfers only when instrumentation leaves unchanged:

- Root/reference ownership.
- Object and native-cell layouts relevant to the property.
- Table/schema binding.
- Reservation/pin/destruction behavior.
- Callback-free operation set.
- E2/E3 ordering.

Native markers may add preallocated scalar evidence. They may not retain the root, block an otherwise possible schedule or manufacture terminal state.

For R10, a meaningful oracle records the existing owning edges, read closure, pin disposition, root-slot clear and associated decrement without adding an owning reference. Survival by itself is insufficient.

Production must separately execute its own artifact/table admission gates and exposed interfaces. Instrumented layout agreement cannot qualify a different production table.

### 48. Adversarial schedules

The following tables use shared preconditions to avoid hiding assumptions in repeated text.

- **L:** pre-origin bootstrap; no responsibility/source effect. Python may run outside protected validation, but metadata discovery must not dispatch through candidate descriptors.
- **V:** same canonical owner, published candidate cell/root, exact profile/table, E1 reserved, intact runtime, no prohibited live ancestry and no legitimate writer executing.
- **R:** admitted enclosing finalization after resource publication, with continuous exclusion.
- **Clean:** native rejection at the stated gate, E1 released if acquired.
- **U:** unresolved architecture obligation; no approval.
- **SC:** process failure, not clean rejection or success.

For V/R rows, Python execution is forbidden until protected return/rejection. Raw reads are permitted only through authenticated live parents; no read of a rejected child is permitted. Every row requires the specified target and disposition evidence before cleanup.

### 49. Findings by severity

**BLOCKING — B2.1: Native anchor and metadata GC/lifetime contract is not closed**

- **Affected requirement:** BLOCKING 2; candidate §§11–16.
- **Evidence:** Candidate deliberately excludes the root edge from automatic GC clearing while describing an owning, mutable native-cell reference cycle. Pinned CPython requires a defined container traversal/clearing contract.
- **Schedule:** Caller disappears; GC runs before E1. Ordinary complete traversal can collect an otherwise isolated cycle; omitted traversal cannot simply be assumed compliant and safe.
- **Why it matters:** The same assumption underpins root survival, unresolved retention, metadata lifetime and ER0 release.
- **Smallest correction:** Specify the exact Python object/native-anchor representation, GC allocation/traverse/clear/deallocation behavior, metadata ownership and supported shutdown boundary. Establish retention without relying on an unexplained omitted container edge.
- **Governance:** Existing semantic authority appears sufficient; this is a material lifetime architecture correction.
- **Re-review:** Required. This review does not select or approve a replacement anchor.

**BLOCKING — B2.2: Binding publication and native pin/closure ordering remain implementation choices**

- **Affected requirement:** BLOCKING 2 and the prospective association seam.
- **Evidence:** No binding operation exists in current bytes. The candidate specifies “returns/rejects,” invariant statements and pins, but not the indivisible binding publication or pin/destruction protocol.
- **Schedule:** Reentrant binding publishes between an outer unbound check and attachment; or a root-capable operation races read closure/root clearing.
- **Why it matters:** Authority could become partially published, replaced, leaked in an unpublished state or used after closure.
- **Smallest correction:** Freeze one publication transition, exact repeated-bind outcome, unpublished disposal, live-handle pin acquisition, root-capable versus evidence-only pins, and closure-before-release ordering.
- **Governance:** No new semantic owner is needed.
- **Re-review:** Required because native association lifetime changes materially.

**MAJOR — N1: Runtime helper/raw-load/error whitelist is incomplete**

- **Affected requirement:** New CS-R1 finding; callback-free operation set.
- **Evidence:** Candidate specifies categories—“bounded integer/string checks,” “fixed-table loads,” “error inspection”—rather than exact helpers and all valid/error paths.
- **Schedule:** An otherwise exact string is inspected through an allocating conversion, or an existing Python error is normalized/replaced inside E1.
- **Why it matters:** Public/exact-type operations are not automatically allocation-free; raw-load aliasing and failure semantics remain choices.
- **Smallest correction:** Freeze the exact scalar helpers, raw-load representation/aliasing rule, existing-error policy and complete whitelist.
- **Governance:** Existing authority is sufficient.
- **Re-review:** Required for the completed operation set.

**MAJOR — N2: Required matrix rows lack decisive boundary-specific construction/oracles**

- **Affected requirement:** New CS-R1 finding; qualification readiness.
- **Evidence:** Candidate decoy, writer/pin and root-release rows list desired outcomes without exact routes and synchronization sufficient to reach the target.
- **Schedule:** A stale-association decoy rejects at an earlier profile gate; an ER0 observer supplies an extra Registry reference; a writer never attempts entry.
- **Why it matters:** Those histories can appear green without testing their stated property.
- **Smallest correction:** Freeze per-row setup mechanism, target acknowledgement, current-edge evidence, non-owning observation and artifact equivalence. Label helper-only decoy tests with their narrower claim.
- **Governance:** Existing authority is sufficient; no direct K/Q/U fixture writes.
- **Re-review:** Required for repaired affected matrix families.

**MINOR:** None requiring a separate finding.

**INFORMATIONAL:** S3 and existing-state integrity minimization survive. ER0’s local non-last argument is valid conditional on the corrected lifetime contract. No conflict with frozen F2 or a need for native semantic mirroring was established.

### 50. BLOCKING 1 independent result

**BLOCKING 1 — layout provenance: PASS**

This applies to S3’s architecture-level provenance, complete schema, metadata boundary and pre-read authentication—not native owner lifetime or runtime qualification.

### 51. BLOCKING 2 independent result

**BLOCKING 2 — root/evidence-reference lifetime: FAIL**

The smallest remaining defects are B2.1 and B2.2: the native anchor/GC/metadata lifetime mechanism and the publication/pin/closure protocol.

### 52. MAJOR 1 independent result

**MAJOR 1 — integrity census/minimization: PASS**

The current `_graph`/`_qualify` families, ActionBinding coverage, fault sharing, cut causality and adapter/bridge reductions survive.

This does not excuse the new helper or matrix findings, nor authenticate future binding bytes.

### 53. OPTION A independent result

**OPTION A — DIRECT CANONICAL READS:  
REQUIRES CORRECTION AND RE-REVIEW**

The direction remains viable. No impossibility of bounded direct observation was established.

### 54. CS-R1 bounded claim

The full proposed claim is **not independently approved**.

What this review establishes is narrower:

> For the specified exact-build architecture, S3 provides a non-circular method of authenticating the finite project-slot layout. The current canonical integrity and terminal-state predicates identified here can be observed without creating new semantic authority, provided the native lifetime, operation and oracle defects are corrected.

This does not establish all R1 terms, root-retention correctness, successful source restoration, the K/Q/U seam, I5, production activation, COMPLETE, Gap 4D, crash recovery or real-SOL readiness.

### 55. CS-R1 STOP conditions

The inherited STOP set remains controlling. In particular:

- Any authority, schema, metadata, native artifact or runtime mismatch.
- Missing V4 promoted into reconstructed authority.
- Profile/image invalidation.
- Wrong exact type or stale root/association/current edge.
- Unproved root/metadata lifetime, unsafe temporary release or required ER3.
- Unspecified GC, binding-publication or pin/destruction behavior.
- Graph/qualification failure or retained canonical fault.
- K false/unprovable; assigned Q unresolved; assigned U non-clear.
- Writer exclusion failure.
- Any helper absent from the completed whitelist.
- Required allocation, GC, descriptor execution, coercion, GIL release, signal/pending processing or last-reference destruction during E1→E2.
- Arbitrary address/object/offset selection.
- Semantic repair/mirroring.
- E2 uncertainty or stale-attempt reuse.
- Missing target oracle, instrumentation-created ownership or weakened qualification.
- Scope expansion.

Unknown consequential risk remains STOP.

### 56. K/Q/U-seam input-readiness result

**CS-R1 is not ready as an input to focused K/Q/U-seam completion.**

That next task would still have to invent material lifetime and execution rules. The correction must remain focused on B2.1, B2.2, N1 and N2, followed by independent re-review. S3 and existing-state integrity results can remain frozen unless a correction directly contradicts them.

### 57. Protected repository-boundary result

No demonstrated correction requires modifying:

- `runtime.py`;
- worker-boundary tests;
- filesystem capability or frozen F2;
- V7;
- sole-reaper/spawn;
- production actor wiring;
- governance;
- checkpoints;
- unrelated research.

Those exclusions remain.

Prospective work remains confined to the canonical association seam, private source-lifetime/native representation, build/manifest contract and dedicated qualification infrastructure. No edits are authorized.

Original-six-finding integration remains pending. R0/R1/P1/G1/T1/N1/Q2/QC1/C1 are preserved; this review establishes no independent final correction of the original six.

### 58. Checkpoint/continuity recommendation

**Recommendation B: a separately authorized checkpoint after this review, because continuity risk is already high.**

The transport failure and report-only architecture chain make that risk concrete. Such a checkpoint must preserve the failed CS-R1 re-review, the two surviving findings, all four correction targets, exact candidate versus review status, missing V4 and implementation non-authorization. It must authenticate durable inputs and receive independent review.

No checkpoint was created or edited.

### 59. Remaining unqualified scope

Remain unqualified:

- CS-R1 implementation and runtime qualification.
- Focused K/Q/U → native-admission seam.
- I5 and the proposed native finalization primitive.
- Full production source lifetime/restoration.
- Production activation and actor attachment.
- Broader production COMPLETE.

- Whole-miner and real-SOL readiness.

All previously qualified I1–I4, H1–H8/both-H8, Gap 3/4A–4C, CLOSED finality and specified actual-signal boundaries remain preserved.

Case F remains exactly: **candidate restoration FAILED/unresolved; ambient cleanup later restored the environment.** Cleanup supplies no CS-R1, root-release or resource-completion proof.

**THIS REVIEW DOES NOT AUTHORIZE IMPLEMENTATION.**
````````

### Evidence ARCH — Broader owner-integration architecture — historical report

````````text
ORE Miner V3 — Stage 3C — Broker / Source-Cut / Invocation-Owner Architecture

**A — BROADER STAGE-3C ARCHITECTURE IS SUFFICIENTLY DEFINED FOR INCREMENTAL IMPLEMENTATION AUTHORIZATION.**

**OWNER-INTEGRATION-FIRST**, limited to an internal, unattached component. Live production source activation, restoration/handoff and actor attachment remain blocked until separately qualified. **GOVERNANCE SUFFICIENT** for the increments and constraints specified here; this design is not implementation authorization.

The first implementation increment is I1: non-authoritative state views, interface declarations and invariant tests in two new files. It creates no runtime owner, installs no handler, receives no cancellation and invokes no action. Later increments are deliberately separate qualification steps. This resolves the prior architecture questions rather than proposing wholesale copying of the experiment.

**A. Authentication and preservation**

The complete prior integration report and inventory were read. Its manifest authenticates at 1,776 bytes, SHA-256 `c6fa6c1d615d09f75b3795a0fecd776649e0261ca8bd0dc1480178de2dffc7c8`. The corrected manifest authenticates at SHA-256 `0b2cc25976244b4093afe6cb21894a89964e65d75769152488b448ac248a7904`; independent review manifest at `dde2ac91645a2a6f1f846daa2e566adb57bbf31b57d83641181c843cbe1d94ac`. All their listed artifacts, manifests and controlling prior chain remain unchanged. The controlling audit remains 21,372 bytes, SHA-256 `e4caa3984a4d2f152bd0b2754f882b8b7ae8c175a0d522e93d2d38322918408e`.

Repository `/Users/erale/Documents/orev3`: branch `research/post-v1`, upstream `origin/research/post-v1`; local/tracking/independently queried remote HEAD `83b53e5294ad336e5b02fe0f941fb074d2d5756d`; ahead/behind 0/0; empty index; 5 tracked modifications + 27 untracked = 32. Authentication covers all 698 repository identities, nine governance documents/bindings, checkpoint, 28 promoted implementation blobs, paused/unrelated work, V7, frozen acquisition and the original/remediation/corrected/review evidence chain. Before/after results and protected identity inventory are retained. Optional index refresh is disabled; remote queries do not fetch; `git diff --check` passes. No repository or protected evidence mutation, prototype, test or runtime experiment is performed.

The authority reread includes receipt/settlement supplement §§4–8 and §10, and parent interruption/admission clarification §§10–13. Their controlling requirements are qualified receipt before classification, no discretionary delayed acceptance, failed settlement not being clean settlement, retained uncertain obligations, no replay, no successor-owner authority by implication, and whole-lifetime source qualification before operational use.

**B. Responsibility decomposition**

Classification: A existing repository; B new abstraction; C unchanged V7 semantic responsibility; D frozen acquisition; E future unqualified source/shutdown responsibility. C is a semantic boundary, not an assertion that an importable production V7 module already exists. Repository translation must be requalified without modifying protected V7 evidence.

| Responsibility | Production owner | Class |
|---|---|---|
| Capability lifetime and strong retention | CapabilityRegistry owned by the eventual executable actor; retains CapabilityRecord before activation | B; production actor attachment E |
| Cancellation source ownership | SourceAuthority held by CapabilityRecord; exclusive installation/restoration obligation | B/E |
| Handler installation/removal | SourceLifetimeAdapter, acting only for canonical SourceAuthority | E |
| Callback ingress | Private source adapter route; cannot dispatch or settle | B/C |
| Qualified receipt and episode membership | EpisodeLedger, implementing V7 receipt ordering as one logical operation | C |
| Retained/pending obligation and subsumption | EpisodeLedger; adapter/binding retention references the same epoch authority | C |
| Invocation ownership and FAILED | InvocationRecord, strongly owned by CapabilityRecord, using runtime executor lifecycle | B |
| COMPLETE | Sole admitted invocation driver, gated by all required completion evidence | B |
| Source cut / authoritative CLOSED | Shared ledger cut discriminator, retained by SourceAuthority | B/C |
| Restoration evidence | SourceAuthority restoration ledger; actual producer is SourceLifetimeAdapter | B/E |
| Action claim/invocation/accounting/uncertainty | EpisodeLedger and sole runner dispatcher; never callback | C |
| At-most-once | Single claim permission and accounted/uncertain state; no new claim on later receipt | C |
| Failure evidence | InvocationRecord phase/basis plus retained ledger and subordinate evidence | B |
| New-work exclusion | CapabilityRecord admission gate, consulted by every eventual governed caller | B; caller attachment E |
| Ordinary post-handoff cancellation | Existing enclosing handler behavior after qualified source handoff | A/E |
| Acquisition resource/restoration/result ownership | Existing `_ControllerAcquisitionPolicy`, DescriptorOwner/result cells | D/A |
| Native wait and child ownership | GovernedSpawnHandle and bounded resources, separately paused | A; not imported into this component |

Use one new internal module initially, with private conceptual owners rather than separate public mutable classes for every row. The lifetime relation is registry → capability → invocation, source and ledger. An episode is subordinate, never a replacement capability owner. No weak ownership or finalizer is involved. Registry retention cannot depend on an immediate caller local, traceback, returned handle or successful catch/finally.

**C. Interface design**

Names below are architectural operation names, not copied experimental class names. Public inputs are selectors and read-only views, not writable records, arbitrary callbacks, mutable evidence dictionaries or externally minted authorization tokens. All mutations are private to canonical module-owned objects. Ordinary Python private naming is not a security boundary against arbitrary memory corruption; supported callers and reentry are the threat model.

Profile selection is closed. The component dispatcher has module-defined argument-insensitive cancellation actions for the explicitly qualified KI/SystemExit profiles. The experimental ProbeCancellation remains a test-only outcome unless separately included in the qualified production profile. No user-supplied callable, observer closure, subclass or per-source action is accepted. Source mapping must establish same enclosing work and no independent per-request effects. Diagnostic observers do not run as supplied callbacks on the critical path. Read-only snapshots are diagnostic and cannot authorize a transition.

Runtime construction must reject an unsupported interpreter/thread/profile before any source mutation. The initial qualification target remains CPython 3.14.5/main interpreter thread/macOS arm64 and the inherited finite source mapping. This is not permission to drop required SIGINT: an unsupported host remains ineligible for activation. No generic GIL-as-lock claim is made. Module-owned function/profile identities must be validated and sealed before publication; legitimate internal writes are narrowly enumerated. Production identities replace temporary Harness identities, and the complete foreign-observer/outcome/configuration negative matrix must be rerun. This is requalification, not a claim that renaming preserves proof.

The following operation contracts define callers, ownership, inputs/outputs, conditions and effects. Rejected supported contenders do not invalidate the admitted owner. A diagnostic snapshot copied successfully may be stale; the actual operation rechecks canonical live state. No boolean or enum provided by a caller constitutes restoration, terminal or receipt evidence.

| Operation | Caller / authoritative owner | Inputs → output | Preconditions, transition, retained evidence | Failure / idempotence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| describe(view) | Any reader / none | Immutable view → diagnostic interpretation | No authority or writes; invalid combinations rejected | Repeatable; interrupted observation changes nothing |
| reserve_capability | Future legitimate actor / registry | Closed profile selector, work identity → canonical reference | Registry exists first; no source mutation; register one record before work; duplicate work resolves to original owner or rejects without replacing it | Allocation before registration admits no work; after registration reference retained despite caller interruption; same identity cannot create second owner |
| prepare_invocation | Canonical runner / capability | Reference and existing prerequisite references → prepared record | One non-yielding invocation created and retained before entry; source/work ownership already explicit; new governed admission closed | Repeated prepare cannot replace prior invocation; failures leave no admitted work or retained prepared record |
| enter_invocation | Canonical main runner / invocation | Canonical reference → return or propagated exception | PREPARED, no active executor, subordinate blockers checked; runtime entry is executor authority; fixed internal driver only | Reentry rejects before second execution; abnormal runtime closure without COMPLETE derives FAILED; never mark-failed-after-catch |
| source_callback | Installed adapter / source authority | Runtime signum/frame → receipt path or qualified ordinary path | Source registered before installation; valid mapping; pre-cut enters receipt even when invocation FAILED | No action/settlement. CLOSED explicit old ingress rejects; runtime CLOSED routing needs separately qualified coverage, not blind rejection of ordinary cancellation |
| receive | Adapter or canonical cooperative route / ledger | Source identity, work identity, existing retention reference if any → read-only receipt result | Eligible source, canonical owner, source not CLOSED; retain/classify in same ordered operation; existing unresolved epoch selects/subsumes | Return may be interrupted but ownership must already be retained. Repeated physical requests are not deduplicated by timestamp; repeated processing of a retention reference is continuation |
| continue_retained | Qualified adapter/acquisition bridge / ledger | Existing retained membership reference → processing disposition | Exact old reference/epoch; no fresh receipt; exclusion held through continuation | Cannot discard/reclassify membership; repeated processing cannot claim second action |
| claim_and_dispatch | Sole runner / ledger | No caller-supplied action; closed profile and current epoch → accounted result or exception | Required native terminal and protection evidence, no FAILED invocation, unclaimed pending action; consume single attempt before possible entry | Interruption before/after observed entry retains unresolved claimed permission; no replay API. Intended raise counts only when accounted under qualified contract |
| settle_episode | Sole runner / ledger | Existing subordinate evidence references → settled epoch | All adopted §7 prerequisites; action accounted, no uncertainty, all receipts disposed, exact protection restoration; ordered with receipt | Stale call cannot settle a successor; incomplete/FAILED rejects without clearing intent |
| request_source_cut | Invocation driver / source authority + ledger | Current epoch authority → receipt-won or cut-won result | Quiescing, no unresolved adapter/acquisition responsibility, valid source coverage plan. Same first-disposition cell arbitrates receipt versus CLOSED | Receipt winner is handled under its epoch; cut winner persistently forbids old receipt. No cut after FAILED via a new recovery call. Repeat reads existing winner, never overwrites |
| inspect_obligations | Reader / none | Canonical reference → immutable ledger/restoration/failure view | No mutation, no delegation of authority, no health claim from partial snapshot | Repeatable; interrupted read does not erase underlying responsibility |
| restore_sources | Future source lifetime runner / source authority | Existing original configuration references → exact progress evidence | Qualified restoration implementation and surviving executor required; absent in owner-first production activation | Partial failure retains attempted/unverified state. No public mark-restored or replay. Not implemented before separate qualification |
| commit_complete | Private invocation driver / invocation | No public evidence arguments → positive terminal cell | Native/prerequisite evidence, cut, required exact restoration, action/receipt obligations resolved, no FAILED; one positive commit | Commit is unconditional code, not assert side effect. Before commit interruption leaves non-success; after commit later events cannot revoke COMPLETE |
| admit_new_work | Eventual governed caller / capability | Work request → rejection or separately qualified admission | Registered closing/FAILED/COMPLETE old capability is never healthy-reuse authority | Always reject old-capability work after reservation; a different work lifecycle needs separately qualified creation, not reset |
| handoff_ordinary | Future source adapter / source authority | Actual verified configuration/coverage facts → ordinary routing | Full qualified handoff prerequisites; not a caller token and not FAILED alone | No implementation in owner-first increments; absence stays explicit, never synthesized from empty queue |

Effect permissions are exclusive. R=qualified receipt; M=membership; A=claim/invoke; T=invocation terminality; C=cut; S=physical restoration evidence. A dash means no permission.

| Operation | R | M | A | T | C | S |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| describe / inspect_obligations | — | — | — | — | — | — |
| reserve_capability / prepare_invocation | — | — | — | — | — | — |
| enter_invocation | — | — | — | runtime-derived FAILED on abnormal closure | — | — |
| source_callback | delegates receive only | delegates only | — | — | — | — |
| receive | yes | same logical operation | — | — | — | — |
| continue_retained | no fresh receipt | preserve only | — | — | — | — |
| claim_and_dispatch | — | — | sole authority | no direct terminal setter | — | — |
| settle_episode | — | no reassignment | — | — | — | — |
| request_source_cut | — | no fabricated receipt | — | — | sole cut writer | — |
| restore_sources | — | — | — | — | — | actual adapter only |
| commit_complete | — | — | — | sole positive writer | — | — |
| admit_new_work / handoff_ordinary | — | — | — | — | — | no restoration declaration |

A driver composes these private operations but cannot bypass their prerequisites. The effect table separates method responsibility from effects of operations it calls. Native closure supplies unsuccessful meaning; it is not a callable FAILED publication operation.

**D. State model and transition authority**

State is a product, not one health enum:

- Registry: absent or canonical retained capability.
- Invocation: not prepared, PREPARED, ACTIVE, FAILED or COMPLETE. FAILED is native executor closed with no positive COMPLETE. COMPLETE dominates a still-returning post-commit epilogue; runtime frame closure and logical completion are distinct.
- Episode: no intent, retained/unresolved, claimed, accounted, cleanly settled. Repetition is bounded first-intent plus subsumption information; no unbounded signal-count queue.
- Action: unclaimed; claimed/entry unobserved; entry observed; accounted completion; unresolved uncertainty before/after observed entry. Claimed plus escaped executor without completion already implies no-replay uncertainty; a later uncertainty flag is not required to make replay unsafe.
- Source: uninstalled/unqualified, broker-owned pre-cut, cut-authoritative CLOSED. A cut attempt is not CLOSED; the successful arbitration witness is. CLOSED means old-receipt authority ended, not physical handler removal.
- Restoration: original facts retained, not attempted, attempted/unverified, verified per setting; aggregate handoff unavailable or separately qualified. No assumed restoration-resumption transition from FAILED.

| Transition / trigger | Authority and required evidence | Permitted afterward | Prohibited afterward |
|---|---|---|---|
| No owner → canonical record | Legitimate actor registry registration before mutation/work | Prepare, inspect | Work or source installation before ownership |
| PREPARED → ACTIVE | Sole registered non-yielding executor enters | Driver, eligible receipt, diagnostics | Second executor, replacement owner |
| ACTIVE → FAILED | Runtime closes executor without positive COMPLETE; pre-existing basis and obligation references survive | Pre-cut receipt/retention, inspect, ordinary unrelated computation subject to host qualification | New work, delivery/replay, clean settlement, COMPLETE, detachment |
| ACTIVE → COMPLETE | Private positive commit after all prerequisites | Diagnostic reads; lawful ordinary later behavior | FAILED/reopen/work reuse of old capability |
| No intent → retained epoch request | Qualified receipt ordered with settlement/cut | Same-epoch repeat retention; eligible sole dispatch | Generation-based membership; delayed discretionary acceptance |
| Retained → subsumed repeat | Same-work eligible receipt before clean settlement | Retain bounded repeated disposition | New action merely because callback is later |
| Unclaimed → claimed | Sole runner consumes attempt with prerequisites | At most that attempt can enter action | Second claim |
| Claimed → entry observed | Action entry itself, not callback | Account completion if proven | Infer completion from entry |
| Claimed/entered → uncertain | Missing qualified completion on escape or failed recording | Retain receipts and evidence | Replay to obtain certainty |
| Accounted → settled | All §7 obligations, qualified ordering | Fresh receipts under applicable later rules | Stale clearing, premature settlement |
| Pre-cut → CLOSED | Cut wins common arbitration, source coverage contract valid | Qualified restoration/routing only; inspect | Old qualified ingress, FAILED-as-cut inference |
| Restoration → verified | Actual configuration operation plus verification from owned adapter | Only authorized later handoff/completion | Caller assertion as evidence |
| Pre-cut FAILED → restoration resumption | No qualified transition exists yet | Report unavailable | Invent resume, call new close, reset generator |

Required invariants: FAILED never implies CLOSED or healthy reuse; native closure never supplies a source cut; retained intent never supplies action completion; CLOSED rejects old explicit qualified ingress; positive COMPLETE cannot follow FAILED; one claimed action cannot be replayed; restoration uncertainty blocks clean completion; later fresh delivery is not suppressed after genuine completion. These are enforced by private state authority, not by trusting a report object.

**E–F. Source ownership and Gate 13**

The eventual actor retains CapabilityRegistry before interruptible source changes. SourceAuthority captures exact originals and unresolved installation/restoration obligations before mutation. Only SourceLifetimeAdapter may install/remove handlers; it is unavailable for live use in early increments. Module import must be inert. Failure during activation cannot leave an unowned source: activation itself requires the later source-lifetime qualification, not simply an owner constructor test.

Cut authority is the winning discriminator in the ledger arbitration cell, with the corresponding epoch reference retained. A separate `closed=True` write after winning would recreate a visibility gap and is not the architecture. A cut request alone grants nothing. All source routes must consult the authoritative discriminator, not invocation disposition.

Gate-13 path: canonical source callback → pre-cut check → qualified V7-semantic receipt → unresolved epoch 0 selected/subsumed → retained obligation. Invocation FAILED is not part of the receipt rejection predicate. The callback cannot dispatch. The sole dispatcher separately rejects FAILED, and uncertainty separately consumes replay permission. Thus retention cannot reopen work or manufacture COMPLETE.

CLOSED explicit old ingress: canonical source check finds cut witness → reject before old receipt. Actual runtime callbacks after cut must instead use the separately qualified ordinary/coverage route; rejecting an ordinary later cancellation is not acceptable merely because explicit old ingress rejects. In early repository integration no live source factory is exposed, so this missing handoff route cannot be exercised by production callers. Repository tests can qualify bounded isolated routes without claiming whole-host activation.

FAILED before cut leaves SourceAuthority and EpisodeLedger reachable through the capability. The future actor must not detach them or exit under a claimed successful shutdown. Ordinary handoff eventually requires verified actual originals, resolved required obligations and continuous coverage through pending delivery, under the future source-lifetime contract. The experiment's ordinary post-cut forwarding is evidence for its fixed profile, not sufficient production handoff authority.

**G. Uncertain-action contract**

| Action state | Evidence retained by ledger | Later eligible pre-cut receipt | Replay rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| Unclaimed | Pending epoch, chosen closed profile | Select/subsume same epoch | Only sole eligible first claim can dispatch |
| Claimed, entry unobserved | Attempt consumed; no trustworthy entry evidence | Retain/subsume | No second invocation even if entry may not have happened |
| Entry observed | Claim plus entry witness | Retain/subsume while active/unresolved | No second invocation |
| Accounted intended raise/return | Claim, completion disposition, required evidence | Subsumed until clean settlement; later fresh receipt follows authority | Intended exception is not replay permission |
| Uncertain before/after entry | Claim plus absence of qualified completion; entry fact when available | Retain obligation, remain FAILED if invocation escaped | Never reinvoke to learn outcome |

Existing `GovernedSpawnHandle.wait` is not this action dispatcher and its `_wait_consumed` field is not imported as an action claim. Descriptor F2 delivery is also separate. The new ledger consumes established subordinate evidence but does not replace it. Recording diagnostics may fail; claim consumption and no-completion interpretation must already make retry impossible.

**H. V7 and frozen acquisition boundary**

V7 input responsibilities remain: fixed same-work action profile, admitted source mapping, canonical main runner, existing episode identity, one-attempt acquisition binding and exact continuation provenance. V7 output responsibilities remain: selected/subsumed qualified receipt, retained earlier membership, claim/accounted/uncertain state, clean-settlement ordering and binding/restoration disposition. The invocation owner consumes those facts and adds no second membership ledger or action claimant.

V7 is a protected temporary implementation, not a repository package to import via `/private/tmp`. The new component will translate its semantic operations into module-owned private code with a per-operation equivalence map. It must preserve the closed profile, not introduce a generic callable protocol. Test-only diagnostics move outside semantic decisions. Foreign outcome/observer, vocabulary, nested representative, publication-before-ingress and one-attempt tests are mandatory requalification. No protected V7 source changes are needed or proposed.

Frozen acquisition keeps descriptor scope, result-cell ownership, its own failed/restoration state, continuation and retirement semantics. The bridge may observe depth, pending/binding retention, actual restoration completion and policy failure through the already qualified integration points. It holds exclusion through continuation and never treats a changed representative as new membership. Outer completion is blocked by unresolved acquisition. Outer FAILED cannot cure acquisition failure; acquisition success cannot COMPLETE the outer invocation. No generic F2 wrapper around waits, no status-12 child-cleanup fallback, no policy edits. If faithful integration requires a protected semantic change, the increment stops as architectural conflict.

**I. OWNER-INTEGRATION-FIRST boundary**

Owner/state/receipt implementation may precede production shutdown only as an internal unattached component. Production source activation and actor entry points remain absent, not controlled by a mutable `experimental=True` switch. No public constructor accepts caller-created restoration/native-terminal certificates. Read-only snapshots are expressly non-authoritative.

Hooks reserved for future work are read-only unresolved-obligation inspection and private adapter-owned update points for actual original capture, attempted change and verified change. There is no public `mark_restored`, `handoff`, `resume_failed` or `set_complete`. Until a separately qualified adapter exists, those production transitions remain unavailable. Tests may use isolated fixtures and actual interpreter signals under instrumentation; fixtures are not shipped as production source factories. No false source-handoff or child-terminal evidence is substituted merely to enable COMPLETE.

This permits safe dormant code integration, not operation with permanently broker-controlled failed sources. Live activation is **shutdown-prerequisite**, even though unattached owner integration is owner-first. Gap 4D remains INTENTIONALLY UNQUALIFIED, with production activation blocked by its broader source-lifetime prerequisite.

Prefer one internal new module rather than inserting another lifecycle into the 170 KB paused runtime. No package export or actor import is needed for early increments.

**K. Incremental implementation sequence**

Every increment requires explicit authorization; the paths below are proposed allowlists, not permission from this report. Tests accompany each semantic increment rather than being deferred to a final testing slice.

| Increment | Objective and exact proposed paths | Semantics / tests | Prerequisite and qualification after review |
|---|---|---|---|
| I1 | Two paths in section J only | Immutable diagnostic views/enums and operation contract declarations; pure interpretation/validation of state combinations. No owner allocation, registry, mutable transition engine, signals, actions, runpy, temporary imports or runtime imports | This architecture; invariant tests and import-inertness checks. Qualifies representation only, no runtime gate |
| I2 | Same two paths | Private registry and non-yielding invocation lifecycle with fixed internal driver; runtime-derived failure and private positive-completion mechanism, no live source activation | Independent I1 review; registration/native-entry/multi-frame/reentry/real-interruption tests in isolation. Qualifies owner mechanism under test prerequisites, not source activation or production COMPLETE |
| I3 | Same two paths | Module-owned closed action profile, V7-semantic ledger/receipt/cut integration and Gate 13; no caller callbacks or production activation | Independent I2 review and operation equivalence map; Gate 4A/B/C, CLOSED, Gap 3, H1–H7, no-replay, qualification negatives; bounded repository component evidence |
| I4 | Same two paths | Existing F2 bridge/retaining adapter composition, exact continuation and both H8 forms; complete component regression | Independent I3 review; actual unchanged acquisition, all 20 applicable gates, positive/post-COMPLETE in bounded fixture profile, gaps 1–4. No Gap-4D promotion |
| I5 | Separate future allowlist, not authorized or guessed here | Qualify full source lifetime/installation/restoration/handoff and actual executable actor ownership; then attachment | Independent I4 review plus separately authorized shutdown design. Must resolve real lifetime/coverage prerequisites, not fake restoration certificates |

For I1, views may represent externally reported COMPLETE/FAILED facts for diagnostics but are never accepted as capabilities or authorization evidence. A view constructor creates no invocation and does not assert that any real operation completed. Invalid combinations such as FAILED plus successful reuse, uncertain plus accounted clean settlement, or no cut plus claimed handoff are rejected as descriptions. A COMPLETE record may describe a post-commit still-returning native executor; tests must not confuse frame closure with the positive commit. The contract exposes no state setter that later implementation could mistake for authority.

I1 tests: valid/invalid view combinations; source CLOSED independent of FAILED; action entry independent of accounting; membership provenance distinguishable from generation; immutable diagnostic objects; no authority/token constructors; imports do not alter signals/masks/GC, create threads/processes, or import experimental/runtime machinery. These are meaningful boundary tests, not duplicated assertions of field spelling. No runtime signal experiment is needed to call I1 complete. State views are not a solution to durability by themselves.

I2–I4 test fixtures may arrange faults and provide isolated bounded prerequisites, but must label synthetic terminal facts as such and cannot leave a production API that accepts them. Production dispatch/COMPLETE remains unavailable until actual prerequisite producers are qualified. The runtime mechanism's implementation can be exercised by tests without exposing a usable live-source constructor to application code.

Preserve all old 698 paths in I1; only two new paths are permitted. Later increments preserve every path outside those two plus all original external evidence. Each increment ends with independent repository-level review before proceeding. Stop for incorrect receipt ordering, false completion, replay, owner loss, new source/action authority, import side effects, paused-byte changes or any need to alter protected V7/F2. A failed increment is diagnosed, not automatically repaired under the same permission.
````````
